<div class="col-sm order-1">
      <h2 class="chapter_title">CHAPTER 115A. WASTE MANAGEMENT</h2>
      
      <h1 class="fgh">CITATION, PURPOSE, AND DEFINITIONS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.01">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.01 CITATION.</h1>
        <p>Chapter 115A shall be known as the "Waste Management Act."</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.01.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.1.1.0">1980 c 564 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/325/#laws.0.1.0">1989 c 325 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.02">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.02 LEGISLATIVE DECLARATION OF POLICY; PURPOSES.</h1>
        <p>(a) It is the goal of this chapter to protect the state's land, air, water, and other natural resources and the public health by improving waste management in the state to serve the following purposes:</p>
        <p>(1) reduction in the amount and toxicity of waste generated;</p>
        <p>(2) separation and recovery of materials and energy from waste;</p>
        <p>(3) reduction in indiscriminate dependence on disposal of waste;</p>
        <p>(4) coordination of solid waste management among political subdivisions; and</p>
        <p>(5) orderly and deliberate development and financial security of waste facilities including disposal facilities.</p>
        <p>(b) The waste management goal of the state is to foster an integrated waste management system in a manner appropriate to the characteristics of the waste stream and thereby protect the state's land, air, water, and other natural resources and the public health. The following waste management practices are in order of preference:</p>
        <p>(1) waste reduction and reuse;</p>
        <p>(2) waste recycling;</p>
        <p>(3) composting of source-separated compostable materials, including but not limited to, yard waste and food waste;</p>
        <p>(4) resource recovery through mixed municipal solid waste composting or incineration;</p>
        <p>(5) land disposal which produces no measurable methane gas or which involves the retrieval of methane gas as a fuel for the production of energy to be used on site or for sale; and</p>
        <p>(6) land disposal which produces measurable methane and which does not involve the retrieval of methane gas as a fuel for the production of energy to be used on site or for sale.</p>
        <p>(c) As a means of accomplishing state waste management goals with respect to surplus food and food waste, the following waste management practices are in order of preference:</p>
        <p>(1) reducing the amount generated at the source;</p>
        <p>(2) upcycling or donating for human consumption;</p>
        <p>(3) diversion for animal consumption or leaving crops unharvested;</p>
        <p>(4) composting or anaerobic digestion when the biogas and digestate are not disposed of but are used as a salable product; and</p>
        <p>(5) either using anaerobic digestion, when the biogas is used as a salable product but the digestate is disposed of, or land application of food waste.</p>
        <p>(d) For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given:</p>
        <p>(1) "anaerobic digestion" means a process through which microorganisms break down organic material in the absence of oxygen and generate biogas and digestate;</p>
        <p>(2) "biogas" means a gas that is produced when organic materials decompose and is primarily composed of methane and carbon dioxide;</p>
        <p>(3) "composting" means controlled, aerobic biological decomposition of organic material to produce a nutrient-rich material;</p>
        <p>(4) "digestate" means the solid or liquid residual material remaining after the anaerobic digestion process has been completed;</p>
        <p>(5) "diversion for animal consumption" means diverting food, food scraps, food waste, or surplus food not fitting the conditions of adulteration under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/25.37">25.37</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/34A.02">34A.02</a>;</p>
        <p>(6) "food" means a raw, cooked, processed, or prepared substance, beverage, or ingredient used for, entering into the consumption of, or used or intended for use in the preparation of a food, drink, confectionery, or condiment for humans or animals;</p>
        <p>(7) "food scraps" means inedible food, trimmings from preparing food, and food-processing by-products. Food scraps does not include used cooking oil, grease, any material fitting the conditions of adulteration under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/25.37">25.37</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/34A.02">34A.02</a>, or food that is subject to a governmental or producer recall and that cannot be made to be safe for human or animal consumption;</p>
        <p>(8) "food waste" means all discarded food, surplus food that is not donated, food scraps, food fitting the conditions of adulteration under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/25.37">25.37</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/34A.02">34A.02</a>, and food subject to governmental or producer recall and that cannot be made to be safe for human or animal consumption;</p>
        <p>(9) "land application of food waste" means the direct application of food waste from food manufacturing or processing activities onto or below the surface of the land to enhance soil health;</p>
        <p>(10) "leaving crops unharvested" means not harvesting crops that are otherwise ready for harvesting and instead leaving them in the field or tilling them into the soil;</p>
        <p>(11) "surplus food" means food that is not sold or used and that is still safe to be consumed by humans or animals. Surplus food does not include food damaged by pests, mold, bacteria, or other contamination; food that is subject to governmental or producer recall due to food safety and that cannot be made to be safe for human or animal consumption; or any material fitting the conditions of adulteration under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/25.37">25.37</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/34A.02">34A.02</a>; and</p>
        <p>(12) "upcycling" means capturing, processing, and remaking parts of food and food scraps into new food products for human or animal consumption when the parts of food and food scraps do not fit the conditions of adulteration under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/25.37">25.37</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/34A.02">34A.02</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.02.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.1.2.0">1980 c 564 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/325/#laws.0.2.0">1989 c 325 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.5.0">1991 c 337 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/593/#laws.1.4.0">1992 c 593 art 1 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.2.0">1994 c 585 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.131.0">1999 c 231 s 131</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/272/#laws.0.1.0">2010 c 272 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.2.7.0">2024 c 116 art 2 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.03">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.03 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this chapter, the terms defined in this section have the meanings given them, unless the context requires otherwise.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agency" means the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.270.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 270</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Arrange for management.</span></h2>
          <p>"Arrange for management" means an activity undertaken by a person that determines the ultimate disposition of solid waste that is under the control of the person, including delivery of the waste to a transfer station for transport to another solid waste management facility. Knowledge of the destination of waste by a generator is by itself insufficient for arranging for management unless the generator knows that the destination is an environmentally inferior facility as defined in this section, has the ability to redirect the waste to an environmentally superior facility and ensure its delivery to that facility, and chooses not to redirect the waste.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Cities.</span></h2>
          <p>"Cities" means statutory and home rule charter cities and towns authorized to plan under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/462.351">462.351</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/462.364">462.364</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Collection.</span></h2>
          <p>"Collection" means the aggregation of waste from the place at which it is generated and includes all activities up to the time the waste is delivered to a waste facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Commercial waste facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commercial waste facility" means a waste facility established and permitted to sell waste processing or disposal services to generators other than the owner and operator of the facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.6a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6a.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Construction debris.</span></h2>
          <p>"Construction debris" means waste building materials, packaging, and rubble resulting from construction, remodeling, repair, and demolition of buildings and roads.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.7a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7a.<span class="headnote">Containment.</span></h2>
          <p>"Containment" means isolating, controlling, and monitoring waste in a waste facility in order to prevent a release of waste from the facility that would have an adverse impact upon human health and the environment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Development region.</span></h2>
          <p>"Development region" means a region designated pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/462.381">462.381</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/462.397">462.397</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.8a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.162.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 162</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Disposal or dispose.</span></h2>
          <p>"Disposal" or "dispose" means the discharge, deposit, injection, dumping, spilling, leaking, or placing of any waste into or on any land or water so that the waste or any constituent thereof may enter the environment or be emitted into the air, or discharged into any waters, including groundwaters.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Disposal facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Disposal facility" means a waste facility permitted by the agency that is designed or operated for the purpose of disposing of waste on or in the land, together with any appurtenant facilities needed to process waste for disposal or transfer to another waste facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.10a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10a.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Renumbered subd 10c]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.10b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10b.<span class="headnote">Environmental justice area.</span></h2>
          <p>"Environmental justice area" means one or more census tracts in Minnesota:</p>
          <p>(1) in which, based on the most recent decennial census data published by the United States Census Bureau:</p>
          <p>(i) 40 percent or more of the population is nonwhite;</p>
          <p>(ii) 35 percent or more of the households have an income at or below 200 percent of the federal poverty level; or</p>
          <p>(iii) 40 percent or more of the population over the age of five has limited English proficiency; or</p>
          <p>(2) located within Indian Country, as defined under United States Code, title 18, section 1151.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.10c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10c.<span class="headnote">Environmentally inferior.</span></h2>
          <p>"Environmentally inferior" means a solid waste management method that is lower on the list of preferred waste management methods in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.02">115A.02</a> than a solid waste management method chosen by a county or, as applied to a facility, means a waste management facility that utilizes a waste management method that is lower on the list of preferred waste management methods than the waste management method chosen by a county. In addition, as applied to disposal facilities, a facility that does not meet the standards for new facilities in Code of Federal Regulations, title 40, chapters 257 and 258, is environmentally inferior to a facility that does meet these standards.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Generation.</span></h2>
          <p>"Generation" means the act or process of producing waste.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Generator.</span></h2>
          <p>"Generator" means any person who generates waste.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Hazardous waste.</span></h2>
          <p>"Hazardous waste" has the meaning given it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.06#stat.116.06.11">116.06, subdivision 11</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.13a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13a.<span class="headnote">Industrial waste.</span></h2>
          <p>"Industrial waste" means solid waste resulting from an industrial, manufacturing, service, or commercial activity that is managed as a separate waste stream.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Intrinsic hazard.</span></h2>
          <p>"Intrinsic hazard" of a waste means the propensity of the waste to migrate in the environment, and thereby to become exposed to the public, and the significance of the harm or damage likely to result from exposure of natural resources or the public to the waste, as a result of such inherent or induced attributes of the waste as its chemical and physical stability, solubility, bioconcentratability, toxicity, flammability, and corrosivity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Intrinsic suitability.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Intrinsic suitability" of a land area or site means that, based on existing data on the inherent and natural attributes, physical features, and location of the land area or site, there is no known reason why the waste facility proposed to be located in the area or site cannot reasonably be expected to qualify for permits in accordance with agency rules. Agency certification of intrinsic suitability shall be based on data submitted to the agency by the proposing entity and data included by the administrative law judge in the record of any public hearing on recommended certification, and applied against criteria in agency rules and any additional criteria developed by the agency in effect at the time the proposing entity submits the site for certification.</p>
          <p>(b) In the event that all candidate sites selected by the board before May 3, 1984, are eliminated from further consideration and a new search for candidate sites is commenced, "intrinsic suitability" of a land area or site shall mean that, because of the inherent and natural attributes, physical features, and location of the land area or site, the waste facility proposed to be located in the area or site would not be likely to result in material harm to the public health and safety and natural resources and that therefore the proposed facility can reasonably be expected to qualify for permits in accordance with agency rules.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/7/#laws.1.26.0">1997 c 7 art 1 s 26</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.<span class="headnote">Local government unit.</span></h2>
          <p>"Local government unit" means cities, towns, and counties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.17a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17a.<span class="headnote">Major appliances.</span></h2>
          <p>"Major appliances" means clothes washers and dryers, dishwashers, hot water heaters, heat pumps, furnaces, garbage disposals, trash compactors, conventional and microwave ovens, ranges and stoves, air conditioners, dehumidifiers, refrigerators, and freezers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.18">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18.<span class="headnote">Metropolitan area.</span></h2>
          <p>"Metropolitan area" has the meaning given it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121">473.121</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.19">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 19.<span class="headnote">Metropolitan Council.</span></h2>
          <p>"Metropolitan Council" means the council established in chapter 473.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.20">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 20.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/628/#laws.3.209.0">1994 c 628 art 3 s 209</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.21">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 21.<span class="headnote">Mixed municipal solid waste.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) "Mixed municipal solid waste" means garbage, refuse, and other solid waste from residential, commercial, industrial, and community activities that the generator of the waste aggregates for collection, except as provided in paragraph (b).</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Mixed municipal solid waste does not include auto hulks, street sweepings, ash, construction debris, mining waste, sludges, tree and agricultural wastes, tires, lead acid batteries, motor and vehicle fluids and filters, and other materials collected, processed, and disposed of as separate waste streams.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.22">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 22.<span class="headnote">Natural resources.</span></h2>
          <p>"Natural resources" has the meaning given it in chapter 116B.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.22a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 22a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.162.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 162</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.22b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 22b.<span class="headnote">Packaging.</span></h2>
          <p>"Packaging" means a container and any appurtenant material that provide a means of transporting, marketing, protecting, or handling a product. "Packaging" includes pallets and packing such as blocking, bracing, cushioning, weatherproofing, strapping, coatings, closures, inks, dyes, pigments, and labels.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.23">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 23.<span class="headnote">Person.</span></h2>
          <p>"Person" has the meaning given it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.06">116.06</a>, but does not include the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.24">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 24.<span class="headnote">Political subdivision.</span></h2>
          <p>"Political subdivision" means any municipal corporation, governmental subdivision of the state, local government unit, special district, or local or regional board, commission, or authority authorized by law to plan or provide for waste management.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.24a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 24a.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Renumbered subd 24c]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.24b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 24b.<span class="headnote">Postconsumer material.</span></h2>
          <p>"Postconsumer material" means a finished material that would normally be discarded as a solid waste having completed its life cycle as a consumer item.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.24c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 24c.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered subd 24e]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.24d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 24d.<span class="headnote">Prepared sewage sludge.</span></h2>
          <p>"Prepared sewage sludge" means exceptional quality sewage sludge, as defined in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/7041.0100">7041.0100</a>, subpart 20, applied to a lawn or home garden and sold or given away in a bag or other container that:</p>
          <p>(1) meets low limits on metal concentrations;</p>
          <p>(2) has been treated to ensure pathogens, pollutants, and vectors that can transport disease have been carefully managed; and</p>
          <p>(3) is labeled with the nutrient content.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.24e">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 24e.<span class="headnote">Problem material.</span></h2>
          <p>"Problem material" means a material that, when it is processed or disposed of with mixed municipal solid waste, contributes to one or more of the following results:</p>
          <p>(1) the release of a hazardous substance, or pollutant or contaminant, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.02">115B.02, subdivisions 8, 13, and 15</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) pollution of water as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.01#stat.115.01.13">115.01, subdivision 13</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) air pollution as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.06#stat.116.06.4">116.06, subdivision 4</a>; or</p>
          <p>(4) a significant threat to the safe or efficient operation of a solid waste facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.25">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 25.<span class="headnote">Processing.</span></h2>
          <p>"Processing" means the treatment of waste after collection and before disposal. Processing includes but is not limited to reduction, storage, separation, exchange, resource recovery, physical, chemical, or biological modification, and transfer from one waste facility to another.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.25a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 25a.<span class="headnote">Recyclable materials.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Recyclable materials" means materials that are separated from mixed municipal solid waste for the purpose of recycling or composting, including paper, glass, plastics, metals, automobile oil, batteries, source-separated compostable materials, and sole source food waste streams that are managed through biodegradative processes. Refuse-derived fuel or other material that is destroyed by incineration is not a recyclable material.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.25b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 25b.<span class="headnote">Recycling.</span></h2>
          <p>"Recycling" means the process of collecting and preparing recyclable materials and reusing the materials in their original form or using them in manufacturing processes that do not cause the destruction of recyclable materials in a manner that precludes further use.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.25c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 25c.<span class="headnote">Recycling facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Recycling facility" means a facility at which materials are prepared for reuse in their original form or for use in manufacturing processes that do not cause the destruction of the materials in a manner that precludes further use.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.25d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 25d.<span class="headnote">Refuse-derived fuel.</span></h2>
          <p>"Refuse-derived fuel" means a product resulting from the processing of mixed municipal solid waste in a manner that reduces the quantity of noncombustible material present in the waste, reduces the size of waste components through shredding or other mechanical means, and produces a fuel suitable for combustion in existing or new solid fuel-fired boilers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.26">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 26.<span class="headnote">Regional development commission.</span></h2>
          <p>"Regional development commission" means a commission established pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/462.381">462.381</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/462.397">462.397</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.26a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 26a.<span class="headnote">Resource conservation.</span></h2>
          <p>"Resource conservation" means the reduction in the use of water, energy, and raw materials.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.27">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 27.<span class="headnote">Resource recovery.</span></h2>
          <p>"Resource recovery" means the reclamation for sale, use, or reuse of materials, substances, energy, or other products contained within or derived from waste.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.28">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 28.<span class="headnote">Resource recovery facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Resource recovery facility" means a waste facility established and used primarily for resource recovery, including related and appurtenant facilities such as transmission facilities and transfer stations primarily serving the resource recovery facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.28a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 28a.<span class="headnote">Retrievable storage.</span></h2>
          <p>"Retrievable storage" means a method of disposal whereby wastes are placed in a facility established pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.18">115A.18</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.30">115A.30</a> for an indeterminate period in a manner designed to allow the removal of the waste at a later time.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.28b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 28b.<span class="headnote">Sanitary district.</span></h2>
          <p>"Sanitary district" means a sanitary district with the authority to regulate solid waste.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.29">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 29.<span class="headnote">Sewage sludge.</span></h2>
          <p>"Sewage sludge" means solid, semisolid, or liquid residue generated during the treatment of domestic sewage in a treatment works. It includes, but is not limited to, scum or solids removed in primary, secondary, or advanced wastewater treatment processes and a material derived from sewage sludge. Sewage sludge does not include ash generated during the firing of sewage sludge in a sewage sludge incinerator or grit and screenings generated during preliminary treatment of domestic sewage in a treatment works. Sewage sludge that is acceptable and beneficial for recycling on land as a soil conditioner and nutrient source is also known as biosolids.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.30">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 30.<span class="headnote">Sewage sludge disposal facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Sewage sludge disposal facility" means property owned or leased by a political subdivision and used for interim or final disposal or land spreading of sewage sludge.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.31">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 31.<span class="headnote">Solid waste.</span></h2>
          <p>"Solid waste" has the meaning given it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.06#stat.116.06.22">116.06, subdivision 22</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.32">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 32.<span class="headnote">Solid waste management district or waste district.</span></h2>
          <p>"Solid waste management district" or "waste district" means a geographic area extending into two or more counties in which the management of solid waste is vested in a special district established pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.62">115A.62</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.72">115A.72</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.32a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 32a.</h2>
          <p>MS 1994 [Renumbered subd 32c]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.32a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 32a.<span class="headnote">Source-separated compostable materials.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Source-separated compostable materials" means materials that:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) are separated at the source by waste generators for the purpose of preparing them for use as compost;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) are collected separately from mixed municipal solid waste, and are governed by the licensing provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.93">115A.93</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) are comprised of food wastes, fish and animal waste, plant materials, diapers, sanitary products, and paper that is not recyclable because the commissioner has determined that no other person is willing to accept the paper for recycling;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) are delivered to a facility to undergo controlled microbial degradation to yield a humus-like product meeting the agency's class I or class II, or equivalent, compost standards and where process rejects do not exceed 15 percent by weight of the total material delivered to the facility; and</p>
          <p class="in">(5) may be delivered to a transfer station, mixed municipal solid waste processing facility, or recycling facility only for the purposes of composting or transfer to a composting facility, unless the commissioner determines that no other person is willing to accept the materials.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.32b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 32b.</h2>
          <p>MS 1994 [Renumbered subd 32d]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.32b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 32b.<span class="headnote">Source-separated recyclable materials.</span></h2>
          <p>"Source-separated recyclable materials" means recyclable materials, including commingled recyclable materials, that are separated by the generator.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.32c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 32c.<span class="headnote">Stabilization.</span></h2>
          <p>"Stabilization" means a chemical or thermal process in which materials or energy are added to waste in order to reduce the possibility of migration of any hazardous constituents of the resulting stabilized waste in preparation for placement of the waste in a stabilization and containment facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.32d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 32d.<span class="headnote">Stabilization and containment facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Stabilization and containment facility" means a waste facility that is designed for stabilization and containment of waste, together with other appurtenant facilities needed to process waste for stabilization, containment, or transfer to another facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.33">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 33.<span class="headnote">Transfer station.</span></h2>
          <p>"Transfer station" means an intermediate waste facility in which waste collected from any source is temporarily deposited to await transportation to another waste facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.34">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 34.<span class="headnote">Waste.</span></h2>
          <p>"Waste" means solid waste, sewage sludge, and hazardous waste.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.35">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 35.<span class="headnote">Waste facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Waste facility" means all property, real or personal, including negative and positive easements and water and air rights, which is or may be needed or useful for the processing or disposal of waste, except property for the collection of the waste and property used primarily for the manufacture of scrap metal or paper. Waste facility includes but is not limited to transfer stations, processing facilities, and disposal sites and facilities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.36">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 36.<span class="headnote">Waste management.</span></h2>
          <p>"Waste management" means activities which are intended to affect or control the generation of waste and activities which provide for or control the collection, processing and disposal of waste.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.36a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 36a.<span class="headnote">Waste management method chosen by a county.</span></h2>
          <p>"Waste management method chosen by a county" means:</p>
          <p>(1) a waste management method that is mandated for waste generated in the county by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.415">115A.415</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.848">473.848</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.849">473.849</a>, or other state law, or by county ordinance based on the county solid waste management plan developed, adopted, and approved under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.46">115A.46</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/458D.05">458D.05</a> or the county solid waste management master plan developed, adopted, and approved under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.803">473.803</a>; or</p>
          <p>(2) a waste management facility or facilities, developed under the county solid waste management plan or master plan, to which solid waste generated in a county is directed by an ordinance developed, adopted, and approved under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.80">115A.80</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.893">115A.893</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.36b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 36b.<span class="headnote">Waste reduction or source reduction.</span></h2>
          <p>"Waste reduction" or "source reduction" means an activity that prevents generation of waste or the inclusion of toxic materials in waste, including:</p>
          <p>(1) reusing a product in its original form;</p>
          <p>(2) increasing the life span of a product;</p>
          <p>(3) reducing material or the toxicity of material used in production or packaging; or</p>
          <p>(4) changing procurement, consumption, or waste generation habits to result in smaller quantities or lower toxicity of waste generated.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.37">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 37.<span class="headnote">Waste rendered nonhazardous.</span></h2>
          <p>"Waste rendered nonhazardous" means (1) waste excluded from regulation as a hazardous waste under the delisting requirements of United States Code, title 42, section 6921 and any federal and state delisting rules, and (2) other nonhazardous residual waste from the processing of hazardous waste.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.37a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 37a.<span class="headnote">Waste treated seed.</span></h2>
          <p>"Waste treated seed" means seed that is treated, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/21.81#stat.21.81.28">21.81, subdivision 28</a>, and that is withdrawn from sale or that the end user considers unusable or otherwise a waste.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.03.38">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 38.<span class="headnote">Yard waste.</span></h2>
          <p>"Yard waste" means garden wastes, leaves, lawn cuttings, weeds, shrub and tree waste, and prunings.</p>
        </div>
      </div>

			">

      <h1 class="fgh">ENFORCEMENT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.034">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.034 ENFORCEMENT.</h1>
        <p>This chapter may be enforced under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.071">115.071</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072">116.072</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.034.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/593/#laws.1.8.0">1992 c 593 art 1 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/249/#laws.0.9.0">1993 c 249 s 9</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.04"><b>115A.04</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.270.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 270</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.05"><b>115A.05</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.270.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 270</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.115A.055">
        <h1>115A.055 </h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.055.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.162.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 162</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.055.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/13/#laws.2.5.0">2007 c 13 art 2 s 5</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">POLLUTION CONTROL AGENCY</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.06">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.06 POWERS OF POLLUTION CONTROL AGENCY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.06.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.270.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 270</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.06.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless otherwise provided, the commissioner shall promulgate rules in accordance with chapter 14 to govern the agency's activities and implement this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.06.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.270.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 270</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.06.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/310/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 310 s 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.06.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Right of access.</span></h2>
          <p>Whenever the agency or the commissioner acting on behalf of the agency deems it necessary to the accomplishment of its purposes, the agency or any member, employee, or agent thereof, when authorized by it or the commissioner, may enter upon any property, public or private, for the purpose of obtaining information or conducting surveys or investigations, provided that the entrance and activity is undertaken after reasonable notice and during normal business hours and provided that compensation is made for any damages to the property caused by the entrance and activity. The agency may pay a reasonable estimate of the damages it believes will be caused by the entrance and activity before entering any property.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.06.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Acquiring easements.</span></h2>
          <p>If the agency determines that any activity deemed necessary to accomplish its purposes under subdivision 5 constitutes a substantial interference with the possession, enjoyment, or value of the property where the activity will take place, the agency may acquire a temporary easement interest in the property that permits the agency to carry out the activity and other activities incidental to the accomplishment of the same purposes. The agency may acquire temporary easement interests under this subdivision by purchase, gift, or condemnation. The right of the agency to acquire a temporary easement is subject to the same requirements and may be exercised with the same authority as provided for acquisition of property interests by the commissioner of administration under Minnesota Statutes 1994, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.06#stat.115A.06.4">115A.06, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.06.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Gifts and grants.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency, or the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency or commissioner of administration on behalf of the agency, may apply for and accept gifts, loans, or other property from the United States, the state, or any person for any of the purposes of the agency, may enter into any agreement required in connection therewith, and may hold, use, and dispose of the money or property in accordance with the terms of the gift, grant, loan or agreement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.06.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Property exempt from taxation.</span></h2>
          <p>Any real or personal property owned, used, or occupied by the agency or the commissioner of administration for any purpose referred to in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.01">115A.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.72">115A.72</a> is declared to be acquired, owned, used, and occupied for public and governmental purposes, and shall be exempt from taxation by the state or any political subdivision of or other governmental unit of or within the state, provided that those properties shall be subject to special assessments levied for a local improvement in amounts proportionate to and not exceeding the special benefit received by the properties from the improvement. No possible use of the properties in any manner different from their use for hazardous waste management at the time shall be considered in determining the special benefit received by the properties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.06.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Contracts.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may enter into any contract necessary or proper for the exercise of the commissioner's powers or the accomplishment of the agency's purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.06.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Joint powers.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may act under the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a>, or any other law providing for joint or cooperative action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.06.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Research.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may conduct research studies and programs, collect and analyze data, prepare reports, maps, charts, and tables, and order all necessary hearings and investigations in connection with the commissioner's work and may advise and assist other government units on planning matters within the scope of the commissioner's powers, duties, and objectives.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.06.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Employees; contracts for services.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may employ persons and contract for services to perform research, engineering, legal, or other services necessary to carry out the commissioner's functions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.06.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Insurance.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may procure insurance in amounts the commissioner deems necessary to insure against liability of the agency and employees or both, for personal injury or death and property damage or destruction, with the force and effect stated in chapter 466, and against risks of damage to or destruction of any of the agency's property as the commissioner deems necessary.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.06.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Private and nonpublic data.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Any data held by the commissioner which consists of trade secret information as defined by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.37#stat.13.37.1">13.37, subdivision 1</a>, clause (b), or sales information, shall be classified as private or nonpublic data as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02">13.02</a>, subdivisions 9 and 12. When data is classified private or nonpublic pursuant to this subdivision the commissioner may:</p>
          <p>(1) use the data to compile and publish analyses or summaries and to carry out the commissioner's statutory responsibilities in a manner which does not identify the subject of the data; or</p>
          <p>(2) disclose the data when the commissioner is obligated to disclose it to comply with federal law or regulation but only to the extent required by the federal law or regulation.</p>
          <p>(b) The subject of data classified as private or nonpublic pursuant to this subdivision may authorize the disclosure of some or all of that data by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.06.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Waste rendered nonhazardous and industrial waste.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall encourage improved management of waste rendered nonhazardous and industrial waste that should be managed separately from mixed municipal solid waste, and may provide technical and planning assistance to political subdivisions, waste generators, and others for the purpose of identifying, developing, and implementing alternative management methods for those wastes.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.06.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.2.3.0">1980 c 564 art 2 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/311/#laws.0.39.0">1981 c 311 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/352/#laws.0.4.0">1981 c 352 s 4</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/545/#laws.0.24.0">1982 c 545 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/569/#laws.0.1.0">1982 c 569 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/373/#laws.0.9.0">1983 c 373 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/644/#laws.0.3.0">1984 c 644 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/425/#laws.0.19.0">1986 c 425 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/348/#laws.0.3.0">1987 c 348 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/199/#laws.2.7.0">1991 c 199 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/326/#laws.0.5.0">1991 c 326 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.8.0">1991 c 337 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.5.3.0">1994 c 639 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.1.29.0">2002 c 379 art 1 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.07">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.07 DUTIES; GENERAL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.07.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Interagency coordination.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency shall inform the commissioner of employment and economic development of the agency's activities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.07.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.98.0">2012 c 272 s 98</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.07.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Uniform waste statistics; rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner, after consulting with local government units and other interested persons, may adopt rules to establish uniform methods for collecting and reporting waste reduction, generation, collection, transportation, storage, recycling, processing, and disposal statistics necessary for proper waste management and for reporting required by law. Prior to publishing proposed rules, the commissioner shall submit draft rules to the senate and house committees having jurisdiction over environment and natural resources and environment and natural resources finance for review and comment. Rules adopted under this subdivision apply to all persons and units of government in the state for the purpose of collecting and reporting waste-related statistics requested under or required by law.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.07.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.2.4.0">1980 c 564 art 2 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.119.0">1981 c 356 s 119</a>,248; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/289/#laws.0.115.0">1983 c 289 s 115</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/312/#laws.1.26.0">1987 c 312 art 1 s 26</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/384/#laws.2.18.0">1987 c 384 art 2 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/593/#laws.1.9.0">1992 c 593 art 1 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.2.2.0">1995 c 247 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.27.0">1996 c 470 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/4/#laws.0.1.0">1Sp2003 c 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.071"><b>115A.071</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/644/#laws.0.82.0">1984 c 644 s 82</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.0715"><b>115A.0715</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.28.0">1996 c 470 s 28</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.0716">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.0716 ENVIRONMENTAL ASSISTANCE GRANT AND LOAN PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.0716.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Grants.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may make grants to any person for the purpose of researching, developing, and implementing projects or practices related to collection, processing, recycling, reuse, resource recovery, source reduction, and prevention of waste, hazardous substances, toxic pollutants, and problem materials; the development or implementation of pollution prevention projects or practices; the collection, recovery, processing, purchasing, or market development of recyclable materials or compost; resource conservation; and for environmental education.</p>
          <p>(b) In making grants, the agency may give priority to projects or practices that have broad application in the state and are consistent with the policies established under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.02">115A.02</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115D.02">115D.02</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner shall adopt rules to administer the grant program.</p>
          <p>(d) For the purposes of this section:</p>
          <p>(1) "pollution prevention" has the meaning given it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115D.03">115D.03</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) "toxic pollutant" has the meaning given it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115D.03">115D.03</a>; and</p>
          <p>(3) "hazardous substance" has the meaning given it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115D.03">115D.03</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.0716.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Loans.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may make loans, or participate in loans, for capital costs or improvements related to any of the activities listed in subdivision 1.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may work with financial institutions or other financial assistance providers in participating in loans under this section. The commissioner may contract with financial institutions or other financial assistance providers for loan processing and/or administration.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may also make grants, as authorized in subdivision 1, to enable persons to receive loans from financial institutions or to reduce interest payments for those loans.</p>
          <p>(d) In making loans, the agency may give priority to projects or practices that have broad application in the state and are consistent with the policies established under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.02">115A.02</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115D.02">115D.02</a>.</p>
          <p>(e) The commissioner shall adopt rules to administer the loan program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.0716.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Revolving account.</span></h2>
          <p>All repayments of loans awarded under this section, including principal and interest, must be credited to the environmental fund. Money deposited in the fund under this section is annually appropriated to the commissioner for loans for purposes identified in subdivisions 1 and 2.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.0716.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.6.0">1996 c 470 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.122.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 122</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.2.5.0">2003 c 128 art 2 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/212/#laws.3.8.0">2006 c 212 art 3 s 8</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.072">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.072 PUBLIC EDUCATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.072.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Environmental education programs.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall provide for the development and implementation of environmental education programs that are designed to meet the goals listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.073">115A.073</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.072.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Waste management education.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to the commissioner's general duties established in subdivision 1, the commissioner shall:</p>
          <p>(1) develop a statewide waste management public education campaign with materials that may be easily adapted by political subdivisions to meet their program needs; and</p>
          <p>(2) develop and make available to schools educational curricula on waste education for grades kindergarten to 12 to address at least waste reduction, recycling, litter, and proper management and disposal of problem materials.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.072.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.28.0">1996 c 470 s 28</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.072.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Waste reduction and reuse program; procurement.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall include: (1) waste reduction and reuse, including packaging reduction and reuse; and (2) the hazards of open burning, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/88.01">88.01</a>, of mixed municipal solid waste, especially the hazards of dioxin emissions to children, as elements of the commissioner's program of public education on waste management required under this section. The waste reduction and reuse education program must include dissemination of information and may include an award program for model waste reduction and reuse efforts. Waste reduction and reuse educational efforts must also include provision of information about and promotion of the model procurement program developed by the commissioner of administration under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.15#stat.115A.15.7">115A.15, subdivision 7</a>, or any other model procurement program that results in significant waste reduction and reuse.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.072.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/348/#laws.0.4.0">1987 c 348 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.21.1.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 21 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/345/#laws.2.19.0">1991 c 345 art 2 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/480/#laws.0.7.0">1994 c 480 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.4.0">1994 c 585 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.5.3.0">1994 c 639 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.1.4.0">1995 c 247 art 1 s 4</a>,5; art 2 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.9.1.0">1996 c 412 art 9 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">1998 c 397 art 11 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.128.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 128</a>,161; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/76/#laws.1.6.0">2011 c 76 art 1 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.073">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.073 ENVIRONMENTAL EDUCATION GOALS AND PLAN.</h1>
        <p>The environmental education program described in this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.074">115A.074</a> has these goals for the pupils and other citizens of this state:</p>
        <p>(a) Pupils and citizens should be able to apply informed decision-making processes to maintain a sustainable lifestyle. In order to do so, citizens should:</p>
        <p>(1) understand ecological systems;</p>
        <p>(2) understand the cause and effect relationship between human attitudes and behavior and the environment;</p>
        <p>(3) be able to evaluate alternative responses to environmental issues before deciding on alternative courses of action; and</p>
        <p>(4) understand the effects of multiple uses of the environment.</p>
        <p>(b) Pupils and citizens shall have access to information and experiences needed to make informed decisions about actions to take on environmental issues.</p>
        <p>(c) For the purposes of this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.074">115A.074</a>, "state plan" means "Greenprint for Minnesota: A State Plan for Environmental Education."</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.073.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/595/#laws.0.1.0">1990 c 595 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.12.32.0">1993 c 224 art 12 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/374/#laws.0.22.0">1993 c 374 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.11.3.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 11 s 3</a>,20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.3.98.0">1998 c 397 art 3 s 98</a>,103; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.074">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.074 ENVIRONMENTAL EDUCATION RESOURCE CENTERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.074.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may establish environmental education resource centers throughout the state as needed. The environmental education resource centers shall serve as a source of information and programs for citizens, provide ongoing contact with the public for feedback to the commissioner on regional environmental education issues and priorities, and serve as distribution centers for environmental education programs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.074.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The resource centers shall:</p>
          <p>(1) implement the programs and priorities of the agency as defined in the plan;</p>
          <p>(2) convey regional program priorities to the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(3) evaluate regional implementation of environmental education programs and report to the commissioner on the evaluations;</p>
          <p>(4) provide regional liaison and coordination for organizations, agencies, and individuals providing environmental education programs on particular issues;</p>
          <p>(5) be a distribution and publicity center for agencies, environmental organizations, environmental learning center publications, programs, and services;</p>
          <p>(6) be a central source of information for citizens interested in issues that are the responsibility of many agencies, boards, task forces, and organizations;</p>
          <p>(7) provide technical assistance to local and state organizations and agencies on program design, promotion, and publicity to reach the chosen target audiences; and</p>
          <p>(8) assist the service cooperatives by collecting and distributing environmental education teaching materials, displays, computer programs, resource person lists, and audio-visual aids, and provide assistance with teacher training workshops and programs on request.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.074.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/595/#laws.0.6.0">1990 c 595 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/305/#laws.1.138.0">1996 c 305 art 1 s 138</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.3.103.0">1998 c 397 art 3 s 103</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.075">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.075 LEGISLATIVE POLICY AGAINST DISPOSAL OF HAZARDOUS WASTE.</h1>
        <p>(a) The legislature finds that hazardous waste must be managed in a manner that protects the health, safety, and welfare of the citizens of the state and protects and conserves the state's natural resources and environment; that reduction of the amount of waste generated and processing, treatment, separation, and resource recovery are the preferred methods to manage hazardous waste; and that disposal of hazardous waste should be used only as a last resort when all other management methods are ineffective, and then only if an environmentally suitable site can be identified in the state.</p>
        <p>(b) The agency, in its planning, facility approval, and other activities related to hazardous waste shall give first priority to eliminating the generation of hazardous waste and eliminating or reducing the hazardous character of the waste generated in the state through processing, treatment, separation, and resource recovery.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.075.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/644/#laws.0.4.0">1984 c 644 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.08"><b>115A.08</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/310/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 310 s 1</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.09"><b>115A.09</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/310/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 310 s 1</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.10">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.10 DUTIES OF POLLUTION CONTROL AGENCY; HAZARDOUS WASTE FACILITIES; ENCOURAGING PRIVATE ENTERPRISE.</h1>
        <p>The agency and the commissioner on behalf of the agency shall encourage the development and operation of hazardous waste facilities by private enterprise to the extent practicable and consistent with the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.01">115A.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.72">115A.72</a> and the agency's hazardous waste management plan adopted pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.11">115A.11</a>. In adopting the management plan, and in its actions and decisions under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.18">115A.18</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.30">115A.30</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.32">115A.32</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.39">115A.39</a>, the agency and the commissioner on behalf of the agency shall solicit the active participation of private waste management firms and shall so conduct its activities as to encourage private permit applications for facilities needed in the state.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.10.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.2.7.0">1980 c 564 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/373/#laws.0.14.0">1983 c 373 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/7/#laws.1.27.0">1997 c 7 art 1 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.11">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.11 HAZARDOUS WASTE MANAGEMENT PLAN.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.11.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency shall adopt, amend as appropriate, and implement a hazardous waste management plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.11.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Policy.</span></h2>
          <p>In developing and implementing the plan, the highest priority of the agency must be placed upon alternatives to land disposal of hazardous wastes including: technologies to modify industrial processes or introduce new processes that will reduce or eliminate hazardous waste generation; recycling, reuse, and recovery methods to reduce or eliminate hazardous waste disposal; and conversion and treatment technologies to reduce the degree of environmental risk from hazardous waste. The agency shall also consider technologies for retrievable storage of hazardous wastes for later recycling, reuse, recovery, conversion, or treatment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.11.1b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1b.<span class="headnote">Contents.</span></h2>
          <p>The plan must include at least the elements prescribed in this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(a) The plan must estimate the types and quantities of hazardous waste that will be generated in the state through the year 2000.</p>
          <p>(b) The plan must set out specific and quantifiable objectives for reducing to the greatest feasible and prudent extent the need for and use of disposal facilities located within the state, through waste reduction, pretreatment, retrievable storage, processing, and resource recovery.</p>
          <p>(c) The plan must estimate the minimum disposal capacity and capability required by generators in the state for use through the year 2000. The estimate must be based on the achievement of the objectives under paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(d) The plan must describe and recommend the implementation strategies required to assure availability of disposal capacity for the types and quantities of waste estimated under paragraph (c) and to achieve the objectives required by paragraph (b). The recommendations must address at least the following: the necessary private and government actions; the types of facilities and programs required; the availability and use of specific facilities outside of the state; development schedules for facilities, services, and rules that should be established in the state; revenue-raising and financing measures; levels of public and private effort and expenditure; legal and institutional changes; and other similar matters.</p>
          <p>(e) The plan must provide for the orderly development of hazardous waste management sites and facilities to protect the health and safety of rural and urban communities. In preparing the plan the agency shall consider its impact upon agriculture and natural resources.</p>
          <p>(f) The plan must include methods and procedures that will encourage the establishment of programs, services, and facilities that the agency recommends for development in the state for the recycling, reuse, recovery, conversion, treatment, destruction, transfer, storage, or disposal, including retrievable storage, of hazardous waste.</p>
          <p>(g) The plan must be consistent with the estimate of need and feasibility analysis prepared under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.24">115A.24</a> and the decisions made by the agency under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.28">115A.28</a>.</p>
          <p>(h) The agency may make the implementation of elements of the plan contingent on actions of the legislature that have been recommended in the draft plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.11.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Procedure.</span></h2>
          <p>The plan and the procedures for hearings on the plan are not subject to the contested case provisions of chapter 14. Before revising the draft plan or amending its adopted plan, the agency shall provide notice and hold a public meeting.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.11.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.270.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 270</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.11.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.2.8.0">1980 c 564 art 2 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/615/#laws.0.60.0">1980 c 615 s 60</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/352/#laws.0.11.0">1981 c 352 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/569/#laws.0.5.0">1982 c 569 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/373/#laws.0.15.0">1983 c 373 s 15</a>,16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/644/#laws.0.8.0">1984 c 644 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/348/#laws.0.5.0">1987 c 348 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/7/#laws.1.28.0">1997 c 7 art 1 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/187/#laws.1.9.0">1997 c 187 art 1 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.12"><b>115A.12</b> MS 2008 [Expired, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.129.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 129</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.121">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.121 TOXICS AND POLLUTION PREVENTION EVALUATION; CONSOLIDATED REPORT.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall prepare and adopt a report on pollution prevention activities required in chapters 115A, 115D, and 325E. The report must include activities required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1320">115A.1320</a>. The commissioner must submit the report to the senate and house of representatives committees having jurisdiction over environment and natural resources by December 31, 2013, and every four years thereafter.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.121.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.63.0">2012 c 272 s 63</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.13"><b>115A.13</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/348/#laws.0.52.0">1987 c 348 s 52</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">VIDEO DISPLAYS AND ELECTRONIC DEVICES; <br>COLLECTION AND RECYCLING</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.1310">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.1310 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1310">115A.1310</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1330">115A.1330</a>, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
        </div>
