        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Cathode-ray tube or CRT.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Cathode-ray tube" or "CRT" means a vacuum tube or picture tube used to convert an electronic signal into a visual image.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Collection.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Collection" means the aggregation of covered electronic devices from households and includes all the activities up to the time the covered electronic devices are delivered to a recycler.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Collector.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Collector" means a public or private entity that receives covered electronic devices from households and arranges for the delivery of the devices to a recycler.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Computer.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Computer" means an electronic, magnetic, optical, electrochemical, or other high-speed data processing device performing logical, arithmetic, or storage functions, but does not include an automated typewriter or typesetter, a portable handheld calculator or device, or other similar device.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Computer monitor.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Computer monitor" means an electronic device that is a cathode-ray tube or flat panel display primarily intended to display information from a central processing unit or the Internet.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Covered electronic device.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Covered electronic device" means computers, including tablet computers and laptop computers, peripherals, facsimile machines, DVD players, video cassette recorders, and video display devices that are sold to a household by means of retail, wholesale, or electronic commerce.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Department.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Department" means the Department of Revenue.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Dwelling unit.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Dwelling unit" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/238.02">238.02</a>, subdivision 21a.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Household.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Household" means an occupant of a single detached dwelling unit or a single unit of a multiple dwelling unit located in this state who has used a video display device at a dwelling unit primarily for personal use.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Manufacturer.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Manufacturer" means a person who:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) manufactures video display devices to be sold under its own brand as identified by its own brand label; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) sells video display devices manufactured by others under its own brand as identified by its own brand label.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Peripheral.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Peripheral" means a keyboard, printer, or any other device sold exclusively for external use with a computer that provides input or output into or from a computer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.12a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12a.<span class="headnote">Phase I recycling credits.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Phase I recycling credits" means the number of pounds of covered electronic devices recycled by a manufacturer from households during program years one through nine, less the product of the number of pounds of video display devices sold to households during the same program year, multiplied by the proportion of sales a manufacturer is required to recycle.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.12b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12b.<span class="headnote">Phase II recycling credits.</span></h2>
          <p>"Phase II recycling credits" means an amount calculated in a program year beginning July 1, 2019, and in each program year thereafter, according to the formula (1.5 x A) - (B - C), where:</p>
          <p>A = the number of pounds of covered electronic devices a manufacturer recycled or arranged to have collected and recycled during a program year from households located outside the 11-county metropolitan area, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1314#stat.115A.1314.2">115A.1314, subdivision 2</a>;</p>
          <p>B = the manufacturer's recycling obligation calculated for the same program year in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1320#stat.115A.1320.1">115A.1320, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (g); and</p>
          <p>C = the number of pounds of covered electronic devices a manufacturer recycled or arranged to have collected and recycled, up to but not exceeding B, during the same program year from households in the 11-county metropolitan area.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.12c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12c.<span class="headnote">Portable battery.</span></h2>
          <p>"Portable battery" means a rechargeable battery as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.9157">115A.9157</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Program year.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Program year" means the period from July 1 through June 30.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Recycler.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Recycler" means a public or private individual or entity who accepts covered electronic devices from households and collectors for the purpose of recycling. A manufacturer who takes products for refurbishment or repair is not a recycler.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Recycling.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Recycling" means the process of collecting and preparing video display devices or covered electronic devices for use in manufacturing processes or for recovery of usable materials followed by delivery of such materials for use. Recycling does not include the destruction by incineration or other process or land disposal of recyclable materials nor reuse, repair, or any other process through which video display devices or covered electronic devices are returned to use for households in their original form.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/166/#laws.0.18.0">2016 c 166 s 18</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.<span class="headnote">Retailer.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Retailer" means a person who sells, rents, or leases, through sales outlets, catalogs, or the Internet, a video display device to a household and not for resale in any form.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.18">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18.<span class="headnote">Sell or sale.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Sell" or "sale" means any transfer for consideration of title or of the right to use, by lease or sales contract, including, but not limited to, transactions conducted through sales outlets, catalogs, or the Internet, or any other similar electronic means either inside or outside of the state, by a person who conducts the transaction and controls the delivery of a video display device to a consumer in the state, but does not include a manufacturer's or distributor's wholesale transaction with a distributor or a retailer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.19">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 19.<span class="headnote">Television.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Television" means an electronic device that is a cathode-ray tube or flat panel display primarily intended to receive video programming via broadcast, cable, or satellite transmission or video from surveillance or other similar cameras.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1310.20">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 20.<span class="headnote">Video display device.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Video display device" means a television or computer monitor that contains a cathode-ray tube or a flat panel screen that is marketed by manufacturers for use by households. Video display device does not include any of the following:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a video display device that is part of a motor vehicle or any component part of a motor vehicle assembled by, or for, a vehicle manufacturer or franchised dealer, including replacement parts for use in a motor vehicle;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a video display device, including a touch-screen display, that is functionally or physically part of a larger piece of equipment or is designed and intended for use in an industrial; commercial, including retail; library checkout; traffic control; kiosk; security, other than household security; border control; or medical setting, including diagnostic, monitoring, or control equipment;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) a video display device that is contained within a clothes washer, clothes dryer, refrigerator, refrigerator and freezer, microwave oven, conventional oven or range, dishwasher, room air conditioner, dehumidifier, or air purifier; or</p>
          <p class="in">(4) a telephone of any type.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.1310.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/48/#laws.0.1.0">2007 c 48 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/166/#laws.0.1.0">2016 c 166 s 1</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.85.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 85</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.1312">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.1312 REGISTRATION PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1312.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Requirements for sale.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) On or after September 1, 2007, a manufacturer must not sell or offer for sale or deliver to retailers for subsequent sale a new video display device unless:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the video display device is labeled with the manufacturer's brand, which label is permanently affixed and readily visible; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the manufacturer has filed a registration with the agency, as specified in subdivision 2.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A retailer must not sell, offer for sale, rent, or lease a video display device unless the video display device is labeled according to this subdivision and listed as registered on the agency website according to subdivision 2.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A retailer is not responsible for an unlawful sale under this subdivision if the manufacturer's registration expired or was revoked and the retailer took possession of the video display device prior to the expiration or revocation of the manufacturer's registration and the unlawful sale occurred within six months after the expiration or revocation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1312.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Manufacturer registration.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) By August 15 each year, a manufacturer of video display devices sold or offered for sale to households in the state must submit a registration to the agency that includes:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a list of the manufacturer's brands of video display devices offered for sale in this state;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the name, address, and contact information of a person responsible for ensuring compliance with this chapter; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) a certification that the manufacturer has complied and will continue to comply with the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1312">115A.1312</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1318">115A.1318</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A manufacturer of video display devices sold or offered for sale to a household must include in the registration submitted under paragraph (a), a statement disclosing whether:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) any video display devices sold to households exceed the maximum concentration values established for lead, mercury, cadmium, hexavalent chromium, polybrominated biphenyls (PBB's), and polybrominated diphenyl ethers (PBDE's) under the RoHS (restricting the use of certain hazardous substances in electrical and electronic equipment) Directive 2002/95/EC of the European Parliament and Council and any amendments thereto; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the manufacturer has received an exemption from one or more of those maximum concentration values under the RoHS Directive that has been approved and published by the European Commission.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A manufacturer who begins to sell or offer for sale video display devices to households after August 15, 2016, and has not filed a registration under this subdivision must submit a registration to the agency within ten days of beginning to sell or offer for sale video display devices to households.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) A registration must be updated within ten days after a change in the manufacturer's brands of video display devices sold or offered for sale to households.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) A registration is effective upon receipt by the agency and is valid until August 15 each year.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) The agency must review each registration and notify the manufacturer of any information required by this section that is omitted from the registration. Within 30 days of receipt of a notification from the agency, the manufacturer must submit a revised registration providing the information noted by the agency.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) The agency must maintain on its website the names of manufacturers and the manufacturers' brands listed in registrations filed with the agency. The agency must update the website information promptly upon receipt of a new or updated registration. The website must contain prominent language stating, in effect, that sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1310">115A.1310 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1330">115A.1330</a> are directed at household equipment and the manufacturers' brands list is, therefore, not a list of manufacturers qualified to sell to industrial, commercial, or other markets identified as exempt from the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1310">115A.1310</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1330">115A.1330</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1312.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Collector registration.</span></h2>
          <p class="in"> No person may operate as a collector of covered electronic devices from households unless that person has submitted a registration with the agency by July 15 each year on a form prescribed by the commissioner. Registration information must include the name, address, telephone number, and location of the business and a certification that the collector has complied and will continue to comply with the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1312">115A.1312</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1318">115A.1318</a> and any regulations adopted by a local government unit for the jurisdiction in which the collector operates. A collector must indicate any end-of-life fees that will be charged at the collection point. A registration is effective upon receipt by the agency and is valid until July 15 each year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1312.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Recycler registration.</span></h2>
          <p class="in"> No person may recycle video display devices generated by households unless that person has submitted a registration with the agency by July 15 each year on a form prescribed by the commissioner. Registration information must include the name, address, telephone number, and location of all recycling facilities under the direct control of the recycler that may receive covered electronic devices from households and a certification that the recycler has complied and will continue to comply with the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1312">115A.1312</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1318">115A.1318</a>. A registered recycler must conduct recycling activities that are consistent with this chapter. A registration is effective upon receipt by the agency and is valid until July 15 each year.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.1312.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/48/#laws.0.2.0">2007 c 48 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/166/#laws.0.7.0">2016 c 166 s 7</a>-9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.86.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 86</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.1314">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.1314 MANUFACTURER REGISTRATION FEE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1314.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Registration fee.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Each manufacturer who registers under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1312">115A.1312</a> must, by August 15 each year, pay to the commissioner of revenue an annual registration fee, on a form and in a manner prescribed by the commissioner of revenue. The commissioner of revenue must deposit the fee in the state treasury and credit the fee to the environmental fund.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The registration fee for manufacturers that sell 100 or more video display devices to households in the state during the previous calendar year is $2,500, plus a variable recycling fee. The registration fee for manufacturers that sell fewer than 100 video display devices in the state during the previous calendar year is a variable recycling fee. The variable recycling fee is calculated according to the formula:</p>
          <p class="in">[A - (B + C)] x D, where:</p>
          <p class="in">A = the manufacturer's recycling obligation as determined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1320">115A.1320</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">B = the number of pounds of covered electronic devices that a manufacturer recycled or arranged to have collected and recycled from households during the immediately preceding program year, as reported under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1316#stat.115A.1316.1">115A.1316, subdivision 1</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">C = the number of phase I or phase II recycling credits a manufacturer elects to use to calculate the variable recycling fee; and</p>
          <p class="in">D = the estimated per-pound cost of recycling, initially set at $0.50 per pound for manufacturers who recycle less than 50 percent of the manufacturer's recycling obligation; $0.40 per pound for manufacturers who recycle at least 50 percent but less than 90 percent of the manufacturer's recycling obligation; $0.30 per pound for manufacturers who recycle at least 90 percent but less than 100 percent of the manufacturer's recycling obligation; and $0.00 per pound for manufacturers who recycle 100 percent or more of the manufacturer's recycling obligation.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A manufacturer may petition the agency to waive the per-pound cost of recycling fee, element D in the formula in paragraph (b), required under this section. The agency shall direct the commissioner of revenue to waive the per-pound cost of recycling fee if the manufacturer demonstrates to the agency's satisfaction a good faith effort to meet its recycling obligation as determined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1320">115A.1320</a>. The petition must include:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) documentation that the manufacturer has met at least 75 percent of its recycling obligation as determined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1320">115A.1320</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a list of political subdivisions and public and private collectors with whom the manufacturer had a formal contract or agreement in effect during the previous program year to recycle or collect covered electronic devices;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the total amounts of covered electronic devices collected from both within and outside of the 11-county metropolitan area, as defined in subdivision 2;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) a description of the manufacturer's best efforts to meet its recycling obligation as determined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1320">115A.1320</a>; and</p>
          <p class="in">(5) any other information requested by the agency.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) A manufacturer may retain phase I and phase II recycling credits to be added, in whole or in part, to the actual value of C, as reported under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1316#stat.115A.1316.2">115A.1316, subdivision 2</a>, during any succeeding program year, provided that no more than 25 percent of a manufacturer's recycling obligation A for any program year may be met with phase I and phase II recycling credits, separately or in combination, generated in a prior program year. A manufacturer may sell any portion or all of its phase I and phase II recycling credits to another manufacturer, at a price negotiated by the parties, who may use the credits in the same manner.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) For the purpose of determining B in calculating a manufacturer's variable recycling fee using the formula under paragraph (b), starting with the program year beginning July 1, 2019, and continuing each year thereafter, the weight of covered electronic devices that a manufacturer recycled or arranged to have collected and recycled from households located outside the 11-county metropolitan area, as defined in subdivision 2, paragraph (b), is calculated at 1.5 times their actual weight.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1314.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Use of registration fees.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Registration fees may be used by the commissioner for:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) implementing sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1312">115A.1312</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1330">115A.1330</a>, including transfer to the commissioner of revenue to carry out the department's duties under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1320#stat.115A.1320.2">115A.1320, subdivision 2</a>, and transfer to the commissioner of administration for responsibilities under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1324">115A.1324</a>; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) grants to counties outside the 11-county metropolitan area, as defined in paragraph (b), and to private entities that collect for recycling covered electronic devices in counties outside the 11-county metropolitan area, where the collection and recycling is consistent with the respective county's solid waste plan, for the purpose of carrying out the activities under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1312">115A.1312</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1330">115A.1330</a>. In awarding competitive grants under this clause, the commissioner must give preference to counties and private entities that are working cooperatively with manufacturers to help them meet their recycling obligations under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1318#stat.115A.1318.1">115A.1318, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The 11-county metropolitan area consists of the counties of Anoka, Carver, Chisago, Dakota, Hennepin, Isanti, Ramsey, Scott, Sherburne, Washington, and Wright.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.1314.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/48/#laws.0.3.0">2007 c 48 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/366/#laws.14.1.0">2008 c 366 art 14 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.41.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/42/#laws.0.1.0">2009 c 42 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.4.17.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 4 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.106.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 106</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/166/#laws.0.10.0">2016 c 166 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.87.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 87</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.1316">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.1316 REPORTING REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1316.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Manufacturer reporting requirements.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) By March 1 each year, each manufacturer must report to the agency using the form prescribed:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the total weight of each specific model of its video display devices sold to households during the previous calendar year; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) either:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) the total weight of its video display devices sold to households during the previous calendar year; or</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) an estimate of the total weight of its video display devices sold to households during the previous calendar year, calculated by multiplying the weight of its video display devices sold nationally times the quotient of Minnesota's population divided by the national population. All manufacturers with sales of 99 or fewer video display devices to households in the state during the previous calendar year must report using the method under this item for calculating sales.</p>
          <p class="bl">A manufacturer must submit with the report required under this paragraph a description of how the information or estimate was calculated.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) By August 15 each year, each manufacturer must report to the agency:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the total weight of covered electronic devices the manufacturer collected from households and recycled or arranged to have collected and recycled during the preceding program year;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the number of phase I and phase II recycling credits the manufacturer has purchased and sold during the preceding program year;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the number of phase I and phase II recycling credits possessed by the manufacturer that the manufacturer elects to use in the calculation of its variable recycling fee under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1314#stat.115A.1314.1">115A.1314, subdivision 1</a>; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) the number of phase I and phase II recycling credits the manufacturer retains at the beginning of the current program year.</p>
          <p>(c) Upon request of the commissioner of revenue, the agency shall provide a copy of each report to the commissioner of revenue.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1316.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Recycler reporting requirements.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) By July 15 each year, a recycler of covered electronic devices must report to the agency:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the total weight of covered electronic devices recycled during the preceding program year and must certify that the recycler has complied with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1318#stat.115A.1318.2">115A.1318, subdivision 2</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the weight of video display devices recycled as part of covered electronic devices recycled during the previous program year; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) an estimate of the weight of portable batteries and any mercury-containing lamps that are associated with the covered electronic devices managed.</p>
          <p>(b) Upon request of the commissioner of revenue, the agency shall provide a copy of each report to the commissioner of revenue.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1316.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Collector reporting requirements.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">By July 15 each year, a collector must report separately to the agency using the form prescribed by the commissioner:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the total pounds of covered electronic devices collected in the state;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a list of all recyclers to whom collectors delivered covered electronic devices; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) whether the collector had a contract with a recycler or manufacturer to provide pounds toward meeting a manufacturer's obligation.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.1316.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/48/#laws.0.4.0">2007 c 48 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/42/#laws.0.2.0">2009 c 42 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/166/#laws.0.11.0">2016 c 166 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.88.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 88</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.1318">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.1318 RESPONSIBILITIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1318.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Manufacturer responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) In addition to fulfilling the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1310">115A.1310</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1330">115A.1330</a>, a manufacturer must comply with paragraphs (b) to (f).</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A manufacturer must annually recycle or arrange for the collection and recycling of an amount of video display devices as determined by the agency in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1320#stat.115A.1320.1">115A.1320, subdivision 1</a>. A manufacturer must assume all financial responsibility associated with transporting and recycling covered electronic devices that are used to meet the manufacturer's recycling obligation determined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1320">115A.1320</a> or that are counted as phase I or II recycling credits, including any necessary supplies. This excludes costs that are associated with receiving and aggregating covered electronic devices from households and all the activities up to the time that covered electronic devices are loaded for transport to a recycler or arranged for transportation to a recycler.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The obligations of a manufacturer apply only to video display devices received from households and do not apply to video display devices received from sources other than households.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) A manufacturer must conduct and document due diligence assessments of collectors and recyclers it contracts with, including an assessment of items specified under subdivision 2. A manufacturer is responsible for maintaining, for a period of three years, documentation that all covered electronic devices recycled, partially recycled, or sent to downstream recycling operations comply with the requirements of subdivision 2.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) A manufacturer must provide the agency with contact information for a person who can be contacted regarding the manufacturer's activities under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1310">115A.1310 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1320">115A.1320</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) Only the covered electronic devices that are recycled by a registered recycler that is certified by an ANSI-ASQ National Accreditation Board-accredited third-party certification body to an environmentally sound management standard are eligible to meet the manufacturer's obligation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1318.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Collector responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Collection sites must be:</p>
          <p>(1) staffed; and</p>
          <p>(2) open to the public at a frequency adequate to meet the needs of the area being served.</p>
          <p>(b) A collector may limit the number of covered electronic devices or covered electronic devices by product type accepted per customer per day or per delivery at a collection site or service.</p>
          <p>(c) A collector must use only registered recyclers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1318.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Recycler responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) As part of the report submitted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1316#stat.115A.1316.2">115A.1316, subdivision 2</a>, a recycler must certify, except as provided in paragraph (b), that facilities that recycle covered electronic devices, including all downstream recycling operations:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) use only registered collectors;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) comply with all applicable health, environmental, safety, and financial responsibility regulations;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) are licensed by all applicable governmental authorities;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) use no prison labor to recycle video display devices;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) possess liability insurance of not less than $1,000,000 for environmental releases, accidents, and other emergencies;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) provide a report annually to each registered collector regarding the video display devices received from that entity; and</p>
          <p class="in">(7) do not charge collectors for transporting, recycling, or any necessary supplies related to transporting or recycling covered electronic devices that meet a manufacturer's recycling obligation as determined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1320">115A.1320</a>, unless otherwise mutually agreed upon.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A nonprofit corporation that contracts with a correctional institution to refurbish and reuse donated computers in schools is exempt from paragraph (a), clauses (4) and (5).</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Except to the extent otherwise required by law and unless agreed upon otherwise by the recycler or manufacturer, a recycler has no responsibility for any data that may be contained in a covered electronic device if an information storage device is included in the covered electronic device.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1318.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Retailer responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p class="in"> A retailer who sells new video display devices shall provide information to households describing where and how they may recycle video display devices and advising them of opportunities and locations for the convenient collection of video display devices for the purpose of recycling. This requirement may be met by providing to households the agency's toll-free number and website address. Retailers selling through catalogs or the Internet may meet this requirement by including the information in a prominent location on the retailer's website.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.1318.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/48/#laws.0.5.0">2007 c 48 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/42/#laws.0.3.0">2009 c 42 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/166/#laws.0.12.0">2016 c 166 s 12</a>-14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.89.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 89</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.1320">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.1320 AGENCY AND DEPARTMENT DUTIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1320.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Duties of agency.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The agency shall administer sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1310">115A.1310 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1330">115A.1330</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The agency shall establish procedures for:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) receipt and maintenance of the registration statements and certifications filed with the agency under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1312">115A.1312</a>; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) making the statements and certifications easily available to manufacturers, retailers, and members of the public.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The agency shall annually review the following variables that are used to calculate a manufacturer's annual registration fee under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1314#stat.115A.1314.1">115A.1314, subdivision 1</a>:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the obligation-setting mechanism for manufacturers as specified under paragraph (g);</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the estimated per-pound price of recycling covered electronic devices sold to households; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the base registration fee.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) If the agency determines that any of these values must be changed in order to improve the efficiency or effectiveness of the activities regulated under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1312">115A.1312 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1330">115A.1330</a>, or if the revenues exceed the amount that the agency determines is necessary, the agency shall submit recommended changes and the reasons for them to the chairs of the senate and house of representatives committees with jurisdiction over solid waste policy.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) By May 1 each year, the agency shall publish a statewide recycling goal for all video display device waste that is the weight of all video display devices collected for recycling during each of the three most recently completed program years, excluding the most recently concluded program year, divided by two.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) By May 1 each year, the agency shall determine each registered manufacturer's market share of video display devices to be collected and recycled based on the manufacturer's percentage share of the total weight of video display devices sold as reported to the agency under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1316#stat.115A.1316.1">115A.1316, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) By May 1 each year, the agency shall provide each manufacturer with a determination of the manufacturer's share of video display devices to be collected and recycled. A manufacturer's market share of video display devices as specified in paragraph (f) is applied proportionally to the statewide recycling goal as specified in paragraph (e) to determine an individual manufacturer's recycling obligation. Upon request by the commissioner of revenue, the agency must provide the information submitted to manufacturers under this paragraph to the commissioner of revenue.</p>
          <p class="in">(h) The agency shall provide a report to the governor and the legislature on the implementation of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1310">115A.1310</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1330">115A.1330</a>. For each program year, the report must discuss the total weight of covered electronic devices recycled and a summary of information in the reports submitted by manufacturers and recyclers under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1316">115A.1316</a>. The report must also discuss the various collection programs used by manufacturers to collect covered electronic devices; information regarding covered electronic devices that are being collected by persons other than registered manufacturers, collectors, and recyclers; and information about covered electronic devices, if any, being disposed of in landfills in this state. The report must examine which covered electronic devices, based on economic and environmental considerations, should be subject to the obligation-setting mechanism under paragraph (g). The report must include a description of enforcement actions under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1310">115A.1310</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1330">115A.1330</a>. The agency may include in its report other information received by the agency regarding the implementation of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1312">115A.1312</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1330">115A.1330</a>. The report must be done in conjunction with the report required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.121">115A.121</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(i) The agency shall promote public participation in the activities regulated under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1312">115A.1312</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1330">115A.1330</a> through public education and outreach efforts.</p>
          <p class="in">(j) The agency shall enforce sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1310">115A.1310</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1330">115A.1330</a> in the manner provided by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.071">115.071, subdivisions 1</a>, 3, 4, 5, and 6; and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072">116.072</a>, except for those provisions enforced by the department, as provided in subdivision 2. The agency may revoke a registration of a collector or recycler found to have violated sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1310">115A.1310 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1330">115A.1330</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(k) The agency shall facilitate communication between counties, collection and recycling centers, and manufacturers to ensure that manufacturers are aware of video display devices available for recycling.</p>
          <p class="in">(l) The agency shall post on its website the contact information provided by each manufacturer under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1318#stat.115A.1318.1">115A.1318, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (e).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1320.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Additional duties.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The agency must collect the data submitted to it annually by each manufacturer on the total weight of each specific model of video display device sold to households, if provided; the total weight of video display devices sold to households; the total weight of covered electronic devices collected from households that are recycled; and data on phase I and phase II recycling credits, as required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1316">115A.1316</a>. The department must use this data to review each manufacturer's annual registration fee submitted to the department to ensure that the fee was calculated accurately.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The agency must estimate, for each registered manufacturer, the sales of video display devices to households during the previous program year, based on:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) data provided by a manufacturer on sales of video display devices to households, including documentation describing how that amount was calculated and certification that the amount is accurate; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) if a manufacturer does not provide the data specified in clause (1), national data on sales of video display devices.</p>
          <p class="bl">The department must use the data specified in this subdivision to review each manufacturer's annual registration fee submitted to the department to ensure that the fee was calculated accurately according to the formula in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1314#stat.115A.1314.1">115A.1314, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The department must enforce section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1314#stat.115A.1314.1">115A.1314, subdivision 1</a>. The audit, assessment, appeal, collection, enforcement, disclosure, and other administrative provisions of chapters 270B, 270C, and 289A that apply to the taxes imposed under chapter 297A apply to the fee imposed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1314#stat.115A.1314.1">115A.1314, subdivision 1</a>. To enforce section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1314#stat.115A.1314.1">115A.1314, subdivision 1</a>, the commissioner of revenue may grant extensions to pay, and impose and abate penalties and interest on, the fee due under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1314">115A.1314</a>, subdivision 1, in the manner provided in chapters 270C and 289A as if the fee were a tax imposed under chapter 297A.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The department may disclose nonpublic data to the agency only when necessary for the efficient and effective administration of the activities regulated under sections 115A.1310 to 115A.1330. Any data disclosed by the department to the agency retains the classification it had when in the possession of the department.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.1320.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/48/#laws.0.6.0">2007 c 48 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.4.18.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 4 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.64.0">2012 c 272 s 64</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.4.77.0">2013 c 114 art 4 s 77</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/166/#laws.0.15.0">2016 c 166 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.90.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 90</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.1322">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.1322 OTHER RECYCLING PROGRAMS.</h1>
        <p class="in">A city, county, or other public agency may not require households to use public facilities to recycle their covered electronic devices to the exclusion of other lawful programs available. Cities, counties, and other public agencies, including those awarded contracts by the agency under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1314#stat.115A.1314.2">115A.1314, subdivision 2</a>, are encouraged to work with manufacturers to assist them in meeting their recycling obligations under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1318#stat.115A.1318.1">115A.1318, subdivision 1</a>. Nothing in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1310">115A.1310</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1330">115A.1330</a> prohibits or restricts the operation of any program recycling covered electronic devices in addition to those provided by manufacturers or prohibits or restricts any persons from receiving, collecting, transporting, or recycling covered electronic devices, provided that those persons are registered under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1312">115A.1312</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.1322.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/48/#laws.0.7.0">2007 c 48 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.1323">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.1323 ANTICOMPETITIVE CONDUCT.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) A manufacturer that organizes collection or recycling under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1310">115A.1310</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1322">115A.1322</a> is authorized to engage in anticompetitive conduct to the extent necessary to plan and implement its chosen organized collection or recycling system and is immune from liability under state laws relating to antitrust, restraint of trade, unfair trade practices, and other regulation of trade or commerce.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) An organization of manufacturers, an individual manufacturer, and its officers, members, employees, and agents who cooperate with a political subdivision that organizes collection or recycling under this section are authorized to engage in anticompetitive conduct to the extent necessary to plan and implement the organized collection or recycling system, provided that the political subdivision actively supervises the participation of each entity. An organization, entity, or person covered by this paragraph is immune from liability under state law relating to antitrust, restraint of trade, unfair trade practices, and other regulation of trade or commerce.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.1323.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/48/#laws.0.8.0">2007 c 48 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/166/#laws.0.16.0">2016 c 166 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.1324">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.1324 REQUIREMENTS FOR PURCHASES BY STATE AGENCIES.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) The Department of Administration must ensure that acquisitions of video display devices under chapter 16C are in compliance with or not subject to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1310">115A.1310</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1318">115A.1318</a>.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) The solicitation documents must specify that the prospective responder is required to cooperate fully in providing reasonable access to its records and documents that evidence compliance with paragraph (a) and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1310">115A.1310</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1318">115A.1318</a>.</p>
        <p class="in">(c) Any person awarded a contract under chapter 16C for purchase or lease of video display devices that is found to be in violation of paragraph (a) or sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1310">115A.1310</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1318">115A.1318</a> is subject to the following sanctions:</p>
        <p class="in">(1) the contract must be voided if the commissioner of administration determines that the potential adverse impact to the state is exceeded by the benefit obtained from voiding the contract;</p>
        <p class="in">(2) the contractor is subject to suspension and disbarment under Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/1230.1150">1230.1150</a>; and</p>
        <p class="in">(3) if the attorney general establishes that any money, property, or benefit was obtained by a contractor as a result of violating paragraph (a) or sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1310">115A.1310</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1318">115A.1318</a>, the court may, in addition to any other remedy, order the disgorgement of the unlawfully obtained money, property, or benefit.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.1324.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/48/#laws.0.9.0">2007 c 48 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.1326">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.1326 REGULATING VIDEO DISPLAY DEVICES.</h1>
        <p class="in">If the United States Environmental Protection Agency adopts regulations under the Resource Conservation and Recovery Act regarding the handling, storage, or treatment of any type of video display device being recycled, those regulations are automatically effective in this state on the same date and supersede any rules previously adopted by the agency regarding the handling, storage, or treatment of all video display devices being recycled.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.1326.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/48/#laws.0.10.0">2007 c 48 s 10</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.1328">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.1328 MULTISTATE IMPLEMENTATION.</h1>
        <p class="in">The agency and department are authorized to participate in the establishment of a regional multistate organization or compact to assist in carrying out the requirements of this chapter.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.1328.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/48/#laws.0.11.0">2007 c 48 s 11</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.1330">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.1330 LIMITATIONS.</h1>
        <p class="in">Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1310">115A.1310</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.1330">115A.1330</a> expire if a federal law, or combination of federal laws, take effect that is applicable to all video display devices sold in the United States and establish a program for the collection and recycling or reuse of video display devices that is applicable to all video display devices discarded by households.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.1330.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/48/#laws.0.12.0">2007 c 48 s 12</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.14"><b>115A.14</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/310/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 310 s 1</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">PAINT STEWARDSHIP PROGRAM</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.1415">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.1415 ARCHITECTURAL PAINT; PRODUCT STEWARDSHIP PROGRAM; STEWARDSHIP PLAN.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1415.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given:</p>
          <p>(1) "annual operating expenses" means the total amount of a producer's or stewardship organization's expenses in a calendar year for developing a stewardship plan, operating and administering the program in accordance with the stewardship plan, and meeting the requirements of this section, determined at the time the annual report required under subdivision 12 is submitted;</p>
          <p>(2) "architectural paint" means interior and exterior architectural coatings sold in containers of five gallons or less. Architectural paint does not include industrial coatings, original equipment coatings, or specialty coatings;</p>
          <p>(3) "brand" means a name, symbol, word, or mark that identifies architectural paint, rather than its components, and attributes the paint to the owner or licensee of the brand as the producer;</p>
          <p>(4) "discarded paint" means architectural paint that is no longer used for its manufactured purpose;</p>
          <p>(5) "producer" means a person that:</p>
          <p>(i) has legal ownership of the brand, brand name, or cobrand of architectural paint sold in the state;</p>
          <p>(ii) imports architectural paint branded by a producer that meets item (i) when the producer has no physical presence in the United States;</p>
          <p>(iii) if items (i) and (ii) do not apply, makes unbranded architectural paint that is sold in the state; or</p>
          <p>(iv) sells architectural paint at wholesale or retail, does not have legal ownership of the brand, and elects to fulfill the responsibilities of the producer for the architectural paint by certifying that election in writing to the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(6) "recycling" means the process of collecting and preparing recyclable materials and reusing the materials in their original form or using them in manufacturing processes that do not cause the destruction of recyclable materials in a manner that precludes further use;</p>
          <p>(7) "retailer" means any person who offers architectural paint for sale at retail in the state;</p>
          <p>(8) "reuse" means donating or selling collected architectural paint back into the market for its original intended use, when the architectural paint retains its original purpose and performance characteristics;</p>
          <p>(9) "sale" or "sell" means transfer of title of architectural paint for consideration, including a remote sale conducted through a sales outlet, catalog, website, or similar electronic means. Sale or sell includes a lease through which architectural paint is provided to a consumer by a producer, wholesaler, or retailer;</p>
          <p>(10) "stewardship assessment" means the amount added to the purchase price of architectural paint sold in the state to implement a product stewardship program according to an approved stewardship plan;</p>
          <p>(11) "stewardship organization" means an organization appointed by one or more producers to act as an agent on behalf of the producer to design, submit, and administer a product stewardship program under this section; and</p>
          <p>(12) "stewardship plan" means a detailed plan describing the manner in which a product stewardship program under subdivision 2 will be implemented.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1415.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Product stewardship program.</span></h2>
          <p>For architectural paint sold in the state, producers must, individually or through a stewardship organization, implement and finance a statewide product stewardship program that manages the architectural paint by reducing the paint's waste generation, promoting its reuse and recycling, and providing for negotiation and execution of agreements to collect, transport, and process the architectural paint for end-of-life recycling and reuse.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1415.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Participation required to sell.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) No producer, wholesaler, or retailer may sell or offer for sale in the state architectural paint unless the paint's producer participates in an approved stewardship plan, either individually or through a stewardship organization.</p>
          <p>(b) Each producer must operate a product stewardship program approved by the commissioner or enter into an agreement with a stewardship organization to operate, on the producer's behalf, a product stewardship program approved by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1415.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Stewardship plan required.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Before offering architectural paint for sale in the state, a producer must submit a stewardship plan to the commissioner and receive approval of the plan or must submit documentation to the commissioner that demonstrates the producer has entered into an agreement with a stewardship organization to be an active participant in an approved product stewardship program as described in subdivision 2. A stewardship plan must include all elements required under subdivision 5.</p>
          <p>(b) A proposed amendment to the plan, if determined necessary by the commissioner, must be submitted to the commissioner for review and approval or rejection every five years.</p>
          <p>(c) The entities responsible for each stewardship plan must notify the commissioner within 30 days of any significant proposed changes to the plan or its implementation. Within 30 days of the notification, a written proposed plan amendment must be submitted to the commissioner for review and approval or rejection.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1415.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Plan content.</span></h2>
          <p>A stewardship plan must contain:</p>
          <p>(1) certification that the product stewardship program will accept all discarded paint regardless of which producer produced the architectural paint and its individual components;</p>
          <p>(2) contact information for the individual and the entity submitting the stewardship plan, a list of all producers participating in the product stewardship program, and the brands covered by the product stewardship program;</p>
          <p>(3) a description of the methods by which the discarded paint will be collected in all areas in the state without relying on end-of-life fees, including an explanation of how the collection system will be convenient and adequate to serve the needs of small businesses and residents in both urban and rural areas on an ongoing basis and a discussion of how the existing household hazardous waste infrastructure will be considered when selecting collection sites;</p>
          <p>(4) a description of how the adequacy of the collection program will be monitored and maintained;</p>
          <p>(5) the names and locations of collectors, transporters, and recyclers that will manage discarded paint;</p>
          <p>(6) a description of how the discarded paint and the paint's components will be safely and securely transported, tracked, and handled from collection through final recycling and processing;</p>
          <p>(7) a description of the method that will be used to reuse, deconstruct, or recycle the discarded paint to ensure that the paint's components, to the extent feasible, are transformed or remanufactured into finished products for use;</p>
          <p>(8) a description of the promotion and outreach activities that will be used to encourage participation in the collection and recycling programs and how the activities' effectiveness will be evaluated and the program modified, if necessary;</p>
          <p>(9) the proposed stewardship assessment established according to subdivision 5a;</p>
          <p>(10) evidence of adequate insurance and financial assurance that may be required for collection, handling, and disposal operations;</p>
          <p>(11) five-year performance goals, including an estimate of the percentage of discarded paint that will be collected, reused, and recycled during each of the first five years of the stewardship plan. The performance goals must include a specific goal for the amount of discarded paint that will be collected and recycled and reused during each year of the plan. The performance goals must be based on:</p>
          <p>(i) the most recent collection data available for the state;</p>
          <p>(ii) the estimated amount of architectural paint disposed of annually;</p>
          <p>(iii) the weight of the architectural paint that is expected to be available for collection annually; and</p>
          <p>(iv) actual collection data from other existing stewardship programs.</p>
          <p class="bl">The stewardship plan must state the methodology used to determine these goals; and</p>
          <p>(12) a discussion of the status of end markets for collected architectural paint and what, if any, additional end markets are needed to improve the functioning of the program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1415.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Stewardship assessment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The producer or stewardship organization must propose a uniform stewardship assessment for any architectural paint sold in the state that covers but does not exceed the costs of developing the stewardship plan, operating and administering the program in accordance with the stewardship plan and the requirements of this section, and maintaining a financial reserve.</p>
          <p>(b) The producer or stewardship organization must retain an independent auditor to review the proposed stewardship assessment to ensure that the assessment meets the requirements of this section. The independent auditor must recommend an amount for the stewardship assessment.</p>
          <p>(c) A stewardship organization's or producer's product stewardship program must not maintain a financial reserve in excess of 75 percent of its annual operating expenses.</p>
          <p>(d) If the financial reserve exceeds 75 percent of the producer's or stewardship organization's annual operating expenses, the producer or stewardship organization must submit a proposed plan amendment according to subdivision 4, paragraph (c), to comply with this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(e) A producer or stewardship organization may submit a written request to the commissioner for an extension of the time to comply with paragraphs (c) and (d). The commissioner must review and approve or reject the request. If the commissioner approves a request, the commissioner must determine the length of the extension, which must not exceed two consecutive years. The request must demonstrate that the financial reserve is projected to fall below 75 percent of the producer's or stewardship organization's annual operating expenses without a plan amendment within two years of the request.</p>
          <p>(f) If the financial reserve falls below 60 percent of the producer's or stewardship organization's annual operating expenses, the producer or stewardship organization may submit a proposed plan amendment according to subdivision 4, paragraph (c), to comply with this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(g) The commissioner must review and approve or reject the stewardship assessment according to subdivision 7.</p>
          <p>(h) A producer or stewardship organization may not use any money collected through a stewardship assessment to pay for litigation against the state related to this section or to pay penalties imposed according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.071">115.071</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072">116.072</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1415.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Consultation required.</span></h2>
          <p>Each stewardship organization or individual producer submitting a stewardship plan or plan amendment must consult with stakeholders including retailers, contractors, collectors, recyclers, local government, and customers during the development of the plan or plan amendment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1415.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Commissioner review and approval.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Within 90 days after receiving a proposed stewardship plan, the commissioner must determine whether the plan complies with this section. If the commissioner approves a plan, the commissioner must notify the applicant of the plan approval in writing. If the commissioner rejects a plan, the commissioner must notify the applicant in writing of the reasons for rejecting the plan.</p>
          <p>(b) An applicant whose plan is rejected by the commissioner must submit a revised stewardship plan to the commissioner within 60 days after receiving notice of rejection.</p>
          <p>(c) Any proposed amendment to a stewardship plan must be reviewed and approved or rejected by the commissioner in writing according to this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1415.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Plan availability.</span></h2>
          <p>All proposed stewardship plans and amendments and approved stewardship plans and amendments must be placed on the agency's website for at least 30 days and made available at the agency's headquarters for public review and comment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1415.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Conduct authorized.</span></h2>
          <p>A producer or stewardship organization that organizes collection, transport, and processing of architectural paint under this section is immune from liability for the conduct under state laws relating to antitrust, restraint of trade, unfair trade practices, and other regulation of trade or commerce only to the extent that the conduct is necessary to plan and implement the producer's or organization's chosen organized collection or recycling system.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1415.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Producer responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) On and after the date of implementation of a product stewardship program according to this section, a producer of architectural paint must add the stewardship assessment, as established under subdivision 5a, to the cost of architectural paint sold to retailers and distributors in the state by the producer.</p>
          <p>(b) Producers of architectural paint or the stewardship organization must provide consumers with educational materials regarding the stewardship assessment and product stewardship program. The materials must include, but are not limited to, information regarding available end-of-life management options for architectural paint offered through the product stewardship program and information that notifies consumers that a charge for the operation of the product stewardship program is included in the purchase price of architectural paint sold in the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1415.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Retailer responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) No architectural paint may be sold in the state unless the paint's producer is participating in an approved stewardship plan.</p>
          <p>(b) On and after the implementation date of a product stewardship program according to this section, each retailer or distributor, as applicable, must ensure that the full amount of the stewardship assessment added to the cost of architectural paint by producers under subdivision 10 is included in the purchase price of all architectural paint sold in the state.</p>
          <p>(c) Any retailer may participate, on a voluntary basis, as a designated collection point pursuant to a product stewardship program under this section and in accordance with applicable law.</p>
          <p>(d) No retailer or distributor shall be found to be in violation of this subdivision if, on the date the architectural paint was ordered from the producer or its agent, the producer was listed as compliant on the agency's website according to subdivision 14.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1415.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Stewardship reports.</span></h2>
          <p>By April 1 each year, producers of architectural paint sold in the state must individually or through a stewardship organization submit an annual report to the commissioner describing the product stewardship program for the preceding calendar year. At a minimum, the report must contain:</p>
          <p>(1) a description of the methods used to collect, transport, and process architectural paint in all regions of the state;</p>
          <p>(2) the weight of all architectural paint collected in all regions of the state and a comparison to the performance goals and recycling rates established in the stewardship plan;</p>
          <p>(3) the amount of unwanted architectural paint collected in the state by method of disposition, including reuse, recycling, and other methods of processing;</p>
          <p>(4) samples of educational materials provided to consumers and an evaluation of the effectiveness of the materials and the methods used to disseminate the materials; and</p>
          <p>(5) an independent financial audit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1415.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Data classification.</span></h2>
          <p>Trade secret and sales information, as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.37">13.37</a>, submitted to the commissioner under this section are private or nonpublic data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.37">13.37</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1415.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Commissioner responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must provide, on the agency's website, a list of all compliant producers and brands participating in stewardship plans that the commissioner has approved and a list of all producers and brands the commissioner has identified as noncompliant with this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1415.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Local government responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A city, county, or other public agency may choose to participate voluntarily in a product stewardship program.</p>
          <p>(b) Cities, counties, and other public agencies are encouraged to work with producers and stewardship organizations to assist in meeting product stewardship program reuse and recycling obligations, by providing education and outreach or using other strategies.</p>
          <p>(c) A city, county, or other public agency that participates in a product stewardship program must report for the first year of the program to the commissioner using the reporting form provided by the commissioner on the cost savings as a result of participation and must describe how the savings were used.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1415.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.<span class="headnote">Administrative fee.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The stewardship organization or individual producer submitting a stewardship plan must pay an annual administrative fee to the commissioner. The commissioner may establish a variable fee based on relevant factors, including but not limited to the portion of architectural paint sold in the state by members of the organization compared to the total amount of architectural paint sold in the state by all organizations submitting a stewardship plan.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Before July 1 each year, the commissioner must identify the costs the agency incurs under this section. The commissioner must set the fee at an amount that, when paid by every stewardship organization or individual producer that submits a stewardship plan, is adequate to reimburse the agency's full costs of administering this section. The total amount of annual fees collected under this subdivision must not exceed the amount necessary to reimburse costs incurred by the agency to administer this section.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A stewardship organization or individual producer subject to this subdivision must pay the commissioner's administrative fee under paragraph (a) on or before July 1 each year. Each year after the initial payment, the annual administrative fee may not exceed five percent of the aggregate stewardship assessment added to the cost of all architectural paint sold by producers in the state for the preceding calendar year.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) All fees received under this section must be deposited in the state treasury and credited to a product stewardship account in the special revenue fund. The amount collected under this section is annually appropriated to the commissioner to implement and enforce this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.1415.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.<span class="headnote">Duty to provide information.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon request of the commissioner for purposes of determining compliance with this section, a person must furnish to the commissioner any information that the person has or may reasonably obtain.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
