      <h1 class="fgh">SUPPLEMENTARY REVIEW OF CERTAIN WASTE FACILITIES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.32">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.32 RULES.</h1>
        <p>The board shall promulgate rules pursuant to chapter 14 to govern its activities under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.32">115A.32</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.39">115A.39</a>. For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.32">115A.32</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.39">115A.39</a>, "board" means the Environmental Quality Board established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116C.03">116C.03</a>. In all of its activities and deliberations under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.32">115A.32</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.39">115A.39</a>, the board shall consult with the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.32.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.4.1.0">1980 c 564 art 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/593/#laws.1.11.0">1992 c 593 art 1 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.2.9.0">1995 c 247 art 2 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.33"><b>115A.33</b> MS 1992 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/628/#laws.3.209.0">1994 c 628 art 3 s 209</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.33">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.33 ELIGIBILITY; REQUEST FOR REVIEW.</h1>
        <p>(a) The following persons are eligible to request supplementary review by the board pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.32">115A.32</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.39">115A.39</a>:</p>
        <p>(1) a generator of sewage sludge within the state who has been issued permits by the agency for a facility to dispose of sewage sludge or solid waste resulting from sewage treatment;</p>
        <p>(2) a political subdivision that has been issued permits by the agency, or a political subdivision acting on behalf of a person who has been issued permits by the agency, for a solid waste facility that is no larger than 250 acres, not including any proposed buffer area, and located outside the metropolitan area;</p>
        <p>(3) a generator of hazardous waste within the state who has been issued permits by the agency for a hazardous waste facility to be owned and operated by the generator, on property owned by the generator, and to be used by the generator for managing the hazardous wastes produced by the generator only;</p>
        <p>(4) a person who has been issued permits by the agency for a commercial hazardous waste processing facility at a site included in the board's inventory of preferred sites for such facilities adopted pursuant to Minnesota Statutes 1996, section 115A.09; and</p>
        <p>(5) a person who has been issued permits by the agency for a disposal facility for the nonhazardous sludge, ash, or other solid waste generated by a permitted hazardous waste processing facility operated by the person.</p>
        <p>(b) The board may require completion of a plan conforming to the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.46">115A.46</a>, before granting review under paragraph (a), clause (2). A request for supplementary review must show that the required permits for the facility have been issued by the agency and that a political subdivision has refused to approve the establishment or operation of the facility.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.33.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.4.2.0">1980 c 564 art 4 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/352/#laws.0.23.0">1981 c 352 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/593/#laws.1.54.0">1992 c 593 art 1 s 54</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/628/#laws.3.209.0">1994 c 628 art 3 s 209</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.1.6.0">1995 c 247 art 1 s 6</a>,60,64; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/86/#laws.1.21.0">1999 c 86 art 1 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/25/#laws.0.31.0">2023 c 25 s 31</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.34">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.34 APPOINTING TEMPORARY BOARD MEMBERS.</h1>
        <p>Within 45 days of the submission of a request determined by the board to satisfy the requirements for review under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.32">115A.32</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.39">115A.39</a>, temporary members shall be added to the board for the purpose of the supplementary review. Three members shall be selected by the governing body of the city or town in which the chair of the board determines the facility would be principally located, and three members shall be selected by the governing body of the county in which the chair of the board determines the proposed facility would be principally located. If the proposed facility is located in unorganized territory, all six members shall be selected by the governing board of the county. Temporary members shall be residents of the county in which the proposed facility would be located and shall be selected to represent broadly the local interests that would be directly affected by the proposed facility. At least one member appointed by the city or town shall live within one mile of the proposed facility, and at least one member appointed by the county shall be a resident of a city or town in which the proposed facility would be located. If the appointing authority fails to appoint temporary members in the period allowed, the governor shall appoint the temporary members to represent the local interests in accordance with this section. Temporary members shall serve for terms lasting until the board has taken final action on the facility.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.34.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.4.3.0">1980 c 564 art 4 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/352/#laws.0.24.0">1981 c 352 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/593/#laws.1.54.0">1992 c 593 art 1 s 54</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.35">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.35 REVIEW PROCEDURE.</h1>
        <p>(a) The board shall meet to commence the supplementary review within 90 days of the submission of a request determined by the board to satisfy the requirements for review under this section. At the meeting commencing the review, the chair shall recommend and the board establish a scope and procedure, in accordance with the rules of the board, for review and final decision on the proposed facility.</p>
        <p>(b) The procedure shall require the board to make a final decision on the proposed facility within 90 days following the commencement of review. The procedure shall require the board to hold, at the call of the chair, at least one public hearing in the county within which the proposed facility would be located.</p>
        <p>(c) A majority of permanent members of the board shall be present at the hearing. The hearing shall be conducted for the board by the state Office of Administrative Hearings in a manner determined by the administrative law judge to be consistent with the expeditious completion of the proceedings as required by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.32">115A.32</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.39">115A.39</a>. The hearing shall not be deemed a contested case under chapter 14.</p>
        <p>(d) Notice of the hearing shall be published in a newspaper or newspapers of general circulation in the area for two successive weeks ending at least 15 days before the date of the meeting. The notice shall describe the proposed facility, its location, the permits, and the board's scope and procedure for review. The notice shall identify a location or locations within the city or town and county where the permit applications, the agency permits, and the board's scope and procedure for review are available for review and where copies may be obtained.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.35.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.4.4.0">1980 c 564 art 4 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/615/#laws.0.60.0">1980 c 615 s 60</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/640/#laws.0.32.0">1984 c 640 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/593/#laws.1.54.0">1992 c 593 art 1 s 54</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.36">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.36 SCOPE AND CONTENT OF REVIEW.</h1>
        <p>In its review and final decision on the proposed facility, the board shall consider at least the following matters:</p>
        <p>(1) the risk and effect of the proposed facility on local residents, units of government, and the local public health, safety, and welfare, including such dangers as an accidental release of wastes during transportation to the facility, water, air, and land pollution, and fire or explosion where appropriate, and the degree to which the risk or effect may be alleviated;</p>
        <p>(2) the consistency of the proposed facility with, and its effect on, existing and planned local land use and development; local laws, ordinances, and permits; and local public facilities and services;</p>
        <p>(3) the adverse effects of the facility on agriculture and natural resources and opportunities to mitigate or eliminate the adverse effects by additional stipulations, conditions, and requirements respecting the proposed facility at the proposed site;</p>
        <p>(4) the need for the proposed facility, especially its contribution to abating solid and hazardous waste disposal, the availability of alternative sites, and opportunities to mitigate or eliminate need by additional and alternative waste management strategies or actions of a significantly different nature;</p>
        <p>(5) whether, in the case of solid waste resource recovery facilities, the applicant has considered the feasible and prudent waste processing alternatives for accomplishing the purposes of the proposed project and has compared and evaluated the costs of the alternatives, including capital and operating costs, and the effects of the alternatives on the cost to generators.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.36.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.4.5.0">1980 c 564 art 4 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/593/#laws.1.54.0">1992 c 593 art 1 s 54</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.37">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.37 FINAL DECISION OF BOARD.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.37.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Approval or disapproval.</span></h2>
          <p>In its final decision on the proposed facility, the board may either approve or disapprove the proposed facility at the proposed site. The board's approval shall embody all terms, conditions, and requirements of the permitting agencies, provided that the board may: (a) finally resolve any conflicts between state agencies regarding permit terms, conditions, and requirements, and (b) require more stringent permit terms, conditions, and requirements respecting the design, construction, operation, inspection, monitoring, and maintenance of the proposed facility at the proposed site. The board's resolution of conflicts under clause (a) shall be in favor of the more stringent terms, conditions, and requirements.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.37.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Decision paramount.</span></h2>
          <p>The decision of the board to approve a facility shall be final and shall supersede and preempt requirements of state agencies and political subdivisions and the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473H.02">473H.02</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473H.17">473H.17</a>; except that the facility shall be subject to those terms, conditions, and requirements of permitting agencies embodied in the board's approval and any requirements imposed pursuant to subdivision 3. The permitting agencies shall issue or amend the permits for the facility within 60 days following and in accordance with the final decision of the board, and all permits shall conform to the terms, conditions, and requirements of the board's decision. No charter provision, ordinance, rule, permit, or other requirement of any state agency or political subdivision shall prevent or restrict the establishment, operation, expansion, continuance, or closure of the facility in accordance with the final decision of the board and permits issued pursuant thereto.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.37.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Local requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>A political subdivision may impose reasonable requirements respecting the construction, inspection, operation, monitoring, and maintenance of a facility. Any such requirements shall be subject to review by the agency to determine their reasonableness and consistency with the establishment and use of a facility in accordance with the final decision of the board and permits issued pursuant thereto. The agency may approve, disapprove, suspend, modify, or reverse any such requirements. The decision of the agency shall be final.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.37.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.4.6.0">1980 c 564 art 4 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/352/#laws.0.25.0">1981 c 352 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/593/#laws.1.54.0">1992 c 593 art 1 s 54</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.38">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.38 RECONCILIATION PROCEDURES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.38.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Reports to legislative commission.</span></h2>
          <p>At least 30 days before making a final decision under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.37">115A.37</a> in a review brought pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.33">115A.33</a>, paragraph (a), clause (4), the chair of the board may report to the legislative commission describing permit conditions or requirements being considered that are not within the existing authority of the agency or the board or that would require legislation or public financial assistance. In any such report, the chair of the board may request intervention in the review pursuant to subdivisions 2 and 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.38.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Preintervention assessment.</span></h2>
          <p>If the legislative commission determines that intervention might be warranted under the terms of subdivision 1, the commission may suspend the review process for up to 60 days to allow a preintervention assessment. The preintervention assessment shall be conducted by an independent, impartial, and qualified public intervenor appointed by the commission with the advice and consent of the parties to the dispute. The intervenor shall report to the commission. The report shall include:</p>
          <p>(1) an assessment of whether the dispute is ripe for mediation and whether the parties are willing to mediate;</p>
          <p>(2) an assessment of whether, within the terms of subdivision 1, substantive issues exist which cannot be resolved effectively through normal administrative and judicial procedures;</p>
          <p>(3) a preliminary definition of the facts and issues in dispute and actions and decisions being considered;</p>
          <p>(4) a description of the diverse parties having a legitimate and direct interest in the outcome of the dispute.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.38.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Suspending review process; intervention proceeding.</span></h2>
          <p>Following the report of the intervenor, the legislative commission may suspend the review process for an additional period not to exceed 90 days for an intervention proceeding. The intervenor shall be in charge of the intervention proceeding and may call for such participation and establish such procedures as the intervenor deems necessary and appropriate to facilitate agreement. The intervenor shall keep the chair of the legislative commission informed on the progress of the intervention proceeding, particularly with respect to agreements or proposed agreements which may require action or decisions not within the authority of the agency or board, legislative action, or public financial assistance. The intervenor shall make recommendations to the commission respecting any such agreements or proposed agreements. The commission may make recommendations to the intervenor respecting any such agreement or proposed agreement. If the commission approves of an agreement, or a decision based upon an agreement, which requires action or decisions not within the authority of the agency or board, legislative action, or public financial assistance, the commission shall cause the matter and recommendations to be submitted to the legislature for consideration.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.38.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.4.7.0">1980 c 564 art 4 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/593/#laws.1.54.0">1992 c 593 art 1 s 54</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/25/#laws.0.32.0">2023 c 25 s 32</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.39">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.39 JUDICIAL REVIEW.</h1>
        <p>Judicial review with respect to conduct or decisions in supplementary reviews brought pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.33">115A.33</a>, paragraph (a), clause (3) or (4), is as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.30">115A.30</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.39.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.4.8.0">1980 c 564 art 4 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/25/#laws.0.33.0">2023 c 25 s 33</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">SOLID WASTE MANAGEMENT POLICY AND PROGRAMS</h1>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.41"><b>115A.41</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/685/#laws.0.44.0">1988 c 685 s 44</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.411">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.411 SOLID WASTE MANAGEMENT POLICY; CONSOLIDATED REPORT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.411.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority; purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall prepare and adopt a report on solid waste management policy and activities under this chapter. The report must be submitted by the commissioner to the senate and house of representatives committees having jurisdiction over environment and natural resources by December 31, 2015, and every four years thereafter and shall include reports required under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.551#stat.115A.551.4">115A.551, subdivision 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.557#stat.115A.557.4">115A.557, subdivision 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.149#stat.473.149.6">473.149, subdivision 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.846">473.846</a>; and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.848#stat.473.848.4">473.848, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.411.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Contents.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The report may also include:</p>
          <p>(1) a summary of the current status of solid waste management, including the amount of solid waste generated and reduced, the manner in which it is collected, processed, and disposed, the extent of separation, recycling, reuse, and recovery of solid waste, and the facilities available or under development to manage the waste;</p>
          <p>(2) an evaluation of the extent and effectiveness of implementation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.02">115A.02</a>, including an assessment of progress in accomplishing state policies, goals, and objectives, including those listed in paragraph (b);</p>
          <p>(3) identification of issues requiring further research, study, and action, the appropriate scope of the research, study, or action, the state agency or political subdivision that should implement the research, study, or action, and a schedule for completion of the activity;</p>
          <p>(4) recommendations for establishing or modifying state solid waste management policies, authorities, responsibilities, and programs; and</p>
          <p>(5) a report on progress made toward implementation of the objectives of the metropolitan area solid waste policy plan as required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.149">473.149, subdivision</a> 6.</p>
          <p>(b) The report must include strategies for:</p>
          <p>(1) achieving the maximum feasible reduction in waste generation;</p>
          <p>(2) encouraging manufacturers to design products that eliminate or reduce the adverse environmental impacts of resource extraction, manufacturing, use, and waste processing and disposal;</p>
          <p>(3) educating businesses, public entities, and other consumers about the need to consider the potential environmental and financial impacts of purchasing products that may create a liability or that may be expensive to recycle or manage as waste, due to the presence of toxic or hazardous components;</p>
          <p>(4) eliminating or reducing toxic or hazardous components in compost from municipal solid waste composting facilities, in ash from municipal solid waste incinerators, and in leachate and air emissions from municipal solid waste landfills, in order to reduce the potential liability of waste generators, facility owners and operators, and taxpayers;</p>
          <p>(5) encouraging the source separation of materials to the extent practicable, so that the materials are most appropriately managed and to ensure that resources that can be reused or recycled are not disposed of or destroyed; and</p>
          <p>(6) maximizing the efficiency of the waste management system by managing waste and recyclables close to the point of generation, taking into account the characteristics of the resources to be recovered from the waste and the type and capacity of local facilities.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.411.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/348/#laws.0.14.0">1987 c 348 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.14.0">1991 c 337 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/593/#laws.1.12.0">1992 c 593 art 1 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.1.7.0">1995 c 247 art 1 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.27.0">1996 c 470 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/73/#laws.0.2.0">1999 c 73 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.66.0">2012 c 272 s 66</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.412">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.412 WASTE COMPOSITION; INFORMATION REQUIRED.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.412.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Study required.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Every three years, beginning in 2029, the commissioner must direct the owners and operators at 20 percent of each of the following facility types to perform a waste composition study:</p>
          <p>(1) mixed municipal solid waste land disposal facilities;</p>
          <p>(2) industrial solid waste land disposal facilities;</p>
          <p>(3) demolition debris land disposal facilities;</p>
          <p>(4) transfer stations that annually transfer more than 5,000 tons of waste to a facility outside Minnesota; and</p>
          <p>(5) other facilities identified by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) The waste composition study must be performed at the sole expense of each owner or operator as directed by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(c) When selecting facilities for waste composition studies, the commissioner must rotate the participants so that, over time, the studies cover the entirety of the facilities identified under paragraph (a). The commissioner must determine the time frame for each study in the three-year cycle. The owner or operator of each selected facility must complete the study within one year of being notified by the commissioner of selection to perform a waste composition study.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.412.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Study requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner must:</p>
          <p>(1) determine the sampling methods to be used and the categories of materials to be sampled for waste composition studies; and</p>
          <p>(2) provide the sampling methods and any additional requirements identified by the commissioner to each owner or operator directed to perform a study.</p>
          <p>(b) The sampling methods must include the number of samples to be taken, the size or weight of each sample, the duration of a sampling event, the sampling interval, and any additional methods identified by the commissioner. The categories of materials to be sampled must include categories and subcategories identified by the commissioner to represent the materials present at each facility.</p>
          <p>(c) Resource recovery facilities required to do waste sorts required under air rules adopted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.07">116.07</a> must use the study requirements developed under this section when conducting waste composition analysis to meet the rule requirements.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner must obtain input from counties, cities, and owners or operators of waste facilities before finalizing the sampling methods and requirements. The commissioner must consider cost effectiveness and data quality when determining the sampling methods.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.412.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p>Within six months after completing a waste composition study required under this section, the owner or operator of a facility must submit the raw data and results of the study to the commissioner in a form and manner prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.412.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Compilation.</span></h2>
          <p>After each three-year cycle, the commissioner must compile and summarize the waste composition data received under subdivision 3. The commissioner must make the summary information available to the public.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.412.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Additional studies; information.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may conduct additional waste composition studies at facilities described in subdivision 1.</p>
          <p>(b) Upon request of the commissioner for purposes of determining compliance with this section, a person must furnish to the commissioner any information that the person has or may reasonably obtain.</p>
          <p>(c) The owner or operator of a facility shall allow access upon reasonable notice to authorized agency staff for the purpose of conducting waste composition studies.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.412.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.2.10.0">2024 c 116 art 2 s 10</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.415">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.415 SUBSTANDARD DISPOSAL FACILITIES.</h1>
        <p>(a) Beginning July 1, 1995:</p>
        <p>(1) a person may not deliver unprocessed mixed municipal solid waste to a substandard disposal facility; and</p>
        <p>(2) an operator of a substandard disposal facility may not accept unprocessed mixed municipal solid waste for deposit in the disposal facility.</p>
        <p>(b) For the purpose of this section, "substandard disposal facility" means a disposal facility that does not meet the design, construction, and operation requirements for a new mixed municipal solid waste facility contained in state rules in effect as of January 1, 1993.</p>
        <p>(c) For the purpose of this section, waste is "unprocessed" if it has not, after collection and before disposal, undergone at least one process, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.03#stat.115A.03.25">115A.03, subdivision 25</a>, excluding storage, exchange, and transfer of the waste.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.415.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/249/#laws.0.10.0">1993 c 249 s 10</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.42">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.42 REGIONAL AND LOCAL SOLID WASTE MANAGEMENT PLANNING.</h1>
        <p>There is established a program to encourage and improve regional and local solid waste management planning activities and efforts and to further the state policies and purposes expressed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.02">115A.02</a>. The program under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.42">115A.42</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.46">115A.46</a> is administered by the commissioner pursuant to rules promulgated under chapter 14, except in the metropolitan area where the program is administered by the commissioner pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.149">473.149</a>. The commissioner shall ensure conformance with federal requirements and programs established pursuant to the Resource Conservation and Recovery Act of 1976 and amendments thereto.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.42.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.5.1.0">1980 c 564 art 5 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/569/#laws.0.12.0">1982 c 569 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/348/#laws.0.15.0">1987 c 348 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.137.0">1987 c 404 s 137</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.2.10.0">1995 c 247 art 2 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.43"><b>115A.43</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/348/#laws.0.52.0">1987 c 348 s 52</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.44"><b>115A.44</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/348/#laws.0.52.0">1987 c 348 s 52</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.45">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.45 TECHNICAL ASSISTANCE.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall provide for technical assistance to encourage and improve solid waste management and to assist political subdivisions in preparing the plans described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.46">115A.46</a>. The commissioner shall provide model plans for regional and local solid waste management. The commissioner may contract for the delivery of technical assistance by a regional development commission, any state or federal agency, private consultants, or other persons. The commissioner shall prepare and publish an inventory of sources of technical assistance for solid waste planning, including studies, publications, agencies, and persons available.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.45.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.5.4.0">1980 c 564 art 5 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/348/#laws.0.16.0">1987 c 348 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.139.0">1987 c 404 s 139</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.5.3.0">1994 c 639 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.2.11.0">1995 c 247 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.46">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.46 REGIONAL AND LOCAL SOLID WASTE MANAGEMENT PLAN; REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.46.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Plans shall address the state policies and purposes expressed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.02">115A.02</a> and may not be inconsistent with state law.</p>
          <p>(b) Plans for the location, establishment, operation, maintenance, and postclosure use of facilities and facility sites, for ordinances, and for licensing, permit, and enforcement activities shall be consistent with the rules adopted by the agency pursuant to chapter 116.</p>
          <p>(c) Plans shall address:</p>
          <p>(1) the resolution of conflicting, duplicative, or overlapping local management efforts;</p>
          <p>(2) the establishment of joint powers management programs or waste management districts where appropriate; and</p>
          <p>(3) other matters as the rules of the agency may require consistent with the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.42">115A.42</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.46">115A.46</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) Political subdivisions preparing plans under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.42">115A.42</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.46">115A.46</a> shall consult with persons presently providing solid waste collection, processing, and disposal services.</p>
          <p>(e) Plans must be submitted to the commissioner for approval. When a county board is ready to have a final plan approved, the county board shall submit a resolution requesting review and approval by the commissioner. After receiving the resolution, the commissioner shall notify the county within 45 days whether the plan as submitted is complete and, if not complete, the specific items that need to be submitted to make the plan complete. Within 90 days after a complete plan has been submitted, the commissioner shall approve or disapprove the plan. If the plan is disapproved, reasons for the disapproval must be provided.</p>
          <p>(f) After initial approval, each plan must be updated and submitted for approval at least every ten years. The plan must be revised as necessary so that it is not inconsistent with state law.</p>
          <p>(g) Rules that regulate plan content under subdivision 2 must reflect demographic, geographic, regional, and solid waste system differences that exist among the counties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.46.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Contents.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The plans shall describe existing collection, processing, and disposal systems, including schedules of rates and charges, financing methods, environmental acceptability, and opportunities for improvements in the systems.</p>
          <p>(b) The plans shall include an estimate of the land disposal capacity in acre-feet which will be needed through the year 2000, on the basis of current and projected waste generation practices. In assessing the need for additional capacity for resource recovery or land disposal, the plans shall take into account the characteristics of waste stream components and shall give priority to waste reduction, separation, and recycling.</p>
          <p>(c) The plans shall require the most feasible and prudent reduction of the need for and practice of land disposal of mixed municipal solid waste.</p>
          <p>(d) The plans shall address at least waste reduction, separation, recycling, and other resource recovery options, and shall include specific and quantifiable objectives, immediately and over specified time periods, for reducing the land disposal of mixed municipal solid waste and for the implementation of feasible and prudent reduction, separation, recycling, and other resource recovery options. These objectives shall be consistent with statewide objectives as identified in statute. The plans shall describe methods for identifying the portions of the waste stream such as leaves, grass, clippings, tree and plant residue, and paper for application and mixing into the soil and use in agricultural practices. The plans shall describe specific functions to be performed and activities to be undertaken to achieve the abatement, reduction, separation, recycling, and other resource recovery objectives and shall describe the estimated cost, proposed manner of financing, and timing of the functions and activities. The plans shall describe proposed mechanisms for complying with the recycling requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.551">115A.551</a>, and the household hazardous waste management requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.96#stat.115A.96.6">115A.96, subdivision 6</a>.</p>
          <p>(e) The plans shall include a comparison of the costs of the activities to be undertaken, including capital and operating costs, and the effects of the activities on the cost to generators and on persons currently providing solid waste collection, processing, and disposal services. The plans shall include alternatives which could be used to achieve the abatement objectives if the proposed functions and activities are not established.</p>
          <p>(f) The plans shall designate how public education shall be accomplished. The plans shall, to the extent practicable and consistent with the achievement of other public policies and purposes, encourage ownership and operation of solid waste facilities by private industry. For solid waste facilities owned or operated by public agencies or supported primarily by public funds or obligations issued by a public agency, the plans shall include criteria and standards to protect comparable private and public facilities already existing in the area from displacement unless the displacement is required in order to achieve the waste management objectives identified in the plan.</p>
          <p>(g) The plans shall establish a siting procedure and development program to assure the orderly location, development, and financing of new or expanded solid waste facilities and services sufficient for a prospective ten-year period, including estimated costs and implementation schedules, proposed procedures for operation and maintenance, estimated annual costs and gross revenues, and proposals for the use of facilities after they are no longer needed or usable.</p>
          <p>(h) The plans shall describe existing and proposed county and municipal ordinances and license and permit requirements relating to solid waste management and shall describe existing and proposed regulation and enforcement procedures.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.46.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/644/#laws.0.82.0">1984 c 644 s 82</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.46.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Delegating solid waste responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p>A county or a solid waste management district established under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.62">115A.62</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.72">115A.72</a> may not delegate to another governmental unit or other person any portion of its responsibility for solid waste management unless it establishes a funding mechanism to assure the ability of the entity to which it delegates responsibility to adequately carry out the responsibility delegated.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.46.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Jurisdiction of plan.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) After a county plan has been submitted for approval under subdivision 1, a public entity, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.073#stat.16C.073.1">16C.073, subdivision 1</a>, within the county may not enter into a binding agreement governing a solid waste management activity that is inconsistent with the county plan without the consent of the county.</p>
          <p>(b) After a county plan has been approved under subdivision 1, the plan governs all solid waste management in the county and a public entity, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.073#stat.16C.073.1">16C.073, subdivision 1</a>, within the county may not develop or implement a solid waste management activity, other than an activity to reduce waste generation or reuse waste materials, that is inconsistent with the county plan that the county is actively implementing without the consent of the county.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.46.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.5.5.0">1980 c 564 art 5 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/569/#laws.0.13.0">1982 c 569 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/644/#laws.0.32.0">1984 c 644 s 32</a>,33; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.140.0">1987 c 404 s 140</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/131/#laws.0.3.0">1989 c 131 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/325/#laws.0.6.0">1989 c 325 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.20.3.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 20 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.15.0">1991 c 337 s 15</a>,16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.1.8.0">1995 c 247 art 1 s 8</a>; art 2 s 12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/13/#laws.0.1.0">2003 c 13 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/196/#laws.1.5.0">2014 c 196 art 1 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.47"><b>115A.47</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.2.55.0">1995 c 247 art 2 s 55</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.471">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.471 PUBLIC ENTITIES; MANAGING SOLID WASTE.</h1>
        <p>(a) Prior to entering into or approving a contract for the management of mixed municipal solid waste which would manage the waste using a waste management practice that is ranked lower on the list of preferred waste management practices in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.02">115A.02, paragraph (b)</a>, than the waste management practice selected for such waste in the county plan for the county in which the waste was generated, a public entity must:</p>
        <p>(1) determine the potential liability to the public entity and its taxpayers for managing the waste in this manner;</p>
        <p>(2) develop and implement a plan for managing the potential liability; and</p>
        <p>(3) submit the information from clauses (1) and (2) to the agency.</p>
        <p>(b) For the purpose of this subdivision, "public entity" means the state; an office, agency, or institution of the state; the Metropolitan Council; a metropolitan agency; the Metropolitan Mosquito Control District; the legislature; the courts; a county; a statutory or home rule charter city; a town; a school district; another special taxing district; or any other general or special purpose unit of government in the state.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.471.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.1.9.0">1995 c 247 art 1 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.48">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.48 MARKET DEVELOPMENT FOR RECYCLABLE MATERIALS AND COMPOST.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.48.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall assist and encourage the development of specific facilities, services, and uses needed to provide adequate, stable, and reliable markets for recyclable materials, solid waste suitable for land application, and compost generated in the state. In carrying out this duty, the commissioner shall coordinate and cooperate with the solid waste management efforts of other public agencies and political subdivisions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.48.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.28.0">1996 c 470 s 28</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.48.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Public procurement.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall provide technical assistance and advice to political subdivisions and other public agencies to encourage solid waste reduction and development of markets for recyclable materials and compost through procurement policies and practices. Political subdivisions, educational institutions, and other public agencies shall aggressively pursue procurement practices that encourage solid waste reduction, recycling, and development of markets for recyclable materials and compost and shall, whenever practical, procure products containing recycled materials.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.48.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Land application of solid waste.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall provide technical assistance and advice to political subdivisions on separating portions of the waste stream such as leaves, grass, clippings, tree and plant residue, and paper for application and mixing into the soil and for use in agricultural practices.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.48.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.28.0">1996 c 470 s 28</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.48.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/348/#laws.0.17.0">1987 c 348 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/685/#laws.0.11.0">1988 c 685 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/131/#laws.0.4.0">1989 c 131 s 4</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.18.10.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 18 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.5.3.0">1994 c 639 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.49">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.49 WASTE MANAGEMENT CAPITAL ASSISTANCE PROGRAM.</h1>
        <p>(a) There is established a program to encourage and assist cities, counties, solid waste management districts, and sanitary districts in the development and implementation of solid waste management projects and to transfer the knowledge and experience gained from such projects to other communities in the state.</p>
        <p>(b) The program must be administered to encourage local communities to develop feasible and prudent alternatives to disposal, including:</p>
        <p>(1) waste reduction;</p>
        <p>(2) reuse;</p>
        <p>(3) recycling;</p>
        <p>(4) composting source-separated compostable materials or yard waste;</p>
        <p>(5) resource recovery;</p>
        <p>(6) waste separation by generators, collectors, and other persons; and</p>
        <p>(7) waste processing.</p>
        <p>(c) The commissioner shall administer the program according to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.49">115A.49</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.54">115A.54</a> and rules adopted under chapter 14. In administering the program, the commissioner shall give priority to projects in the order of preference of the waste management practices listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.02">115A.02</a>. The commissioner shall give special consideration to areas where natural geologic and soil conditions are especially unsuitable for land disposal of solid waste; areas where the capacity of existing solid waste disposal facilities is determined by the commissioner to be less than five years; and projects serving more than one local government unit.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.49.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.6.1.0">1980 c 564 art 6 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/15/#laws.0.32.0">1Sp1985 c 15 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/348/#laws.0.18.0">1987 c 348 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.141.0">1987 c 404 s 141</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/524/#laws.0.2.0">1988 c 524 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.17.0">1991 c 337 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.3.9.0">2023 c 60 art 3 s 9</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.50">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.50 ELIGIBLE RECIPIENTS.</h1>
        <p>Eligible recipients for assistance under the program shall be limited to cities, counties, solid waste management districts established pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.62">115A.62</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.72">115A.72</a>, and sanitary districts. Eligible recipients may apply for assistance under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.0716">115A.0716</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.52">115A.52</a> on behalf of other persons.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.50.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.6.2.0">1980 c 564 art 6 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/524/#laws.0.3.0">1988 c 524 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.7.0">1996 c 470 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.51">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.51 APPLICATION REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <p>(a) Applications for assistance under the program must demonstrate:</p>
        <p>(1) that the project is conceptually and technically feasible;</p>
        <p>(2) that affected political subdivisions are committed to implement the project, to provide necessary local financing, and to accept and exercise the government powers necessary to the project;</p>
        <p>(3) that operating revenues from the project, considering the availability and security of sources of solid waste and of markets for recovered resources or the availability of materials for waste reduction or reuse, together with any proposed federal, state, or local financial assistance, will be sufficient to pay all costs over the projected life of the project;</p>
        <p>(4) that the applicant has evaluated the feasible and prudent alternatives to disposal, including using existing solid waste management facilities and facilities conducting waste reduction or reuse with reasonably available capacity sufficient to accomplish the goals of the proposed project, and has compared and evaluated the costs of the alternatives, including capital and operating costs, and the effects of the alternatives on the cost to generators;</p>
        <p>(5) that the applicant has identified:</p>
        <p>(i) waste management objectives in applicable county and regional solid waste management plans consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.46#stat.115A.46.2">115A.46, subdivision 2</a>, paragraphs (e) and (f), or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.149#stat.473.149.1">473.149, subdivision 1</a>; and</p>
        <p>(ii) other solid waste management facilities and facilities conducting waste reduction or reuse identified in the county and regional plans;</p>
        <p>(6) that the applicant has conducted a comparative analysis of the project against existing public and private solid waste management facilities and facilities conducting waste reduction or reuse, including an analysis of potential displacement of those facilities, to determine whether the project is the most appropriate alternative to achieve the identified waste management objectives that considers:</p>
        <p>(i) conformity with approved county or regional solid waste management plans;</p>
        <p>(ii) consistency with the state's solid waste hierarchy and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.46">115A.46, subdivision </a>2, paragraphs (e) and (f), or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.149#stat.473.149.1">473.149, subdivision 1</a>; and</p>
        <p>(iii) environmental standards related to public health, air, surface water, and groundwater;</p>
        <p>(7) that the applicant has evaluated the project's environmental impact on climate change, including greenhouse gas emissions; and</p>
        <p>(8) that the applicant has reviewed the project's impact on environmental justice areas, conducted stakeholder engagement, and assessed community input.</p>
        <p>(b) The commissioner must require completion of a comprehensive solid waste management plan conforming to the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.46">115A.46</a>, before accepting an application. Within five days of filing an application with the agency, the applicant must submit a copy of the application to each solid waste management facility, including each facility used for waste reduction or reuse, mentioned in the portion of the application addressing the requirements of paragraph (a), clauses (5) and (6).</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.51.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.6.3.0">1980 c 564 art 6 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/348/#laws.0.19.0">1987 c 348 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.142.0">1987 c 404 s 142</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.5.3.0">1994 c 639 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.98.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 98</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.3.10.0">2023 c 60 art 3 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.52">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.52 TECHNICAL ASSISTANCE FOR PROJECTS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall ensure the delivery of technical assistance for projects eligible under the program. The commissioner may contract or issue grants for the delivery of technical assistance by any state or federal agency, a regional development commission, the Metropolitan Council, or private consultants and may use program funds to reimburse the agency, commission, council, or consultants. The commissioner shall prepare and publish an inventory of sources of technical assistance, including studies, publications, agencies, and persons available. The commissioner shall ensure statewide benefit from projects assisted under the program by developing exchange and training programs for local officials and employees and by using the experience gained in projects to provide technical assistance and education for other solid waste management projects in the state.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.52.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.6.4.0">1980 c 564 art 6 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/15/#laws.0.33.0">1Sp1985 c 15 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/348/#laws.0.20.0">1987 c 348 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.143.0">1987 c 404 s 143</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.5.3.0">1994 c 639 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/73/#laws.0.3.0">1999 c 73 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.53"><b>115A.53</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.28.0">1996 c 470 s 28</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.54">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.54 WASTE MANAGEMENT CAPITAL ASSISTANCE PROJECTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.54.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purposes; public interest; declaration of policy.</span></h2>
          <p>The legislature finds that acquiring, establishing, and improving facilities that conduct waste reduction, reuse, recycling, composting source-separated compostable materials or yard waste, resource recovery, and waste processing and transfer stations serving such facilities is needed to reduce and manage properly the solid waste generated in the state and to conserve and protect the natural resources in the state and the health, safety, and welfare of its citizens; that opportunities to acquire, establish, and improve the facilities and transfer stations are not being fully realized by individual political subdivisions or by agreements among subdivisions; and that therefore it is necessary to provide capital assistance to stimulate and encourage the acquisition, establishment, and improvement of the facilities and transfer stations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.54.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Administration; assurance of funds.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall provide technical and financial assistance to acquire, establish, and improve the facilities and transfer stations from revenues derived from issuing bonds authorized by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.58">115A.58</a>. Facilities for incinerating solid waste without resource recovery are not eligible for assistance. Money appropriated for the purposes of the program may be distributed as grants or loans. An individual project may receive assistance totaling up to 100 percent of the capital cost of the project and grants up to 75 percent of the capital cost of the project. No grant or loan shall be disbursed to any recipient until the commissioner has determined the total estimated capital cost of the project and ascertained that financing of the cost is assured by funds provided by the state, by an agency of the federal government within the amount of funds then appropriated to that agency and allocated by it to projects within the state, by any person, or by the appropriation of proceeds of bonds or other funds of the recipient to a fund for constructing the project.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.54.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Solid waste management projects.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall provide technical and financial assistance to acquire, establish, and improve solid waste management projects as provided in this subdivision and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.52">115A.52</a>. Money appropriated for the purposes of this subdivision must be distributed as grants.</p>
          <p>(b) Except as provided in paragraph (c) or (d), a project may receive grant assistance up to 25 percent of the capital cost of the project or $5,000,000, whichever is less, except that projects constructed as a result of intercounty cooperative agreements may receive the lesser of:</p>
          <p>(1) grant assistance up to 25 percent of the capital cost of the project; or</p>
          <p>(2) $5,000,000 times the number of participating counties.</p>
          <p>(c) A recycling project, a project to compost source-separated compostable material or yard waste, or a project to manage household hazardous waste may receive grant assistance up to 50 percent of the capital cost of the project or $5,000,000, whichever is less, except that projects completed as a result of intercounty cooperative agreements may receive the lesser of:</p>
          <p>(1) grant assistance up to 50 percent of the capital cost of the project; or</p>
          <p>(2) $5,000,000 times the number of participating counties.</p>
          <p>(d) The following projects may also receive grant assistance in the amounts specified in paragraph (c):</p>
          <p>(1) a project to improve control of or reduce air emissions at an existing resource recovery facility; and</p>
          <p>(2) a project to substantially increase the recovery of materials or energy, substantially reduce the amount or toxicity of waste processing residuals, or expand the capacity of an existing resource recovery facility to meet the resource recovery needs of an expanded region if each county from which waste is or would be received has achieved a recycling rate in excess of the goals in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.551">115A.551</a>, and is implementing aggressive waste reduction and household hazardous waste management programs.</p>
          <p>(e) A waste reduction project or reuse project may receive grant assistance up to 75 percent of the capital cost of the project or $5,000,000, whichever is less, except that projects completed as a result of intercounty cooperative agreements may receive the lesser of:</p>
          <p>(1) grant assistance up to 75 percent of the capital cost of the project; or</p>
          <p>(2) $5,000,000 times the number of participating counties.</p>
          <p>(f) Notwithstanding paragraph (g), the commissioner may award grants for transfer stations that will initially transfer waste to landfills if the transfer stations are part of a planned resource recovery project, the county where the planned resource recovery facility will be located has a comprehensive solid waste management plan approved by the commissioner, and the solid waste management plan proposes the development of the resource recovery facility. If the proposed resource recovery facility is not in place and operating within 16 years of the date of the grant award, the recipient shall repay the grant amount to the state.</p>
          <p>(g) Projects without waste reduction, reuse, recycling, composting source-separated compostable material or yard waste, or resource recovery are not eligible for assistance. Solid waste disposal facilities and equipment are not eligible for assistance.</p>
          <p>(h) In addition to any assistance received under paragraph (b), (c), (d), or (e), a project may receive grant assistance for the cost of tests necessary to determine the appropriate pollution control equipment for the project or the environmental effects of the use of any product or material produced by the project.</p>
          <p>(i) In addition to the application requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.51">115A.51</a>, an application for a project serving eligible jurisdictions in only a single county must demonstrate that cooperation with jurisdictions in other counties to develop the project is not needed or not feasible. Each application must also demonstrate that the project is not financially prudent without the state assistance, because of the applicant's financial capacity and the problems inherent in the waste management situation in the area, particularly transportation distances and limited waste supply and markets for resources recovered.</p>
          <p>(j) For the purposes of this subdivision, "project" means acquisition, establishment, or improvement of a facility that conducts waste reduction, reuse, recycling, composting source-separated compostable materials or yard waste, resource recovery, or waste processing, together with any transfer stations, transmission facilities, and other related and appurtenant facilities primarily serving the facility.</p>
          <p>(k) The commissioner shall adopt rules for the program.</p>
          <p>(l) Notwithstanding anything in this subdivision to the contrary, a project to construct a new solid waste transfer station that has an enforceable commitment of at least ten years, or of sufficient length to retire bonds sold for the facility, to serve an existing resource recovery facility may receive grant assistance up to 75 percent of the capital cost of the project if addition of the transfer station will increase substantially the geographical area served by the resource recovery facility and the ability of the resource recovery facility to operate more efficiently on a regional basis and the facility meets the criteria in paragraph (d), clause (2). A transfer station eligible for assistance under this paragraph is not eligible for assistance under any other paragraph of this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.54.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Obligations of recipient.</span></h2>
          <p>No grant or loan for any project shall be disbursed until the governing body of the recipient has made an irrevocable undertaking, by resolution, to use all funds made available exclusively for the capital cost of the project and to pay any additional amount by which the cost of the project exceeds the estimate by appropriation to the construction fund of additional funds or proceeds of additional bonds of the recipient. The resolution shall also indicate that any subsequent withdrawal of allocated or additional funds of the recipient will impair the obligation of contract between the state of Minnesota, the recipient, and the bondholders. The resolution shall pledge payment to the debt service account of all revenues of the project to the extent that they exceed costs and shall also obligate the recipient to levy a tax sufficient to make timely payments under the loan agreement, if a deficiency occurs in the amount of user charges, taxes, special assessments, or other money pledged for payment under the loan agreement. Each loan made to a recipient shall be secured by resolutions adopted by the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency and the governing body of the recipient, obligating the recipient to repay the loan to the commissioner of management and budget in annual installments including both principal and interest. Installments shall be in an amount sufficient to pay the principal amount within the period required by the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency. The interest on the loan shall be calculated on the declining balance at a rate not less than the average annual interest rate on the state bonds of the issue from which proceeds of the loan were made. The resolution shall obligate the recipient to provide money for the repayment from user charges, taxes, special assessments or any other funds available to it.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.54.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Terminating obligations; good faith effort.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.695">16A.695</a>, the commissioner may terminate the obligations of a grant or loan recipient under this section, if the commissioner finds that the recipient has made a good faith effort to exhaust all options in trying to comply with the terms and conditions of the grant or loan. In lieu of declaring a default on a grant or a loan under this section, the commissioner may identify additional measures a recipient should take in order to meet the good faith test required for terminating the recipient's obligations under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.54.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.6.6.0">1980 c 564 art 6 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/352/#laws.0.26.0">1981 c 352 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/373/#laws.0.38.0">1983 c 373 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/274/#laws.0.5.0">1985 c 274 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/15/#laws.0.34.0">1Sp1985 c 15 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/348/#laws.0.22.0">1987 c 348 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/325/#laws.0.7.0">1989 c 325 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/594/#laws.1.50.0">1990 c 594 art 1 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/249/#laws.0.11.0">1993 c 249 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.5.0">1994 c 585 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.5.3.0">1994 c 639 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.95.0">1997 c 216 s 95</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.123.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 123</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.125.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 125</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.15.0">2014 c 248 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/25/#laws.0.34.0">2023 c 25 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.3.11.0">2023 c 60 art 3 s 11</a>-13</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.541">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.541 PLAN; GRANT REQUIREMENT.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may approve a plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.46">115A.46</a> or make a grant for a recycling facility under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.54#stat.115A.54.2a">115A.54, subdivision 2a</a>, only if the commissioner finds that the applicant demonstrates a commitment to recycle materials separated by generators to the extent the program is cost-effective in meeting recycling goals.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.541.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/685/#laws.0.12.0">1988 c 685 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.5.3.0">1994 c 639 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.542"><b>115A.542</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.57.0">1994 c 585 s 57</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.545"><b>115A.545</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/13/#laws.2.6.0">2007 c 13 art 2 s 6</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.55">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.55 SOLID WASTE REDUCTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.55.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Coordination.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall develop and coordinate solid waste reduction programs to include at least public education, promotion of waste reduction, and technical and financial assistance to solid waste generators.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.55.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Technical assistance.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall provide technical assistance to solid waste generators to enable the waste generators to implement programs or methods to reduce the amount of solid waste generated. The commissioner may use any means specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.52">115A.52</a> to provide technical assistance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.55.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.28.0">1996 c 470 s 28</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.55.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Statewide reduction goal.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) It is a goal of the state and counties to reduce the generation of municipal solid waste.</p>
          <p>(b) As part of the report required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.411">115A.411</a>, the commissioner shall submit to the senate and house of representatives committees having jurisdiction over environment and natural resources and environment and natural resources finance a proposed strategy for meeting the goal in paragraph (a). The strategy must include a discussion of the different reduction potentials to be found in various sectors and may include recommended interim goals. The commissioner shall report progress on meeting the goal in paragraph (a), as well as recommendations and revisions to the proposed strategy, as part of the report required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.411">115A.411</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.55.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
