        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.20.5.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 20 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.5.3.0">1994 c 639 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.1.10.0">1995 c 247 art 1 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.27.0">1996 c 470 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.13.25.0">2014 c 312 art 13 s 25</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.5501"><b>115A.5501</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.2.35.0">2024 c 116 art 2 s 35</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.5502">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.5502 PACKAGING PRACTICES; PREFERENCES; GOALS.</h1>
        <p>Packaging forms a substantial portion of solid waste and contributes to environmental degradation and the costs of managing solid waste. It is imperative to reduce the amount and toxicity of packaging that must be managed as solid waste. In order to achieve significant reduction of packaging in solid waste, the goal of the state is that items be distributed without any packaging where feasible and, only when necessary to protect health and safety or product integrity, with the minimal amount of packaging possible. The following categories of packaging are listed in order of preference for use by all persons who find it necessary to package items for distribution or use in the state:</p>
        <p>(1) minimal packaging that contains no intentionally introduced toxic materials and that is designed to be and actually is reused for its original purpose at least five times;</p>
        <p>(2) minimal packaging that contains no intentionally introduced toxic materials and consists of a significant percentage of postconsumer material;</p>
        <p>(3) minimal packaging that contains no intentionally introduced toxic materials, that is recyclable, and is regularly collected through recycling collection programs available to at least 75 percent of the residents of the state;</p>
        <p>(4) minimal packaging that does not comply with clause (1), (2), or (3) because it is required under federal or state law and for which there does not exist a commercially feasible alternative that does comply with clause (1), (2), or (3);</p>
        <p>(5) packaging that contains no intentionally introduced toxic materials but does not comply with clauses (1) to (4); and</p>
        <p>(6) all other packaging.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.5502.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.11.0">1994 c 585 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.1.13.0">1995 c 247 art 1 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.2.11.0">2024 c 116 art 2 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.551">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.551 RECYCLING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.551.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, "recycling" means, in addition to the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.03#stat.115A.03.25b">115A.03, subdivision 25b</a>, yard waste and source-separated compostable materials composting and recycling that occurs through mechanical or hand separation of materials that are then delivered for use in manufacturing processes that do not cause the destruction of recyclable materials in a manner that precludes further use.</p>
          <p>(b) For the purposes of this section, "total solid waste generation" means the total by weight of:</p>
          <p>(1) materials separated for recycling;</p>
          <p>(2) materials separated for yard waste and source-separated compostable materials composting;</p>
          <p>(3) mixed municipal solid waste plus motor and vehicle fluids and filters, tires, lead acid batteries, and major appliances; and</p>
          <p>(4) residential waste materials that would be mixed municipal solid waste but for the fact that they are not collected as such.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.551.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.13.48.0">2014 c 312 art 13 s 48</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.551.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">County recycling goals.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) By December 31, 2030, each county will have as a goal to recycle the following amounts:</p>
          <p>(1) for a county outside of the metropolitan area, 35 percent by weight of total solid waste generation; and</p>
          <p>(2) for a metropolitan county, 75 percent by weight of total solid waste generation.</p>
          <p>(b) Each county will develop and implement or require political subdivisions within the county to develop and implement programs, practices, or methods designed to meet its recycling goal. Nothing in this section or in any other law may be construed to prohibit a county from establishing a higher recycling goal.</p>
          <p>(c) Any quantified recyclable materials that meet the definition in subdivision 1, paragraph (a), or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.03#stat.115A.03.25a">115A.03, subdivision 25a</a>, are eligible to be counted toward a county's recycling goal under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.551.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Interim goals; nonmetropolitan counties.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall establish interim recycling goals for the nonmetropolitan counties to assist them in meeting the goals established in subdivision 2a.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.551.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Interim monitoring.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall monitor the progress of each county toward meeting the recycling goals in subdivision 2a. The commissioner shall report to the senate and house of representatives committees having jurisdiction over environment and natural resources as part of the report required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.411">115A.411</a>. If the commissioner finds that a county is not progressing toward the goals in subdivision 2a, the commissioner shall negotiate with the county to develop and implement solid waste management techniques designed to assist the county in meeting the goals, such as organized collection, curbside collection of source-separated materials, and volume-based pricing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.551.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Failure to meet goal.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A county failing to meet the interim goals in subdivision 3 shall, as a minimum:</p>
          <p>(1) notify county residents of the failure to achieve the goal and why the goal was not achieved; and</p>
          <p>(2) provide county residents with information on recycling programs offered by the county.</p>
          <p>(b) If, based on the recycling monitoring described in subdivision 4, the commissioner finds that a county will be unable to meet the recycling goals established in subdivision 2a, the commissioner shall, after consideration of the reasons for the county's inability to meet the goals, recommend legislation for consideration by the senate and house of representatives committees having jurisdiction over environment and natural resources and environment and natural resources finance to establish mandatory recycling standards and to authorize the commissioner to mandate appropriate solid waste management techniques designed to meet the standards in those counties that are unable to meet the goals.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.551.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">County solid waste plans.</span></h2>
          <p>Each county shall include in its solid waste management plan described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.46">115A.46</a>, or its solid waste master plan described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.803">473.803</a>, a recycling implementation strategy for meeting the recycling goal established in subdivision 2a along with mechanisms for providing financial incentives to solid waste generators to reduce the amount of waste generated and to separate recyclable materials from the waste stream.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.551.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Recycling implementation strategy.</span></h2>
          <p>Each county shall submit to the commissioner for approval the recycling implementation strategy required in subdivision 6. The recycling implementation strategy must be submitted by October 31, 1995, and must:</p>
          <p>(1) be consistent with the approved county solid waste management plan;</p>
          <p>(2) identify the materials that are being and will be recycled in the county to meet the goals under this section and the parties responsible and methods for recycling the material;</p>
          <p>(3) provide a budget to ensure adequate funding for needed county and local programs and demonstrate an ongoing commitment to spending the money on recycling programs; and</p>
          <p>(4) include a schedule for implementing recycling activities needed to meet the goals in subdivision 2a.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.551.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.18.12.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 18 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.19.0">1991 c 337 s 19</a>-21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/593/#laws.1.14.0">1992 c 593 art 1 s 14</a>-16,54; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/249/#laws.0.13.0">1993 c 249 s 13</a>,14,61; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.5.3.0">1994 c 639 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.1.14.0">1995 c 247 art 1 s 14</a>-17; art 2 s 15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.27.0">1996 c 470 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/73/#laws.0.4.0">1999 c 73 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.67.0">2012 c 272 s 67</a>,68; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.13.26.0">2014 c 312 art 13 s 26</a>,27; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.108.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 108</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.26.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 26</a>-28</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.552">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.552 OPPORTUNITY TO RECYCLE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.552.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">County requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>Counties shall ensure that residents, including residents of single and multifamily dwellings, have an opportunity to recycle. At least one recycling center shall be available in each county. Opportunity to recycle means availability of recycling and curbside pickup or collection centers for recyclable materials at sites that are convenient for persons to use. Counties shall also provide for the recycling of problem materials and major appliances. Counties shall assess the operation of existing and proposed recycling centers and shall give due consideration to those centers in ensuring the opportunity to recycle. To the extent practicable, the costs incurred by a county for collection, storage, transportation, and recycling of major appliances must be collected from persons who discard the major appliances.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.552.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Recycling opportunities.</span></h2>
          <p>An opportunity to recycle must include:</p>
          <p>(1) a local recycling center in the county and sites for collecting recyclable materials that are located in areas convenient for persons to use them;</p>
          <p>(2) curbside pickup, centralized drop-off, or a local recycling center for at least four broad types of recyclable materials in cities with a population of 5,000 or more persons; and</p>
          <p>(3) monthly pickup of at least four broad types of recyclable materials in cities of the first and second class and cities with 5,000 or more population in the metropolitan area.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.552.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Recycling information, education, and promotion.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Each county shall provide information on how, when, and where materials may be recycled, including a promotional program that publishes notices at least once every three months and encourages source separation of residential, commercial, industrial, and institutional materials.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner shall develop materials for counties to use in providing information on and promotion of recycling.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner shall provide technical assistance to counties to help counties implement recycling programs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.552.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Nonresidential recycling.</span></h2>
          <p>Each county shall encourage building owners and managers, business owners and managers, and collectors of commercial mixed municipal solid waste to provide appropriate recycling services and opportunities to generators of commercial, industrial, and institutional solid waste in the county.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.552.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.18.13.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 18 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.22.0">1991 c 337 s 22</a>-24; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.5.3.0">1994 c 639 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.553">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.553 COLLECTING AND TRANSPORTING RECYCLABLE MATERIALS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.553.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Collection centers and transportation required.</span></h2>
          <p>Each county must ensure alone or in conjunction with other counties that materials separated for recycling are taken to markets for sale or to recyclable material processing centers. An action may not be taken by a county under this section to preclude a person generating or collecting solid waste from delivering recyclable materials to a recycling facility of the generator's or collector's choice.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.553.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Licensing recyclable materials collection.</span></h2>
          <p>Counties may require county or municipal licenses for collection of recyclable materials.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.553.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Transportation systems.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency and the commissioner of transportation shall develop an efficient transportation system for recyclable materials to reach markets and processing centers that may be used by counties. The system may include regional collection centers.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.553.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.18.14.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 18 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.5.3.0">1994 c 639 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.554">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.554 AUTHORITY OF SANITARY DISTRICTS.</h1>
        <p>A sanitary district has the authorities and duties of counties within the district's boundary for purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.0716">115A.0716</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.46">115A.46</a>, subdivisions 4 and 5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.48">115A.48</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.551">115A.551</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.552">115A.552</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.553">115A.553</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.919">115A.919</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.929">115A.929</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.93">115A.93</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.96#stat.115A.96.6">115A.96, subdivision 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.961">115A.961</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072">116.072</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/375.18#stat.375.18.14">375.18, subdivision 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/400.04">400.04</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/400.06">400.06</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/400.07">400.07</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/400.08">400.08</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/400.16">400.16</a>; and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/400.161">400.161</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.554.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.18.15.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 18 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.25.0">1991 c 337 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.12.0">1994 c 585 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.1.18.0">1995 c 247 art 1 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.8.0">1996 c 470 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/231/#laws.16.2.0">1997 c 231 art 16 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/220/#laws.8.14.0">2002 c 220 art 8 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.130.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/277/#laws.1.8.0">2008 c 277 art 1 s 8</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.555">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.555 RECYCLING CENTER DESIGNATION.</h1>
        <p>The agency shall designate recycling centers for the purpose of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/173.086">173.086</a>. To be designated as a recycling center, a recycling facility must be open a minimum of 12 operating hours each week, 12 months each year, and must accept for recycling:</p>
        <p>(1) at least four different materials such as paper, glass, plastic, and metal; and</p>
        <p>(2) if the recycling center accepts metal, hazard signs, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.242#stat.161.242.2">161.242, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (d), to the same extent that a junk yard dealer must accept hazard signs under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.242#stat.161.242.6a">161.242, subdivision 6a</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.555.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.18.16.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 18 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/197/#laws.0.1.0">1991 c 197 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.556">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.556 MATERIALS USED FOR RECYCLING.</h1>
        <p>Materials and products used for recycling such as containers, receptacles, and storage bins with short life cycles must be recyclable and made at least in part from recycled materials from this state, if available.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.556.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/325/#laws.0.8.0">1989 c 325 s 8</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.557">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.557 COUNTY WASTE REDUCTION AND RECYCLING FUNDING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.557.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Distribution; formula.</span></h2>
          <p>Any funds appropriated to the commissioner for the purpose of distribution to counties under this section must be distributed each fiscal year by the commissioner based on population, except a county may not receive less than $55,000 in a fiscal year. If the amount available for distribution under this section is less or more than the amount available in fiscal year 2001, the minimum county payment under this section is reduced or increased proportionately. For purposes of this subdivision, "population" has the definition given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/477A.011#stat.477A.011.3">477A.011, subdivision 3</a>. A county that participates in a multicounty district that manages solid waste and that has responsibility for recycling programs as authorized in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.552">115A.552</a>, must pass through to the districts funds received by the county in excess of the minimum county payment under this section in proportion to the population of the county served by that district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.557.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Permissible expenditures.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A county receiving money distributed by the commissioner under this section may use the money only for the development and implementation of programs to:</p>
          <p>(1) reduce the amount of solid waste generated;</p>
          <p>(2) recycle the maximum amount of solid waste technically feasible;</p>
          <p>(3) create and support markets for recycled products;</p>
          <p>(4) remove problem materials from the solid waste stream and develop proper disposal options for them;</p>
          <p>(5) inform and educate all sectors of the public about proper solid waste management procedures;</p>
          <p>(6) provide technical assistance to public and private entities to ensure proper solid waste management;</p>
          <p>(7) provide educational, technical, and financial assistance for litter prevention;</p>
          <p>(8) process mixed municipal solid waste generated in the county at a resource recovery facility located in Minnesota;</p>
          <p>(9) compost source-separated compostable materials, including the provision of receptacles for residential composting;</p>
          <p>(10) prevent food waste or collect and transport food donated to humans or to be fed to animals; and</p>
          <p>(11) process source-separated compostable materials that are to be used to produce class I or class II compost, as defined in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/7035.2836">7035.2836</a>, after being processed in an anaerobic digester, but not to construct buildings or acquire equipment.</p>
          <p>(b) Beginning in fiscal year 2015 and continuing thereafter, of any money distributed by the commissioner under this section to a metropolitan county, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121#stat.473.121.4">473.121, subdivision 4</a>, that exceeds the amount the county was eligible to receive under this section in fiscal year 2014: (1) at least 50 percent must be expended on activities in paragraph (a), clauses (9) to (11); and (2) the remainder must be expended on activities in paragraph (a), clauses (1) to (7) and (9) to (11), that advance the county toward achieving its recycling goal under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.551">115A.551</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.557.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) To be eligible to receive money distributed by the commissioner under this section, a county shall within one year of October 4, 1989:</p>
          <p>(1) create a separate account in its general fund to credit the money; and</p>
          <p>(2) set up accounting procedures to ensure that money in the separate account is spent only for the purposes in subdivision 2.</p>
          <p>(b) In each following year, each county shall also:</p>
          <p>(1) have in place an approved solid waste management plan or master plan including a recycling implementation strategy under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.551#stat.115A.551.7">115A.551, subdivision 7</a>, and a household hazardous waste management plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.96#stat.115A.96.6">115A.96, subdivision 6</a>, by the dates specified in those provisions;</p>
          <p>(2) submit a report by April 1 of each year to the commissioner, which may be submitted electronically and must be posted on the agency's website, detailing for the previous calendar year:</p>
          <p>(i) how the money was spent including, but not limited to, specific recycling and composting activities undertaken to increase the county's proportion of solid waste recycled in order to achieve its recycling goal established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.551">115A.551</a>; specific information on the number of employees performing SCORE planning, oversight, and administration; the percentage of those employees' total work time allocated to SCORE planning, oversight, and administration; the specific duties and responsibilities of those employees; and the amount of staff salary for these SCORE duties and responsibilities of the employees; and</p>
          <p>(ii) the resulting gains achieved in solid waste management practices; and</p>
          <p>(3) provide evidence to the commissioner that local revenue equal to 25 percent of the money sought for distribution under this section will be spent for the purposes in subdivision 2.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner shall withhold all or part of the funds to be distributed to a county under this section if the county fails to comply with this subdivision and subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.557.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p> The commissioner shall report on how the money was spent and the resulting statewide improvements in solid waste management to the senate and house of representatives committees having jurisdiction over ways and means, finance, environment and natural resources, and environment and natural resources finance. The report shall be included in the report required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.411">115A.411</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.557.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.19.1.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 19 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.26.0">1991 c 337 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/593/#laws.1.17.0">1992 c 593 art 1 s 17</a>,54; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.13.0">1994 c 585 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.5.3.0">1994 c 639 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.1.19.0">1995 c 247 art 1 s 19</a>,20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.27.0">1996 c 470 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/490/#laws.10.1.0">2000 c 490 art 10 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.125.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 125</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/374/#laws.6.2.0">2002 c 374 art 6 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/284/#laws.2.11.0">2004 c 284 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.42.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.69.0">2012 c 272 s 69</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.13.28.0">2014 c 312 art 13 s 28</a>,29; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.109.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.558">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.558 SAFETY GUIDE.</h1>
        <p>The Pollution Control Agency shall prepare and distribute to all interested persons a guide for operation of a recycling or yard waste composting facility to protect the environment and public health.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.558.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.22.2.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 22 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.2.16.0">1995 c 247 art 2 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.559">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.559 COMPOSTING; COMPETITIVE GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.559.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Grant program established.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall make competitive grants to political subdivisions to increase composting, reduce the amount of organic wastes entering disposal facilities, and reduce the costs associated with hauling waste by locating the composting site as close as possible to the site where the waste is generated. To achieve the purpose of the grant program, the commissioner shall actively recruit potential applicants beyond traditional solid waste professionals and organizations, such as soil and water conservation districts and schools. Each grant must include an educational component on the methods and benefits of composting.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.559.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner must develop forms and procedures for soliciting and reviewing applications for grants under this section.</p>
          <p>(b) The determination of whether to make a grant under this section is within the discretion of the commissioner, subject to subdivision 4. The commissioner's decisions are not subject to judicial review, except for abuse of discretion.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.559.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Priorities; eligible projects.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If applications for grants exceed the available appropriations, grants must be made for projects that, in the commissioner's judgment, provide the highest return in public benefits.</p>
          <p>(b) To be eligible to receive a grant, a project must:</p>
          <p>(1) be locally administered;</p>
          <p>(2) have measurable outcomes; and</p>
          <p>(3) include at least one of the following elements:</p>
          <p>(i) the development of erosion control methods that use compost;</p>
          <p>(ii) activities to encourage on-site composting by homeowners; or</p>
          <p>(iii) activities to encourage composting by schools or public institutions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.559.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Cancellation of grant.</span></h2>
          <p>If a grant is awarded under this section and funds are not encumbered for the grant within four years after the award date, the grant must be canceled.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.559.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.43.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 43</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.56"><b>115A.56</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/359/#laws.0.11.0">1996 c 359 s 11</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.565">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.565 WASTE REDUCTION, REUSE, RECYCLING, AND COMPOSTING; COMPETITIVE GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.565.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Grant program established.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must make competitive grants to political subdivisions or federally recognized Tribes for waste reduction, reuse, recycling, and composting of source-separated compostable materials or yard waste. To be eligible for grants under this section, a political subdivision or federally recognized Tribe must be located outside the seven-county metropolitan area and a city must have a population of less than 45,000.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.565.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner must develop forms and procedures for soliciting and reviewing applications for grants under this section.</p>
          <p>(b) The determination of whether to make a grant under this section is within the discretion of the commissioner, subject to subdivision 4. The commissioner's decisions are not subject to judicial review, except for abuse of discretion.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.565.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Priorities; eligible projects.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If applications for grants exceed the available appropriations, grants must be made for projects that, in the commissioner's judgment, provide the highest return in public benefits.</p>
          <p>(b) To be eligible to receive a grant, a project must:</p>
          <p>(1) be locally administered;</p>
          <p>(2) have an educational component and measurable outcomes;</p>
          <p>(3) request $250,000 or less;</p>
          <p>(4) demonstrate local direct and indirect matching support of at least a quarter amount of the grant request;</p>
          <p>(5) include at least one of the following elements:</p>
          <p>(i) waste reduction;</p>
          <p>(ii) reuse;</p>
          <p>(iii) recycling; or</p>
          <p>(iv) composting of source-separated compostable materials or yard waste; and</p>
          <p>(6) demonstrate that the project will reduce waste generation through waste reduction or reuse or that the project will increase the amount of recyclable materials or source-separated compostable materials diverted from a disposal facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.565.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Cancellation of grant.</span></h2>
          <p>If a grant is awarded under this section and funds are not encumbered for the grant within four years after the award date, the grant must be canceled.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.565.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.110.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 110</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.92.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 92</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.3.14.0">2023 c 60 art 3 s 14</a>,15</p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">STATE WASTE MANAGEMENT BONDS</h1>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.57"><b>115A.57</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/271/#laws.0.36.0">1989 c 271 s 36</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.58">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.58 STATE WASTE MANAGEMENT BONDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.58.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority to issue bonds.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of management and budget shall sell bonds of the state of Minnesota for the prompt and full payment of which, together with interest, the full faith, credit, and taxing powers of the state are irrevocably pledged. Bonds shall be sold only upon request of the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency and in the amount as may otherwise be authorized by this or a subsequently enacted law which authorizes the sale of additional bonds and the deposit of the proceeds in a waste management account in the bond proceeds fund. Any authorized amount of bonds in this law or any subsequently enacted law authorizing the issuance of bonds for the purposes of the waste management account, together with this section, constitute complete authority for the issue. The bonds shall not be subject to restrictions or limitations contained in any other law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.58.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Issuing bonds.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon request by the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency and upon authorization as provided in subdivision 1, the commissioner of management and budget shall sell Minnesota state waste management bonds. The bonds shall be in the aggregate amount requested, and sold upon sealed bids upon the notice, at the price in the form and denominations, bearing interest at the rate or rates, maturing in the amounts and on the dates (with or without option of prepayment upon notice and at specified times and prices), payable at a bank or banks within or outside the state (with provisions, if any, for registration, conversion, and exchange and for the issuance of temporary bonds or notes in anticipation of the sale or delivery of definitive bonds), and in accordance with further provisions as the commissioner of management and budget shall determine, subject to the approval of the attorney general, but not subject to chapter 14, including section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a>. The bonds shall be executed by the commissioner of management and budget under official seal. The signature on the bonds and any interest coupons and the seal may be printed, lithographed, engraved, stamped, or otherwise reproduced thereon, except that each bond shall be authenticated by the manual signature on its face of the commissioner of management and budget or of an authorized representative of a bank designated by the commissioner of management and budget as registrar or other authenticating agent. The commissioner of management and budget shall ascertain and certify to the purchasers of the bonds the performance and existence of all acts, conditions, and things necessary to make them valid and binding general obligations of the state of Minnesota, subject to the approval of the attorney general.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.58.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Expenses.</span></h2>
          <p>All expenses incidental to the sale, printing, execution, and delivery of bonds pursuant to this section, including but not limited to actual and necessary travel and subsistence expenses of state officers and employees for these purposes, and any expenses of litigation relating to the validity of the bonds, shall be paid from the waste management account, and the amounts necessary are appropriated from that account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.58.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Debt service account.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of management and budget shall maintain in the state bond fund a separate account to be called the state waste management debt service account. It shall record receipts of premium and accrued interest, loan repayments, project revenue or other money transferred to the fund and income from the investment of the money and record any disbursements to pay the principal and interest on waste management bonds. Income from investment shall be credited to the account in each fiscal year. The amount credited shall be equal to the average return that year on all funds invested by the commissioner of management and budget, as determined by the commissioner of management and budget, times the average balance in the account that year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.58.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Debt service account; paying debt service.</span></h2>
          <p>The premium and accrued interest received on each issue of Minnesota state waste management bonds, and all payments received in repayment of loans and other revenues received, are appropriated to the debt service account. All income from the investment of the waste management account in the bond proceeds fund is appropriated to the debt service account. In order to reduce the amount of taxes otherwise required to be levied, there is also appropriated to the debt service account from any funds available in the general fund on November 1 in each year, a sum of money sufficient in amount, when added to the balance then on hand, to pay all principal and interest on Minnesota waste management bonds due and to become due before July 1 in the second ensuing year. So much of the debt service account as is necessary to pay principal and interest on waste management bonds is annually appropriated from the debt service account for the payment of principal and interest of the waste management bonds. All funds appropriated by this subdivision shall be available in the debt service account prior to any levy of the tax in any year required by the Minnesota Constitution, article XI, section 7.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.58.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Security.</span></h2>
          <p>On or before December 1 in each year, the state auditor shall levy on all taxable property within the state whatever tax may be necessary to produce an amount sufficient, with all money currently credited to the debt service account, to pay the entire amount of principal and interest currently due and the principal and interest to become due before July 1 in the second year thereafter on Minnesota waste management bonds. This tax shall be subject to no limitation of rate or amount until all the bonds and interest thereon are fully paid. The proceeds of this tax are appropriated to the debt service account. The principal of and interest on the bonds are payable from the proceeds of this tax.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.58.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.7.2.0">1980 c 564 art 7 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/301/#laws.0.110.0">1983 c 301 s 110</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.4.13.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 4 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/271/#laws.0.11.0">1989 c 271 s 11</a>-14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.5.3.0">1994 c 639 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/233/#laws.2.56.0">1995 c 233 art 2 s 56</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/187/#laws.5.13.0">1997 c 187 art 5 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.15.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 15</a>,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.59">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.59 BOND AUTHORIZATION.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of management and budget is authorized, upon request of the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency, to sell state bonds in the amount of up to $8,800,000 for the purpose of the waste processing facility capital assistance program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.54">115A.54</a>. The bonds shall be sold in the manner and upon the conditions prescribed in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.631">16A.631</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.675">16A.675</a>, and in the Minnesota Constitution, article XI, sections 4 to 7. The amount of bonds issued pursuant to this authorization shall not exceed at any time the amount needed to produce a balance in the waste management account equal to the aggregate amount of the loans and grants then approved and not previously disbursed, plus the amount of the loans and grants to be approved in the current and the following fiscal year, as estimated by the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.59.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.7.3.0">1980 c 564 art 7 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/271/#laws.0.15.0">1989 c 271 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.5.3.0">1994 c 639 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.1.30.0">2002 c 379 art 1 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">SOLID WASTE MANAGEMENT DISTRICTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.62">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.62 PURPOSE; PUBLIC INTEREST; DECLARATION OF POLICY.</h1>
        <p>The legislature finds that the development of integrated and coordinated solid waste management systems is needed to manage properly the solid waste generated in the state, to conserve and protect the natural resources in the state and the health, safety, and welfare of its citizens, and to further the state policies and purposes expressed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.02">115A.02</a>; that this need cannot always be met solely by the activities of individual political subdivisions or by agreements among subdivisions; and that therefore it is necessary to establish a procedure for the creation of solid waste management districts having the powers and performing the functions prescribed in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.62">115A.62</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.72">115A.72</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.62.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.8.1.0">1980 c 564 art 8 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/569/#laws.0.14.0">1982 c 569 s 14</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.63">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.63 SOLID WASTE MANAGEMENT DISTRICTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.63.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Legal status.</span></h2>
          <p>Solid waste management districts established pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.62">115A.62</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.72">115A.72</a> shall be public corporations and political subdivisions of the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.63.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may establish waste districts as public corporations and political subdivisions of the state, define the powers of such districts in accordance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.62">115A.62</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.72">115A.72</a>, define and alter the boundaries of the districts as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.64">115A.64</a>, and terminate districts as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.66">115A.66</a>. The commissioner shall promulgate rules pursuant to chapter 14 governing the establishment, alteration, and termination of districts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.63.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Restrictions.</span></h2>
          <p>No waste district shall be established within the boundaries of the Western Lake Superior Sanitary District established under chapter 458D. No waste district shall be established wholly within one county. The commissioner shall not establish a waste district within or extending into the metropolitan area, nor define or alter the powers or boundaries of a district, unless the articles of incorporation of the district require that the district will have the same procedural and substantive responsibilities, duties, and relationship to the metropolitan agencies as a metropolitan county. The commissioner shall require the completion of a comprehensive solid waste management plan conforming to the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.46">115A.46</a>, by petitioners seeking to establish a district.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.63.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.8.2.0">1980 c 564 art 8 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/464/#laws.1.15.0">1992 c 464 art 1 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/593/#laws.1.19.0">1992 c 593 art 1 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.5.3.0">1994 c 639 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.2.17.0">1995 c 247 art 2 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.64">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.64 PROCEDURE FOR ESTABLISHING AND ALTERING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.64.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Local petition.</span></h2>
          <p>Waste districts shall be established and their powers and boundaries defined or altered by the commissioner only after petition requesting the action jointly submitted by the governing bodies of petitioners comprising at least one-half of the counties partly or wholly within the district. A petition for alteration shall include a resolution by the board of directors of the district approving the alteration.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.64.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Petition contents.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A petition requesting establishment or alteration of a waste district must contain the information the commissioner may require, including at least the following:</p>
          <p>(1) the name of the proposed district;</p>
          <p>(2) a description of the territory and political subdivisions within and the boundaries of the proposed district or alteration thereto, along with a map showing the district or alteration;</p>
          <p>(3) resolutions of support for the district, as proposed to the commissioner, from the governing body of each of the petitioning counties;</p>
          <p>(4) a statement of the reason, necessity, and purpose for the district, plus a general description of the solid waste management improvements and facilities contemplated for the district showing how its activities will accomplish the purpose of the district and the purposes for waste resource districts stated in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.62">115A.62</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.72">115A.72</a>;</p>
          <p>(5) articles of incorporation stating:</p>
          <p>(i) the powers of the district consistent with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.62">115A.62</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.72">115A.72</a>, including a statement of powers proposed pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.70">115A.70</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.71">115A.71</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.715">115A.715</a>; and</p>
          <p>(ii) provisions for representation and election of the board of directors of the district.</p>
          <p>(b) After the petition has been filed, no petitioner may withdraw from it except with the written consent of all other petitioners for the district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.64.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Local review and comment.</span></h2>
          <p>At least 60 days before submitting the petition to the commissioner, the petitioners shall publish notice of the petition in newspapers of general circulation in the proposed district and shall cause a copy of the petition to be served upon the agency, the governing body of each political subdivision which is wholly or partly within the proposed district or is affected by the proposed alteration and each regional development commission affected by the proposed district or alteration. Each entity receiving service shall have 60 days within which to comment to the petitioners on the petition and the proposed district or alteration. Proof of service, along with any comments received, shall be attached to the petition when it is submitted to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.64.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Review procedures.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon receipt of the petition, the commissioner shall determine whether the petition conforms in form and substance to the requirements of law and rule. If the petition does not conform to the requirements, the commissioner shall return it immediately to the petitioners with a statement describing the deficiencies and the amendments necessary to rectify them. If the petition does conform to the requirements, and if comments have been received objecting to the establishment or alteration of the district as proposed, the commissioner shall request the Office of Administrative Hearings to conduct a hearing on the petition. The hearing shall be conducted in the proposed district in the manner provided in chapter 14 for contested cases. If no comments have been received objecting to the establishment of the district as proposed, the commissioner may proceed to grant or deny the petition without the necessity of conducting a contested case hearing. If the petition conforms to the requirements of law and rule, the commissioner shall also immediately submit the petition to the solid waste and the technical advisory councils for review and recommendation and shall prepare a report containing recommendations on the disposition of the petition. The commissioner's report shall contain at least the commissioner's findings and conclusions on whether the proposed boundaries, purposes, powers, and management plans of the district or alteration thereto serve the purposes of waste resource districts, are appropriately related to the waste generation, collection, processing, and disposal patterns in the area, and are generally consistent with the purposes of the agency's regulatory program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.64.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Corrections allowed.</span></h2>
          <p>No petition submitted by the requisite number of counties shall be void or dismissed on account of defects exposed in the hearing documents or report. The commissioner shall permit the petition to be amended in form and substance to conform to the facts by correcting any errors in the description of the territory or any other defects.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.64.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Order.</span></h2>
          <p>After considering the reports of the administrative law judge, if a contested case hearing has been held, and the recommendations of the advisory councils, the commissioner shall make a final decision on the petition. If the commissioner finds and determines that the establishment or alteration of a district as proposed in the petition would not be in the public interest and would not serve the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.62">115A.62</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.72">115A.72</a>, the commissioner shall give notice to the petitioners of intent to deny the petition. If a contested case hearing has not been held, the petitioners may request a hearing within 30 days of the notice of intent to deny the petition. The request shall be granted. Following the hearing and the report of the administrative law judge, the commissioner shall make a final decision on the petition and mail a copy of the decision to the governing body of each affected political subdivision. If the commissioner finds and determines that the establishment or alteration of a district as proposed in the petition would be in the public interest and would serve the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.62">115A.62</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.72">115A.72</a>, the commissioner shall, by order, establish the district, define its boundaries, and give it a corporate name by which, in all proceedings, it shall thereafter be known. The order shall include articles of incorporation stating the powers of the district and the location of its registered office. Upon the filing of a certified copy of the order of the commissioner with the secretary of state, the district shall become a political subdivision of the state and a public corporation, with the authority, power, and duties prescribed in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.62">115A.62</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.72">115A.72</a> and the order of the commissioner. At the time of filing, a copy of the order shall be mailed by the commissioner to the governing body of each political subdivision wholly or partly within the district or affected by the alteration of the district.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.64.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.8.3.0">1980 c 564 art 8 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/615/#laws.0.60.0">1980 c 615 s 60</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/640/#laws.0.32.0">1984 c 640 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/186/#laws.0.15.0">1987 c 186 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.27.0">1991 c 337 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.5.3.0">1994 c 639 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.65">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.65 PERPETUAL EXISTENCE.</h1>
        <p>A waste district created under the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.62">115A.62</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.72">115A.72</a> shall have perpetual existence to the extent necessary to perform all acts necessary and proper for carrying out and exercising the powers and duties expressly given in it. A district shall not be terminated except pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.66">115A.66</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.65.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.8.4.0">1980 c 564 art 8 s 4</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.66">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.66 TERMINATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.66.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Petition.</span></h2>
          <p>Proceedings for the termination of a district shall be initiated by the filing of a petition with the commissioner. The petition shall be submitted by the governing bodies of not less than one-half of the counties which are wholly or partly in the district. The petition shall state that the existence of the district is no longer in the public interest. The petitioners shall publish notice of the petition in newspapers of general circulation in the district and shall cause to be served upon each political subdivision wholly or partly within the district a copy of the petition, and proof of service shall be attached to the petition filed with the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.66.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Bond; payment of costs.</span></h2>
          <p>If the petition is dismissed or denied, the petitioners shall be required to pay all costs and expenses of the proceeding for termination. At the time of filing the petition, a bond shall be filed by the petitioners with the commissioner in such sum as the commissioner determines to be necessary to ensure payment of costs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.66.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Hearing; decision.</span></h2>
          <p>If objection is made to the commissioner against the petition for termination, a contested case hearing on the petition shall be held in the waste district pursuant to chapter 14. If the commissioner determines that the termination of the district as proposed in the petition would not be in the public interest, the commissioner shall give notice to the petitioner of intent to deny the petition. If a contested case hearing has not been held, the petitioner may request a hearing within 30 days of the notice of intent to deny the petition. The request shall be granted. Following the hearing and the report of the administrative law judge, the commissioner shall make a final decision on the petition. If the petition is dismissed, all costs of the proceeding shall be assessed against the petitioner. If the commissioner determines that the existence of the district is no longer in the public interest, the commissioner shall by findings and order terminate the district. Upon the filing of a certified copy of the findings and order with the secretary of state, the district shall cease to be a public corporation and a political subdivision of the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.66.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Limitation.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall not entertain a petition for termination of a district within five years from the date of the formation of the district nor shall the commissioner entertain a petition for termination of the same district more often than once in five years.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.66.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.8.5.0">1980 c 564 art 8 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/640/#laws.0.32.0">1984 c 640 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.5.3.0">1994 c 639 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.67">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.67 ORGANIZATION OF DISTRICT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.67.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Chair; board.</span></h2>
          <p>The chair shall be elected from outside the board of directors by majority vote of the board of directors. The first chair shall serve for a term of two years. Members of the board of directors shall be residents of the district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.67.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">First meeting.</span></h2>
          <p>The first meeting of the board of directors shall be held at the call of the chair, after notice, for the purpose of proposing the bylaws, electing officers and for any other business that comes before the meeting. The bylaws of the district, and amendments thereto, shall be adopted by a majority vote of the board of directors unless the certificate of incorporation requires a greater vote.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.67.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Bylaws.</span></h2>
          <p>The bylaws shall state:</p>
          <p>(1) the manner and time of calling regular meetings of the representatives and the board of directors, not less than once annually;</p>
          <p>(2) the title, manner of selection, and term of office of officers of the district;</p>
          <p>(3) the term of office of members of the board of directors, the manner of their removal, and the manner of filling vacancies on the board of directors;</p>
          <p>(4) the powers and duties of the board of directors consistent with the order and articles of incorporation establishing the district;</p>
          <p>(5) the definition of a quorum for meetings of the board of directors, which shall be not less than a majority of the members;</p>
          <p>(6) the compensation and reimbursement for expenses for members of the board of directors, which shall not exceed that provided for in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0575#stat.15.0575.3">15.0575, subdivision 3</a>; and</p>
          <p>(7) such other provisions for regulating the affairs of the district as the board of directors shall determine to be necessary.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.67.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.8.6.0">1980 c 564 art 8 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/373/#laws.0.39.0">1983 c 373 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.28.0">1991 c 337 s 28</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.68">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.68 REGISTERED OFFICE.</h1>
        <p>Every district shall maintain an office in this state to be known as its registered office. When a district desires to change the location of its registered office, it shall file with the secretary of state and the commissioner of the agency, a certificate stating the new location by city, town, or other community and the effective date of change. When the certificate has been duly filed, the board of directors may make the change without any further action.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.68.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.8.7.0">1980 c 564 art 8 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/186/#laws.0.15.0">1987 c 186 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.69">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.69 POWERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.69.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General.</span></h2>
          <p>A district shall have all powers necessary or convenient to perform its duties, including the powers provided in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.69.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Actions.</span></h2>
          <p>The district may sue and be sued, and shall be a public body within the meaning of chapter 562.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.69.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Acquiring property.</span></h2>
          <p>The district may acquire by purchase, lease, condemnation, gift, or grant, any right, title, and interest in and to real or personal property deemed necessary for the exercise of its powers or the accomplishment of its purposes, including positive and negative easements and water and air rights. Any local government unit and the commissioners of transportation, natural resources, and administration may convey to or permit the use of any property or facilities by the district, subject to the rights of the holders of any bonds issued with respect thereto, with or without compensation and without an election or approval by any other government agency. The district may hold the property for its purposes, and may lease or rent the property so far as not needed for its purposes, upon the terms and in the manner as it deems advisable. The right to acquire lands and property rights by condemnation shall be exercised in accordance with chapter 117. The district may take possession of any property for which condemnation proceedings have been commenced at any time after the issuance of a court order appointing commissioners for its condemnation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.69.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Right of entry.</span></h2>
          <p>Whenever the district deems it necessary to the accomplishment of its purposes, the district or any member, employee, or agent thereof, when authorized by it, may enter upon any property, public or private, for the purpose of obtaining information or conducting surveys or investigations, provided that the entrance and activity is undertaken after reasonable notice and during normal business hours and provided that compensation is made for any damage to the property caused by the entrance and activity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.69.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Gifts and grants.</span></h2>
          <p>The district may apply for and accept gifts, loans, or other property from the United States, the state, or any person for any of its purposes, may enter into any agreement required in connection therewith, and may hold, use, and dispose of the money or property in accordance with the terms of the gift, grant, loan or agreement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.69.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Property exempt from taxation.</span></h2>
          <p>Any real or personal property owned, used, or occupied by the district for any authorized purpose is declared to be acquired, owned, used and occupied for public and governmental purposes, and shall be exempted from taxation by the state or any political subdivision of the state, except to the extent that the property is subject to the sales and use tax under chapter 297A, provided that those properties shall be subject to special assessments levied by a political subdivision for a local improvement in amounts proportionate to and not exceeding the special benefit received by the properties from the improvement. No possible use of the properties in any manner different from their use for solid waste management at the time shall be considered in determining the special benefit received by the properties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.69.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Facilities and services.</span></h2>
          <p>The district may construct, equip, develop, enlarge, improve, and operate solid waste facilities and services as it deems necessary and may negotiate contracts for the use of public or private facilities and services. The district shall contract with private persons for the construction, maintenance, and operation of facilities and services where the facilities and services are adequate and available for use and competitive with other means of providing the same service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.69.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Rates; charges.</span></h2>
          <p>The district may establish and collect rates and charges for the facilities and services provided by the district and may negotiate and collect rates and charges for facilities and services contracted for by the district. The board of directors of the district may agree with the holders of district obligations which are secured by revenues of the district as to the maximum or minimum amounts which the district shall charge and collect for services provided by the district. Before establishing or raising any rates and charges, the board of directors shall hold a public hearing regarding the proposed rates and charges. Notice of the hearing shall be published at least once in a legal newspaper of general circulation throughout the area affected by the rates and charges. Publication shall be no more than 45 days and no less than 15 days prior to the date of the hearing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.69.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Disposition of property.</span></h2>
          <p>The district may sell or otherwise dispose of any real or personal property acquired by it which is no longer required for accomplishment of its purposes. The property shall be sold in the manner provided by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/469.065">469.065</a>, insofar as practical. The district shall give notice of sale which it deems appropriate. When the district determines that any property which has been acquired from a government unit without compensation is no longer required, the district shall transfer it to the government unit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.69.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Disposition of products and energy.</span></h2>
          <p>The district may use, sell, or otherwise dispose of all of the products and energy produced by its facilities. Section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.345">471.345</a> shall not apply to the sale of products and energy. The district shall give particular consideration to the needs of purchasers in this state and shall actively promote sales to such purchasers so long as this can be done at prices and under conditions that meet constitutional requirements and that are consistent with the district's object of being financially self supporting to the greatest extent possible.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.69.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Contracts.</span></h2>
          <p>The district may enter into any contract necessary or proper for the exercise of its powers or the accomplishment of its purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.69.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Joint powers.</span></h2>
          <p>The district may act under the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a>, or any other law providing for joint or cooperative action between government units.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.69.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Research.</span></h2>
          <p>The district may conduct research studies and programs, collect and analyze data, prepare reports, maps, charts, and tables, and conduct all necessary hearings and investigations in connection with its work and may advise and assist other government units on planning matters within the scope of its powers, duties, and objectives.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.69.14">
