        <p>(c) A person may place waste motor vehicle antifreeze in a wastewater collection or treatment system permitted by the agency, unless prohibited by the operator of the system, if the person:</p>
        <p>(1) generates an annual average of less than 50 gallons per month of waste motor vehicle antifreeze; and</p>
        <p>(2) keeps records of the amount of waste antifreeze generated. Records must be maintained on site and made available for inspection for a minimum of three years following generation of the waste antifreeze.</p>
        <p>(d) Notwithstanding paragraph (a), motor oil filters and portions of motor oil filters may be processed at a permitted mixed municipal solid waste resource recovery facility that directly burns the waste if:</p>
        <p>(1) the facility is subject to an industrial waste management plan that addresses management of motor oil filters and the owner or operator of the facility can demonstrate to the satisfaction of the commissioner that the facility is in compliance with that plan;</p>
        <p>(2) the facility recovers ferrous metal after incineration for recycling as part of its operation; and</p>
        <p>(3) the motor oil filters are collected separately from mixed municipal solid waste and are not combined with it except for the purpose of incinerating the waste.</p>
        <p>(e) The commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency, in conjunction with industry organizations representing automotive repair businesses and antifreeze recycling businesses and environmental organizations shall work together to develop and promote opportunities to recycle waste motor vehicle antifreeze and to review the impact of alternative antifreeze disposal or recycling methods on businesses and the environment.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.916.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/348/#laws.0.25.0">1987 c 348 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/685/#laws.0.17.0">1988 c 685 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/347/#laws.1.18.0">1991 c 347 art 1 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/249/#laws.0.16.0">1993 c 249 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.19.0">1994 c 585 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.9.0">1996 c 470 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.97.0">1997 c 216 s 97</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/379/#laws.0.2.0">1998 c 379 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/109/#laws.0.14.0">2009 c 109 s 14</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.9162"><b>115A.9162</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.28.0">1996 c 470 s 28</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">NEW DISPOSAL FACILITIES; CERTIFICATE OF NEED</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.917">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.917 CERTIFICATE OF NEED.</h1>
        <p>No new capacity for disposal of mixed municipal solid waste may be permitted in counties outside the metropolitan area without a certificate of need issued by the commissioner indicating the commissioner's determination that the additional disposal capacity is needed in the county. A certificate of need may not be issued until the county has a plan approved under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.46">115A.46</a>. If the original plan was approved more than five years before, the commissioner may require the plan to be revised before a certificate of need is issued under this section. The commissioner shall certify need only to the extent that there are no feasible and prudent alternatives to the additional disposal capacity, including waste reduction, source separation, and resource recovery, that would minimize adverse impact upon natural resources. Alternatives that are speculative or conjectural are not feasible and prudent. Economic considerations alone do not justify the certification of need or the rejection of alternatives.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.917.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/644/#laws.0.45.0">1984 c 644 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.145.0">1987 c 404 s 145</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.5.3.0">1994 c 639 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">DISPOSAL FACILITIES; LOCAL FEE AUTHORITY</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.918">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.918 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.918.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to this section and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.919">115A.919</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.929">115A.929</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.918.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Closure.</span></h2>
          <p>"Closure" means actions that will prevent, mitigate, or minimize the threat to public health and the environment posed by a closed solid waste disposal facility including application of final cover; grading and seeding of final cover; installation of an adequate monitoring system, if necessary; and construction of groundwater and surface water diversion structures.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.918.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Equivalent.</span></h2>
          <p>For mixed municipal solid waste, the measure of "equivalent" or "equivalent cubic yards of waste" is <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.33">3.33</a> cubic yards per ton of waste.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.918.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Operator.</span></h2>
          <p>"Operator" means:</p>
          <p>(1) the permittee of a mixed municipal solid waste disposal facility that has an agency permit; or</p>
          <p>(2) the person in control of a mixed municipal solid waste disposal facility that does not have an agency permit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.918.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Postclosure, postclosure care.</span></h2>
          <p>"Postclosure" and "postclosure care" mean actions taken for the care, maintenance, and monitoring of a solid waste disposal facility after closure that will prevent, mitigate, or minimize the threat to public health and environment posed by the closed facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.918.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Response.</span></h2>
          <p>"Response" has the meaning given it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.02#stat.115B.02.18">115B.02, subdivision 18</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.918.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/274/#laws.0.11.0">1985 c 274 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.20.0">1994 c 585 s 20</a>,21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/25/#laws.0.35.0">2023 c 25 s 35</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.919">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.919 COUNTY FEE AUTHORITY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.919.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Fee.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A county may impose a fee, by cubic yard of waste or its equivalent, on operators of facilities for the disposal of mixed municipal solid waste or construction debris located within the county. The revenue from the fees shall be credited to the county general fund and shall be used only for landfill abatement purposes, or costs of closure, postclosure care, and response actions or for purposes of mitigating and compensating for the local risks, costs, and other adverse effects of facilities. The interest generated from fees imposed under this subdivision may be credited to the county general fund for use by a county for other purposes.</p>
          <p>(b) Fees for construction debris facilities may not exceed 50 cents per cubic yard. Revenues from the fees must offset any financial assurances required by the county for a construction debris facility. The maximum revenue that may be collected for a construction debris facility must be determined by multiplying the total permitted capacity of the facility by 15 cents per cubic yard. Once the maximum revenue has been collected for a facility, the fee may no longer be imposed. The limitation on the fees in this paragraph and in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.921#stat.115A.921.2">115A.921, subdivision 2</a>, are not intended to alter the liability of the facility operator or the authority of the agency to impose financial assurance requirements.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.919.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Additional fee.</span></h2>
          <p>A county may impose a fee, by cubic yard or the equivalent of waste collected outside the county, in addition to a fee imposed under subdivision 1, on operators of mixed municipal solid waste disposal facilities located within the county. The fee may not exceed $7.50 per cubic yard or the equivalent. A person licensed to collect solid waste in a county that designates the waste under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.80">115A.80</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.893">115A.893</a> who is referred to a disposal facility outside the county due to temporary closure of the designated facility is exempt from the additional fee; the designated facility is responsible for the fee. Revenue generated from the additional fee must be credited to the county general fund and may be used only for the purposes listed in subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.919.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Joint powers agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>If a facility is owned by a joint powers board, total fees in excess of $1 per cubic yard or equivalent may not be imposed or revenue expended under subdivision 1 or 2 without the approval of the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.919.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Exemptions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Waste residue from recycling facilities at which recyclable materials are separated or processed for the purpose of recycling, or from energy and resource recovery facilities at which solid waste is processed for the purpose of extracting, reducing, converting to energy, or otherwise separating and preparing solid waste for reuse shall be exempt from any fee imposed by a county under this section if there is at least an 85 percent weight reduction in the solid waste processed. Before any fee is reduced, the verification procedures of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.843#stat.473.843.1">473.843, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (c), must be followed and submitted to the appropriate county, except that for facilities operating outside of the metropolitan area the commissioner shall prescribe procedures for verifying the required 85 percent weight reduction.</p>
          <p>(b) A facility permitted for the disposal of construction debris is exempt from 25 percent of a fee imposed under subdivision 1 if the facility has implemented a recycling program approved by the county and 25 percent if the facility contains a liner and leachate collection system approved by the agency.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.919.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/644/#laws.0.46.0">1984 c 644 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/274/#laws.0.12.0">1985 c 274 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/685/#laws.0.19.0">1988 c 685 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/325/#laws.0.18.0">1989 c 325 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.37.0">1991 c 337 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.22.0">1994 c 585 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.1.21.0">1995 c 247 art 1 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.10.0">1996 c 470 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/471/#laws.13.3.0">1996 c 471 art 13 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.128.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 128</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.921">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.921 CITY OR TOWN; FEE AUTHORITY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.921.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Mixed municipal solid waste.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A city or town may impose a fee, not to exceed $1 per cubic yard of waste, or its equivalent, on operators of facilities for the disposal of mixed municipal solid waste located within the city or town. The revenue from the fees must be credited to the city or town general fund. Revenue produced by 25 cents of the fee must be used only for purposes of landfill abatement or for purposes of mitigating and compensating for the local risks, costs, and other adverse effects of facilities. Revenue produced by the balance of the fee may be used for any general fund purpose.</p>
          <p>(b) Waste residue from recycling facilities at which recyclable materials are separated or processed for the purpose of recycling, or from energy and resource recovery facilities at which solid waste is processed for the purpose of extracting, reducing, converting to energy, or otherwise separating and preparing solid waste for reuse shall be exempt from the fee imposed by a city or town under this section if there is at least an 85 percent weight reduction in the solid waste processed. Before any fee is reduced, the verification procedures of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.843#stat.473.843.1">473.843, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (c), must be followed and submitted to the appropriate city or town, except that for facilities operating outside of the metropolitan area the commissioner shall prescribe procedures for verifying the required 85 percent weight reduction.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.921.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Construction debris.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A city or town may impose a fee, not to exceed 50 cents per cubic yard of waste, or its equivalent, on operators of facilities for the disposal of construction debris located within the city or town. The revenue from the fees must be credited to the city or town general fund. Two-thirds of the revenue must be used only for purposes of landfill abatement or for purposes of mitigating and compensating for the local risks, costs, and other adverse effects resulting from the facilities.</p>
          <p>(b) A facility permitted for the disposal of construction debris is exempt from 25 percent of a fee imposed under this subdivision if the facility has implemented a recycling program that has been approved by the county and 25 percent if the facility contains a liner and leachate collection system approved by the agency.</p>
          <p>(c) Two-thirds of the revenue from fees collected under this subdivision must offset any financial assurances required by the city or town for a construction debris facility.</p>
          <p>(d) The maximum revenue that may be collected under this subdivision must be determined by multiplying the total permitted capacity of a facility by 15 cents per cubic yard. Once the maximum revenue has been collected for a facility, the fees in this subdivision may no longer be imposed.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.921.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/644/#laws.0.47.0">1984 c 644 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/348/#laws.0.26.0">1987 c 348 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/685/#laws.0.20.0">1988 c 685 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/325/#laws.0.19.0">1989 c 325 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.38.0">1991 c 337 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.23.0">1994 c 585 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.1.22.0">1995 c 247 art 1 s 22</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.922"><b>115A.922</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/604/#laws.10.32.0">1990 c 604 art 10 s 32</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.923">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.923 GREATER MINNESOTA; LANDFILL CLEANUP FEE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.923.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Amount of fee.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The operator of a mixed municipal solid waste disposal facility outside of the metropolitan area shall charge a fee on solid waste accepted and disposed of at the facility as follows:</p>
          <p>(1) a facility that weighs the waste that it accepts must charge a fee of $2 per cubic yard based on equivalent cubic yards of waste accepted at the entrance of the facility;</p>
          <p>(2) a facility that does not weigh the waste but that measures the volume of the waste that it accepts must charge a fee of $2 per cubic yard of waste accepted at the entrance of the facility; and</p>
          <p>(3) waste residue from recycling facilities at which recyclable materials are separated or processed for the purpose of recycling, or from energy and resource recovery facilities at which solid waste is processed for the purpose of extracting, reducing, converting to energy, or otherwise separating and preparing solid waste for reuse is exempt from the fee imposed by this subdivision if there is at least an 85 percent weight reduction in the solid waste processed.</p>
          <p>(b) To qualify for exemption under paragraph (a), clause (3), waste residue must be brought to a disposal facility separately. The commissioner shall prescribe procedures for determining the amount of waste residue qualifying for exemption.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.923.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Disposition of fee.</span></h2>
          <p>The operator of a disposal facility in greater Minnesota shall remit the fees collected under subdivision 1 to the county or sanitary district where the facility is located, except that the operator of a facility that is owned by a statutory or home rule city shall remit the fees to the city that owns the facility and the operator of a facility that is owned by a joint powers board shall remit the fees to the board. The county, city, joint powers board, or sanitary district may use the revenue from the fees only for the purposes specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.919">115A.919</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.923.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/604/#laws.10.32.0">1990 c 604 art 10 s 32</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.923.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/604/#laws.10.32.0">1990 c 604 art 10 s 32</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.923.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/604/#laws.10.32.0">1990 c 604 art 10 s 32</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.923.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/604/#laws.10.32.0">1990 c 604 art 10 s 32</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.923.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/416/#laws.4.5.0">1994 c 416 art 4 s 5</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.923.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/325/#laws.0.21.0">1989 c 325 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/604/#laws.10.26.0">1990 c 604 art 10 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.39.0">1991 c 337 s 39</a>,40; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/416/#laws.4.1.0">1994 c 416 art 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.1.23.0">1995 c 247 art 1 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.11.0">1996 c 470 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/471/#laws.13.4.0">1996 c 471 art 13 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.924"><b>115A.924</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/604/#laws.10.32.0">1990 c 604 art 10 s 32</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.925"><b>115A.925</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/604/#laws.10.32.0">1990 c 604 art 10 s 32</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.927"><b>115A.927</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/604/#laws.10.32.0">1990 c 604 art 10 s 32</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.928"><b>115A.928</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/604/#laws.10.32.0">1990 c 604 art 10 s 32</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.929">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.929 FEES; ACCOUNTING.</h1>
        <p>Each political subdivision that provides for solid waste management shall account for all revenue collected from waste management fees, together with interest earned on revenue from the fees, separately from other revenue collected by the political subdivision and shall report revenue collected from the fees and use of the revenue separately from other revenue and use of revenue in any required financial report or audit. For the purposes of this section, "waste management fees" means:</p>
        <p>(1) all fees, charges, and surcharges collected under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.919">115A.919</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.921">115A.921</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.923">115A.923</a>;</p>
        <p>(2) all tipping fees collected at waste management facilities owned or operated by the political subdivision;</p>
        <p>(3) all charges imposed by the political subdivision for waste collection and management services; and</p>
        <p>(4) any other fees, charges, or surcharges imposed on waste or for the purpose of waste management, whether collected directly from generators or indirectly through property taxes or as part of utility or other charges for services provided by the political subdivision.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.929.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.41.0">1991 c 337 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/249/#laws.0.17.0">1993 c 249 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.24.0">1994 c 585 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/1/#laws.2.63.0">1Sp2003 c 1 art 2 s 63</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.131.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 131</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">SOLID WASTE COLLECTION; REQUIREMENTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.93">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.93 LICENSING; SOLID WASTE COLLECTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.93.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">License and registration required; reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person may not collect mixed municipal solid waste for hire without a license from the jurisdiction where the mixed municipal solid waste is collected. The local licensing entity shall submit a list of licensed collectors to the agency.</p>
          <p>(b) A person may not collect recyclable materials for hire unless registered with the agency. If a person is licensed under paragraph (a), the person need not register with the agency under this paragraph.</p>
          <p>(c) The agency, in consultation with the Solid Waste Management Coordinating Board, the Association of Minnesota Counties, the Minnesota Solid Waste Administrators Association, and representatives from the waste industry, shall, by July 1, 2016, develop uniform short and long reporting forms that will reduce duplicative reporting to governmental units by collectors of solid waste and recyclable materials.</p>
          <p>(d) A collector of mixed municipal solid waste or recyclable materials shall separately report to the agency on an annual basis information including, but not limited to, the quantity of mixed municipal solid waste and the quantity of recyclable materials collected:</p>
          <p>(1) from commercial customers;</p>
          <p>(2) from residential customers;</p>
          <p>(3) by county of origin; and</p>
          <p>(4) by destination of the material.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.93.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Local licensing.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Each city and town may issue licenses for persons to collect mixed municipal solid waste for hire within their jurisdictions.</p>
          <p>(b) County boards shall by resolution adopt the licensing authority of a city or town that does not issue licenses. A county may delegate its licensing authority to a consortium of counties or to municipalities to license collection of mixed municipal solid waste within the county.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.93.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">License requirements; pricing based on volume or weight.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A licensing authority shall require licensees to impose charges for collection of mixed municipal solid waste that increase with the volume or weight of the waste collected.</p>
          <p>(b) A licensing authority may impose requirements that are consistent with the county's solid waste policies as a condition of receiving and maintaining a license.</p>
          <p>(c) A licensing authority shall prohibit mixed municipal solid waste collectors from imposing a greater charge on residents who recycle than on residents who do not recycle.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner may exempt a licensing authority from the requirements of paragraph (a) if the county within which the authority is located has an approved solid waste management plan that concludes that variable rate pricing is not appropriate for that jurisdiction because it is inconsistent with other incentives and mechanisms implemented within the jurisdiction that are more effective in attaining the goals of this chapter to discourage on-site disposal, littering, and illegal dumping.</p>
          <p>(e) In the interim between revisions to the county solid waste management plan, the commissioner may exempt a licensing authority from the requirements of paragraph (a) if the commissioner makes the determination otherwise made by the plan in paragraph (d) and finds that the licensing authority:</p>
          <p>(1) operates or contracts for the operation of a residential recycling program that collects more categories of recyclable materials than required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.552">115A.552</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) has a residential participation rate in its recycling programs of at least 70 percent or in excess of the participation rate for the county in which it is located, whichever is greater; and</p>
          <p>(3) is located in a county that has exceeded the recycling goals in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.551">115A.551</a>.</p>
          <p class="bl">An exemption granted by the commissioner in the interim between revisions to the county solid waste management plan is only effective until the county solid waste management plan is revised.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.93.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Volume requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>A licensing authority that requires a pricing system based on volume instead of weight under subdivision 3 shall determine a base unit size for an average small quantity household generator and establish, or require the licensee to establish, a multiple unit pricing system that ensures that amounts of waste generated in excess of the base unit amount are priced higher than the base unit price.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.93.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Date certain.</span></h2>
          <p>By January 1, 1993, each county shall ensure that each city or town within the county requires each mixed municipal solid waste collector that provides curbside collection service in the city or town to obtain a license under this section or the county shall directly require and issue the licenses. No person may collect mixed municipal solid waste after January 1, 1993, without a license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.93.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Customer data.</span></h2>
          <p>Customer lists provided to counties or cities by solid waste collectors are private data on individuals as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02#stat.13.02.12">13.02, subdivision 12</a>, with regard to data on individuals, or nonpublic data as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02#stat.13.02.9">13.02, subdivision 9</a>, with regard to data not on individuals.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.93.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.20.8.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 20 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.42.0">1991 c 337 s 42</a>,43; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/593/#laws.1.25.0">1992 c 593 art 1 s 25</a>,26; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/351/#laws.0.23.0">1993 c 351 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.12.0">1996 c 470 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.111.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 111</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.9301">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.9301 SOLID WASTE COLLECTION; VOLUME- OR WEIGHT-BASED PRICING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.9301.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>A local government unit that collects charges for solid waste collection directly from waste generators shall implement charges that increase as the volume or weight of the waste collected on site from each generator's residence or place of business increases.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.9301.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Volume requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>If a local government unit implements a pricing system based on volume instead of weight under subdivision 1, it shall determine a base unit size for an average small quantity household generator and establish a multiple unit pricing system that ensures that amounts of waste generated in excess of the base unit amount are priced higher than the base unit price.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.9301.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Alternative.</span></h2>
          <p>A local government unit may satisfy the requirements of this section by establishing at least three price categories for collection of household mixed municipal solid waste to include, for households that generate small volumes of waste, a waste collection unit that is smaller than and priced lower than for other generators if the local government unit:</p>
          <p>(1) operates or contracts for the operation of a residential recycling program that collects more categories of recyclable materials than required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.552">115A.552</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) has a residential participation rate in its recycling programs of at least 70 percent or in excess of the participation rate for the county in which it is located, whichever is greater;</p>
          <p>(3) is located in a county that has exceeded the recycling goals in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.551">115A.551</a>; and</p>
          <p>(4) generates, by all waste generators in the city, an amount of mixed municipal solid waste that is managed by incineration, production of refuse-derived fuel, mixed municipal solid waste composting, or disposal that is no greater, in proportion to the total amount of waste managed as listed above by all waste generators in the county in which the city is located, than it was for calendar year 1993.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.9301.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Exemption.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may exempt a local government unit from the requirements of subdivision 1 if the county within which the local government unit is located has an approved solid waste management plan that concludes that variable rate pricing is not appropriate for that jurisdiction because it is inconsistent with other incentives and mechanisms implemented within the jurisdiction that are more effective in attaining the goals of this chapter to discourage on-site disposal, littering, and illegal dumping.</p>
          <p>(b) In the interim between revisions to the county solid waste management plan, the commissioner may exempt a local government unit from the requirements of subdivision 1 if the commissioner makes the determination otherwise made by the plan in paragraph (a) and finds that the local government unit:</p>
          <p>(1) operates or contracts for the operation of a residential recycling program that collects more categories of recyclable materials than required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.552">115A.552</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) has a residential participation rate in its recycling programs of at least 70 percent or in excess of the participation rate for the county in which it is located, whichever is greater; and</p>
          <p>(3) is located in a county that has exceeded the recycling goals in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.551">115A.551</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) An exemption granted by the commissioner in the interim between revisions to the county solid waste management plan is only effective until the county solid waste management plan is revised.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.9301.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/593/#laws.1.27.0">1992 c 593 art 1 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.25.0">1994 c 585 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.13.0">1996 c 470 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.9302">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.9302 WASTE DEPOSIT; DISCLOSURE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.9302.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Disclosure required.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) By January 1, 1994, and at least annually thereafter between January 1 and March 31, a person that collects construction debris, industrial waste, or mixed municipal solid waste for transportation to a waste facility shall disclose to each waste generator from whom waste is collected the name, location, and type of, and the number of the permit issued by the agency, or its counterpart in another state, if applicable, for the processing or disposal facility or facilities, excluding a transfer station, at which the waste will be deposited. The collector shall note the approximate percentage of waste deposited at each of the two primary facilities used for the type of waste collected from the generator in the county in which the generator generates the waste and any alternative facilities regularly used by the collector for the type of waste collected from the generator in the county in which the generator generates the waste.</p>
          <p>(b) All written disclosures must include the following statement:</p>
          <p>"You may be responsible for any liability that results from contamination at a facility where your waste has been deposited. Minnesota believes that its waste management system provides substantially more financial and environmental protection than depositing waste in landfills in other states. Managing your waste in Minnesota may minimize your potential liability."</p>
          <p>All oral disclosures must include the following statement:</p>
          <p>"You may be responsible for any liability that results from contamination at a facility where your waste has been deposited. Minnesota believes that its waste management system offers more protection from liability than the waste management systems of other states."</p>
          <p>(c) If any of the primary or alternative disposal facilities identified by the collector in paragraph (a) are not located in Minnesota, the disclosure must state "The landfill to which your waste may be sent during the current calendar year is not a Minnesota landfill."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.9302.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Form of disclosure.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A collector shall make the disclosure to the waste generator in writing at least once per year between January 1 and March 31 and on any written contract for collection services for that year. The written disclosure must include all of the information described in subdivision 1. The oral disclosure required in this section need only include the statement required in subdivision 1, paragraph (b), and the statement required in subdivision 1, paragraph (c), if that paragraph applies. If the license issued by the county to the collector for collection within the county does not require the collector to submit a copy of the disclosure to the county, the collector shall submit a copy to the commissioner by March 31 of each year.</p>
          <p>(b) An oral disclosure is only required with regard to the collection of mixed municipal solid waste. A collector must provide the required disclosure orally to a waste generator at the time the generator agrees to purchase regular collection service and must provide written disclosure to the generator within 45 days from the date of request. This oral disclosure is not required if the city or county within which the waste is generated selects the collector that may provide collection services to the generator.</p>
          <p>(c) If a collector provides onetime or occasional service to a waste generator, the collector must orally provide the generator with the required disclosure at the time the generator agrees to purchase the service. The collector shall then provide written disclosure to the generator within 45 days from the date of request.</p>
          <p>(d) If an additional facility becomes either a primary facility or an alternative facility during the year, the collector shall make the disclosure set forth in subdivision 1 within 30 days. A local government unit that collects solid waste without direct charges to waste generators shall make the disclosure on any statement that includes an amount for waste management, provided that, at a minimum, disclosure to waste generators must be made at least twice annually in a form likely to be available to all generators.</p>
          <p>(e) The agency may develop standard disclosure forms containing the information that is required in this section. Collectors may use the form developed by the agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.9302.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Transfer stations.</span></h2>
          <p>If the collector deposits waste at a transfer station, the collector need not disclose the name and location of the transfer station but must disclose the destination of the waste when it leaves the transfer station.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.9302.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/249/#laws.0.18.0">1993 c 249 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.1.24.0">1995 c 247 art 1 s 24</a>,25</p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">SPECIFIC SOLID WASTE MATERIALS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.931">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.931 YARD WASTE; PROHIBITION.</h1>
        <p>(a) Except as authorized by the agency, in the metropolitan area after January 1, 1990, and outside the metropolitan area after January 1, 1992, a person may not place yard waste:</p>
        <p>(1) in mixed municipal solid waste;</p>
        <p>(2) in a disposal facility; or</p>
        <p>(3) in a resource recovery facility except for the purposes of reuse, composting, or cocomposting.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) MS 2008 [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.03">115A.03, subd 38</a>]</p>
        <p>(c) On or after January 1, 2010, a person may not place yard waste or source-separated compostable materials generated in a metropolitan county in a plastic bag delivered to a transfer station or compost facility unless the bag meets all the specifications in ASTM Standard Specification for Compostable Plastics (D6400). For purposes of this paragraph, "metropolitan county" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121#stat.473.121.4">473.121, subdivision 4</a>, and "ASTM" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/296A.01#stat.296A.01.6">296A.01, subdivision 6</a>.</p>
        <p>(d) A person who immediately empties a plastic bag containing yard waste or source-separated compostable materials delivered to a transfer station or compost facility and removes the plastic bag from the transfer station or compost facility is exempt from paragraph (c).</p>
        <p>(e) Residents of a city of the first class that currently contracts for the collection of yard waste are exempt from paragraph (c) until January 1, 2013, if, by that date, the city implements a citywide source-separated compostable materials collection program using durable carts.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.931.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/685/#laws.0.21.0">1988 c 685 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.44.0">1991 c 337 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/593/#laws.1.28.0">1992 c 593 art 1 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.1.66.0">1995 c 247 art 1 s 66</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.44.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 44</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.932">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.932 MERCURY PROHIBITION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.932.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Prohibitions and recycling requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person may not place mercury or a mercury-containing device or product, as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.92">116.92</a>, subdivision 10, from which the mercury has not been removed for reuse or recycling:</p>
          <p>(1) in solid waste; or</p>
          <p>(2) in a wastewater disposal system.</p>
          <p>(b) A person may not knowingly place mercury or a mercury-containing device or product, as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.92#stat.116.92.10">116.92, subdivision 10</a>, from which the mercury has not been removed for reuse or recycling:</p>
          <p>(1) in a solid waste processing facility; or</p>
          <p>(2) in a solid waste disposal facility.</p>
          <p>(c) A fluorescent or high-intensity discharge lamp must be recycled by delivery of the lamp to a lamp recycling facility, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.93#stat.116.93.1">116.93, subdivision 1</a>, or to a facility that collects and stores lamps for the purpose of delivering them to a lamp recycling facility, including, but not limited to, a household hazardous waste collection or recycling facility, retailer take-back and utility provider program sites, or other sites designated by an electric utility under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.241">216B.241, subdivisions 2</a> and 4.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.932.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided in paragraph (b), a violation of subdivision 1 is subject to enforcement under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.071">115.071</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072">116.072</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) A violation of subdivision 1 by a generator of household hazardous waste, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.96">115A.96</a>, is not subject to enforcement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.071#stat.115.071.3">115.071, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) An administrative penalty imposed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072">116.072</a> for a violation of subdivision 1 by a generator of household hazardous waste, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.96">115A.96</a>, may not exceed $700.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.932.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/560/#laws.0.1.0">1992 c 560 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/249/#laws.0.19.0">1993 c 249 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/62/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 62 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.98.0">1997 c 216 s 98</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/109/#laws.0.1.0">2007 c 109 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/382/#laws.0.18.0">2010 c 382 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/277/#laws.0.1.0">2014 c 277 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.935">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.935 SOLID WASTE GENERATED OUTSIDE MINNESOTA.</h1>
        <p>No person shall transport into or deposit in this state, for the purpose of processing or disposal, solid waste that was generated in another state, unless the waste:</p>
        <p>(1) meets all the solid waste management regulations of the state in which it was generated; and</p>
        <p>(2) contains none of the items specifically banned from mixed municipal solid waste in this state, including waste tires, motor and vehicle fluids and filters, waste lead acid batteries, yard waste, major appliances, and any other item specifically banned from the waste stream under this chapter.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.935.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.45.0">1991 c 337 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/249/#laws.0.61.0">1993 c 249 s 61</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.936">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.936 CONSTRUCTION DEBRIS AS COVER MATERIAL PROHIBITED.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) Construction debris or residuals from processed construction debris containing any amount of gypsum shall not be managed as cover material at disposal facilities unless:</p>
        <p class="in">(1) residual material is managed in an industrial or construction and demolition disposal facility equipped with a liner and leachate collection system;</p>
        <p class="in">(2) residual material is not mechanically pulverized or size-reduced prior to processing, screening, or application;</p>
        <p class="in">(3) a maximum effort is made to remove gypsum from the waste prior to processing, screening, or application;</p>
        <p class="in">(4) residual material is mixed at a ratio of one part soil to one part residual material prior to application; and</p>
        <p class="in">(5) the disposal facility does not accept any amount of cover material greater than what is operationally necessary.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) For the purposes of this section, "residual material" means construction debris or residuals from processed construction debris containing any amount of gypsum.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.936.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/300/#laws.0.2.0">2008 c 300 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ORGANIZED AND MANDATORY COLLECTION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.94">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.94 ORGANIZED COLLECTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.94.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>"Organized collection" means a system for collecting solid waste in which a specified collector, or a member of an organization of collectors, is authorized to collect from a defined geographic service area or areas some or all of the solid waste that is released by generators for collection.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.94.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Local authority.</span></h2>
          <p>A city or town may organize collection, after public notification and hearing as required in subdivisions 4a to 4f. A county may organize collection as provided in subdivision 5. A city or town that has organized collection as of May 1, 2013, is exempt from subdivisions 4a to 4f.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.94.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">General provisions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The local government unit may organize collection as a municipal service or by ordinance, franchise, license, negotiated or bidded contract, or other means, using one or more collectors or an organization of collectors.</p>
          <p>(b) The local government unit may not establish or administer organized collection in a manner that impairs the preservation and development of recycling and markets for recyclable materials. The local government unit shall exempt recyclable materials from organized collection upon a showing by the generator or collector that the materials are or will be separated from mixed municipal solid waste by the generator, separately collected, and delivered for reuse in their original form or for use in a manufacturing process.</p>
          <p>(c) The local government unit shall invite and employ the assistance of interested persons, including persons licensed to operate solid waste collection services in the local government unit, in developing plans and proposals for organized collection and in establishing the organized collection system.</p>
          <p>(d) Organized collection accomplished by contract or as a municipal service may include a requirement that all or any portion of the solid waste, except (1) recyclable materials and (2) materials that are processed at a resource recovery facility at the capacity in operation at the time that the requirement is imposed, be delivered to a waste facility identified by the local government unit. In a district or county where a resource recovery facility has been designated by ordinance under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.86">115A.86</a>, organized collection must conform to the requirements of the designation ordinance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.94.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/45/#laws.0.7.0">2013 c 45 s 7</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.94.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.<span class="headnote">Committee establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Before implementing an ordinance, franchise, license, contract, or other means of organizing collection, a city or town, by resolution of the governing body, must establish a solid waste collection options committee to identify, examine, and evaluate various methods of solid waste collection. The governing body shall appoint the committee members.</p>
          <p>(b) The solid waste collection options committee is subject to chapter <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13D">13D</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.94.4b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4b.<span class="headnote">Committee duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The committee established under subdivision 4a shall:</p>
          <p>(1) determine which methods of solid waste collection to examine, which must include:</p>
          <p>(i) the existing system of collection;</p>
          <p>(ii) a system in which a single collector collects solid waste from all sections of a city or town; and</p>
          <p>(iii) a system in which multiple collectors, either singly or as members of an organization of collectors, collect solid waste from different sections of a city or town;</p>
          <p>(2) establish a list of criteria on which the solid waste collection methods selected for examination will be evaluated, which may include: costs to residential subscribers, impacts on residential subscribers' ability to choose a provider of solid waste service based on the desired level of service, costs and other factors, the impact of miles driven on city streets and alleys and the incremental impact of miles driven by collection vehicles, initial and operating costs to the city of implementing the solid waste collection system, providing incentives for waste reduction, impacts on solid waste collectors, and other physical, economic, fiscal, social, environmental, and aesthetic impacts;</p>
          <p>(3) collect information regarding the operation and efficacy of existing methods of solid waste collection in other cities and towns;</p>
          <p>(4) seek input from, at a minimum:</p>
          <p>(i) the governing body of the city or town;</p>
          <p>(ii) the local official of the city or town responsible for solid waste issues;</p>
          <p>(iii) persons currently licensed to operate solid waste collection and recycling services in the city or town; and</p>
          <p>(iv) residents of the city or town who currently pay for residential solid waste collection services; and</p>
          <p>(5) issue a report on the committee's research, findings, and any recommendations to the governing body of the city or town.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.94.4c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4c.<span class="headnote">Governing body; implementation.</span></h2>
          <p>The governing body of the city or town shall consider the report and recommendations of the solid waste collection options committee. The governing body must provide public notice and hold at least one public hearing before deciding whether to implement organized collection. Organized collection may begin no sooner than six months after the effective date of the decision of the governing body of the city or town to implement organized collection.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.94.4d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4d.<span class="headnote">Participating collectors proposal; requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>Before establishing a committee under subdivision 4a to consider organizing residential solid waste collection, a city or town with more than one licensed collector must notify the public and all licensed collectors in the community. The city or town must provide a period of at least 60 days in which meetings and negotiations shall occur exclusively between licensed collectors and the city or town to develop a proposal in which interested licensed collectors, as members of an organization of collectors, collect solid waste from designated sections of the city or town. The proposal shall include identified city or town priorities, including issues related to zone creation, traffic, safety, environmental performance, service provided, and price, and shall reflect existing haulers maintaining their respective market share of business as determined by each hauler's average customer count during the six months prior to the commencement of the exclusive negotiation period. If an existing hauler opts to be excluded from the proposal, the city may allocate their customers proportionally based on market share to the participating collectors who choose to negotiate. The initial organized collection agreement executed under this subdivision must be for seven years. Upon execution of an agreement between the participating licensed collectors and city or town, the city or town shall establish organized collection through appropriate local controls and is not required to fulfill the requirements of subdivisions 4a, 4b, and 4c, except that the governing body must provide the public notification and hearing required under subdivision 4c.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.94.4e">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4e.<span class="headnote">Parties to meet and confer.</span></h2>
          <p>Before the exclusive meetings and negotiations under subdivision 4d, participating licensed collectors and elected officials of the city or town must meet and confer regarding waste collection issues, including but not limited to road deterioration, public safety, pricing mechanisms, and contractual considerations unique to organized collection.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.94.4f">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4f.<span class="headnote">Joint liability limited.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/604.02">604.02</a>, an organized collection agreement must not obligate a participating licensed collector for damages to third parties solely caused by another participating licensed collector. The organized collection agreement may include joint obligations for actions that are undertaken by all the participating licensed collectors under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.94.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Counties; organized collection.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A county may by ordinance require cities and towns within the county to organize collection. Organized collection ordinances of counties may:</p>
          <p>(1) require cities and towns to require the separation and separate collection of recyclable materials;</p>
          <p>(2) specify the material to be separated; and</p>
          <p>(3) require cities and towns to meet any performance standards for source separation that are contained in the county solid waste plan.</p>
          <p>(b) A county may itself organize collection under subdivisions 4a to 4f in any city or town that does not comply with a county organized collection ordinance adopted under this subdivision, and the county may implement, as part of its organized collection, the source separation program and performance standards required by its organized collection ordinance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.94.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Organized collection not required or prevented.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The authority granted in this section to organize solid waste collection is optional and is in addition to authority to govern solid waste collection granted by other law.</p>
          <p>(b) Except as provided in subdivision 5, a city, town, or county is not:</p>
          <p>(1) required to organize collection; or</p>
          <p>(2) prevented from organizing collection of solid waste or recyclable material.</p>
          <p>(c) Except as provided in subdivision 5, a city, town, or county may exercise any authority granted by any other law, including a home rule charter, to govern collection of solid waste.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.94.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Anticompetitive conduct.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A political subdivision that organizes collection under this section is authorized to engage in anticompetitive conduct to the extent necessary to plan and implement its chosen organized collection system and is immune from liability under state laws relating to antitrust, restraint of trade, unfair trade practices, and other regulation of trade or commerce.</p>
          <p>(b) An organization of solid waste collectors, an individual collector, and their officers, members, employees, and agents who cooperate with a political subdivision that organizes collection under this section are authorized to engage in anticompetitive conduct to the extent necessary to plan and implement the organized collection system, provided that the political subdivision actively supervises the participation of each entity. An organization, entity, or person covered by this paragraph is immune from liability under state law relating to antitrust, restraint of trade, unfair trade practices, and other regulation of trade or commerce.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.94.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/348/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 348 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/325/#laws.0.26.0">1989 c 325 s 26</a>,27; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/600/#laws.0.1.0">1990 c 600 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.46.0">1991 c 337 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/249/#laws.0.20.0">1993 c 249 s 20</a>,21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/45/#laws.0.1.0">2013 c 45 s 1</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/177/#laws.0.1.0">2018 c 177 s 1</a>-8</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.941">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.941 SOLID WASTE; REQUIRED COLLECTION.</h1>
        <p>(a) Except as provided in paragraph (b), each city, and town described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/368.01">368.01</a>, with a population of 1,000 or more, and any other town with a population of 5,000 or more shall ensure that every residential household and business in the city or town has solid waste collection service. To comply with this section, a city or town may organize collection, provide collection, or require by ordinance that every household and business has a contract for collection services. An ordinance adopted under this section must provide for enforcement.</p>
        <p>(b) A city or town described in paragraph (a) may exempt a residential household or business in the city or town from the requirement to have solid waste collection service if the household or business ensures that an environmentally sound alternative is used.</p>
        <p>(c) To the extent practicable, the costs incurred by a city or town under this section must be incorporated into the collection system or the enforcement mechanisms adopted under this section by the city or town.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.941.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.47.0">1991 c 337 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/249/#laws.0.22.0">1993 c 249 s 22</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">VISIBLE COSTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.945">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.945 VISIBLE COSTS; SOLID WASTE MANAGEMENT.</h1>
        <p>Any political subdivision that provides or pays for the costs of collection or disposal of solid waste shall, through a billing or other system, make the prorated share of those costs for each solid waste generator visible and obvious to the generator.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.945.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.20.9.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 20 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">RECYCLABLE MATERIALS PROHIBITED FROM CERTAIN FACILITIES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.95">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.95 RECYCLABLE MATERIALS.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) Recyclable materials must be delivered to the appropriate materials processing facility as outlined in rules of the agency or any other facility permitted to recycle or compost the materials.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) A disposal facility or a resource recovery facility that is composting mixed municipal solid waste, burning waste, or converting waste to energy or to materials for combustion may not accept source-separated recyclable materials, and a solid waste collector or transporter may not deliver source-separated recyclable materials to such a facility, except for recycling or transfer to a recycler, unless the commissioner determines that no other person is willing to accept the recyclable materials.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.95.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/274/#laws.0.13.0">1985 c 274 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/348/#laws.0.28.0">1987 c 348 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.26.0">1994 c 585 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/107/#laws.0.82.0">2011 c 107 s 82</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">TELEPHONE DIRECTORIES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.951">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.951 TELEPHONE DIRECTORIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.951.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, a "telephone directory" means a printed list of residential, governmental, or commercial telephone service subscribers or users, or a combination of subscribers or users, that contains more than 7,500 listings and is distributed to the subscribers or users.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.951.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Prohibition.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not place a telephone directory:</p>
          <p>(1) in solid waste;</p>
          <p>(2) in a disposal facility; or</p>
          <p>(3) in a resource recovery facility, except a recycling facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.951.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Recyclability.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not distribute a telephone directory to any person in this state unless the telephone directory:</p>
          <p>(1) is printed on paper that is recyclable;</p>
          <p>(2) is printed with inks that contain no heavy metals or other toxic materials; and</p>
          <p>(3) is bound with materials that pose no unreasonable barriers to recycling of the directory.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.951.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Collecting used directories.</span></h2>
          <p>Each publisher or distributor of telephone directories shall:</p>
          <p>(1) provide for the collection and delivery to a recycler of waste telephone directories;</p>
          <p>(2) inform recipients of directories of the collection system; and</p>
          <p>(3) submit a report to the agency by August 1 of each year that specifies the percentage of distributed directories collected as waste directories by distribution area and the locations where the waste directories were delivered for recycling and that verifies that the directories have been recycled.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.951.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/593/#laws.1.29.0">1992 c 593 art 1 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.2.20.0">1995 c 247 art 2 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">PROBLEM MATERIALS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.952">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.952 RETAIL SALE OF PROBLEM MATERIALS; UNIFORM LABELING AND CONSUMER INFORMATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.952.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Duties of agency; rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency may adopt rules to identify products that are used primarily for personal, family, or household purposes and that constitute a problem material or contain a problem material as defined in <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.03#stat.115A.03.24e">section 115A.03, subdivision 24e</a>. The rules may also prescribe a uniform label to be affixed by retailers of identified products as provided in subdivision 4. Packaging that is recyclable or made from recycled material shall not constitute a problem material.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.952.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Duties of commissioner of agriculture.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of agriculture may adopt rules to provide consumer information and retail handling practices for pesticides, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/18B.01#stat.18B.01.18">18B.01, subdivision 18</a>; fertilizers, plant amendments, and soil amendments, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/18C.005">18C.005, subdivisions 11, 25, and 33</a>; and wood preservatives.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.952.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Preparing and supplying materials.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency and the commissioner of agriculture shall prepare and the agency shall supply to retailers, without charge to the retailers, the labels and informational materials required to comply with subdivision 4. Informational materials must include specific instructions on environmentally sound ways to use identified products and to handle them when the products or their containers are discarded.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.952.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Duties of retailers.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who sells or offers for sale at retail any product that is identified pursuant to rules of the agency adopted under subdivision 1 or under rules of the commissioner of agriculture under subdivision 2 shall:</p>
          <p>(1) affix a uniform label as prescribed by the rules in a prominent location upon or near the display area of the product. If the adjacent display area is a shelf, the label shall be affixed to the price information for the product on the shelf;</p>
          <p>(2) maintain and prominently display informational materials supplied by the agency at the location where identified products covered by the materials are sold or offered for sale; and</p>
          <p>(3) comply with the handling practices required under subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.952.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.20.10.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 20 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/85/#laws.0.12.0">2024 c 85 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.2.34.0">2024 c 116 art 2 s 34</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.9523"><b>115A.9523</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.160.0">1997 c 216 s 160</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.953"><b>115A.953</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.90.0">1991 c 337 s 90</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.956">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.956 SOLID WASTE DISPOSAL; PROBLEM MATERIALS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.956.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Problem material; processing and disposal plan.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency shall develop a plan that designates problem materials and available capacity for processing and disposal of problem materials including household hazardous waste that should not be in mixed municipal solid waste. In developing the plan, the agency shall consider relevant regional characteristics and the impact of problem materials on specific processing and disposal technologies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.956.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Problem material; separation and collection plan.</span></h2>
          <p>After the agency certifies that sufficient processing and disposal capacity is available, but no later than November 15, 1992, the agency shall develop a plan for separating problem materials from mixed municipal solid waste, collecting the problem materials, and transporting the problem materials to a processing or disposal facility and may by rule prohibit the placement of the designated problem materials in mixed municipal solid waste.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.956.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.20.12.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 20 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/303/#laws.0.2.0">1991 c 303 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.9561">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.9561 MAJOR APPLIANCES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.9561.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Prohibitions.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not:</p>
          <p>(1) place major appliances in mixed municipal solid waste; or</p>
          <p>(2) dispose of major appliances in or on the land or in a solid waste processing or disposal facility. The agency may enforce this section pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.071">115.071</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072">116.072</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.9561.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Recycling required.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Major appliances must be recycled or reused. Each county shall ensure that its households have the opportunity to recycle used major appliances. For the purposes of this section, recycling includes:</p>
          <p>(1) the removal of capacitors that may contain PCBs;</p>
          <p>(2) the removal of ballasts that may contain PCBs;</p>
          <p>(3) the removal of chlorofluorocarbon refrigerant gas; and</p>
          <p>(4) the recycling or reuse of the metals, including mercury.</p>
          <p>(b) To ensure that the materials removed from a major appliance are not introduced into the environment, an activity described in paragraph (a), clauses (1) to (3), must be conducted in a closed facility if the activity is conducted within 500 feet from the ordinary high-water level of a water basin that is a public water, as those terms are described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.005">103G.005</a>, or of a watercourse identified by the public waters inventory under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.201">103G.201</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.9561.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
