        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.20.13.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 20 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.48.0">1991 c 337 s 48</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/347/#laws.1.18.0">1991 c 347 art 1 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/560/#laws.0.2.0">1992 c 560 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.27.0">1994 c 585 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/382/#laws.1.3.0">2002 c 382 art 1 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.9565">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.9565 CATHODE-RAY TUBE PROHIBITION.</h1>
        <p>Effective July 1, 2006, a person may not place in mixed municipal solid waste an electronic product containing a cathode-ray tube.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.9565.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.129.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 129</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.132.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 132</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.96">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.96 HOUSEHOLD HAZARDOUS WASTE; MANAGEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.96.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>The following definitions apply to this section:</p>
          <p>(a) "Household" means a single detached dwelling unit or a single unit of a multiple dwelling unit and appurtenant structures.</p>
          <p>(b) "Household hazardous waste" means waste generated from household activity that exhibits the characteristics of or that is listed as hazardous waste under agency rules, but does not include waste from commercial activities that is generated, stored, or present in a household.</p>
          <p>(c) "Collection site" means a permanent or temporary designated location with scheduled hours for collection where individuals may bring household hazardous wastes.</p>
          <p>(d) "Municipality" has the meaning given it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/466.01#stat.466.01.1">466.01, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.96.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Management program.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency shall establish a statewide program to manage household hazardous wastes. The program must include:</p>
          <p>(1) the establishment and operation of collection sites; and</p>
          <p>(2) the provision of information, education, and technical assistance regarding proper management of household hazardous wastes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.96.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Other participants.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The agency may establish or operate all or part of the management program or may provide for services by contract or other agreement with public or private entities.</p>
          <p>(b) The agency shall allow these programs to accept up to 100 pounds of waste per year from a hazardous waste generator that generates 220 pounds or less of hazardous waste per month.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.96.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Managing collected waste.</span></h2>
          <p>Any person who establishes or operates all or part of a household hazardous waste management program shall manage collected waste in compliance with standards applicable to a hazardous waste generator. If collected waste must be stored for a time exceeding those standards, the entity shall obtain the approval of the commissioner of the agency and shall manage the waste in compliance with applicable standards for the use and management of containers, but no facility permit is required. Waste accepted under subdivision 3, paragraph (b), must be managed in accordance with standards applicable to the waste.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.96.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Other programs.</span></h2>
          <p>A person must notify the commissioner of the agency before establishing and operating any part of a household hazardous waste management program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.96.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Management plans.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Each county shall include in its solid waste management plan required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.46">115A.46</a>, or its solid waste master plan required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.803">473.803</a>, a household hazardous waste management plan. The plan must at least:</p>
          <p>(1) include a broad based public education component;</p>
          <p>(2) include a strategy for reduction of household hazardous waste; and</p>
          <p>(3) include a strategy for separation of household hazardous waste from mixed municipal solid waste and the collection, storage, and proper management of that waste.</p>
          <p>(b) Each county required to submit its plan to the agency under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.46">115A.46</a> shall amend its plan to comply with this subdivision within one year after October 4, 1989.</p>
          <p>(c) Each county in the state shall implement its household hazardous waste management plan by June 30, 1992.</p>
          <p>(d) The agency shall review the plans submitted under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.96.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Indemnification; municipalities.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A municipality, when operating or participating in a household hazardous waste management program pursuant to a contract with the agency under this section or other law, is an employee of the state, certified to be acting within the scope of employment, for purposes of the indemnification provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.736#stat.3.736.9">3.736, subdivision 9</a>, for claims that arise out of the transportation, management, or disposal of any waste covered by the contract:</p>
          <p>(1) from and after the time the waste permanently leaves the municipality's possession and comes into the possession of the agency's authorized transporter; and</p>
          <p>(2) during the time the waste is transported between the municipality's facilities by the agency's authorized transporter.</p>
          <p>(b) The state is not obligated to defend or indemnify a municipality under this subdivision to the extent of the municipality's liability insurance. The municipality's right to indemnity is not a waiver of the limitations, defenses, and immunities available to either the municipality or the state by law.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.96.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/186/#laws.0.15.0">1987 c 186 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/348/#laws.0.29.0">1987 c 348 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.20.15.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 20 s 15</a>,16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/303/#laws.0.3.0">1991 c 303 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.49.0">1991 c 337 s 49</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/172/#laws.0.62.0">1993 c 172 s 62</a>,63; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.1.26.0">1995 c 247 art 1 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/265/#laws.0.1.0">2002 c 265 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/374/#laws.6.3.0">2002 c 374 art 6 s 3</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.961">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.961 HOUSEHOLD BATTERIES; COLLECTION, PROCESSING, AND DISPOSAL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.961.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, "household batteries" means disposable or rechargeable dry cells commonly used as power sources for household or consumer products including, but not limited to, nickel-cadmium, alkaline, mercuric oxide, silver oxide, zinc oxide, lithium, and carbon-zinc batteries, but excluding lead acid batteries.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.961.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Program.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner, in consultation with other state agencies, political subdivisions, and representatives of the household battery industry, may develop household battery programs. The commissioner must coordinate the programs with the Legislative-Citizen Commission on Minnesota Resources study on batteries.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner shall investigate options and develop guidelines for collection, processing, and disposal of household batteries. The options the commissioner may investigate include:</p>
          <p>(1) establishing a grant program for counties to plan and implement household battery collection, processing, and disposal projects;</p>
          <p>(2) establishing collection and transportation systems;</p>
          <p>(3) developing and disseminating educational materials regarding environmentally sound battery management; and</p>
          <p>(4) developing markets for materials recovered from the batteries.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may also distribute funds to political subdivisions to develop battery management plans and implement those plans.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.961.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Participation.</span></h2>
          <p>A political subdivision, on its own or in cooperation with others, may implement a program to collect, process, or dispose of household batteries. A political subdivision may provide financial incentives to any person, including public or private civic groups, to collect the batteries.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.961.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Obsolete, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.961.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.20.14.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 20 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.5.3.0">1994 c 639 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/243/#laws.0.21.0">2006 c 243 s 21</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.965">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.965 PROHIBITIONS ON SELECTED TOXICS IN PACKAGING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.965.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Packaging.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) As soon as feasible but not later than August 1, 1993, no manufacturer or distributor may sell or offer for sale or for promotional purposes in this state packaging or a product that is contained in packaging if the packaging itself, or any inks, dyes, pigments, adhesives, stabilizers, or any other additives to the packaging contain any lead, cadmium, mercury, or hexavalent chromium that has been intentionally introduced as an element during manufacture or distribution of the packaging.</p>
          <p>(b) For the purposes of this section:</p>
          <p>(1) "distributor" means a person who imports packaging or causes packaging to be imported into the state; it does not include a person involved solely in delivering packages on behalf of a third party;</p>
          <p>(2) "intentional introduction" means the act of deliberately using a regulated metal in the formulation of a package where its continued presence is desired in the final package to provide a specific characteristic, appearance, or quality. It does not include:</p>
          <p>(i) the use of a regulated metal as a processing agent or intermediate to impart certain chemical or physical changes during manufacturing, where the incidental retention of a residue of the metal in the final package is neither desired nor deliberate if the final package is in compliance with subdivision 2;</p>
          <p>(ii) the use of recycled materials as feedstock for the manufacture of new packaging materials, where some portion of the recycled materials may contain amounts of a regulated metal if the new package is in compliance with subdivision 2; or</p>
          <p>(iii) the incidental presence of any of the regulated metals.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.965.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Total toxics concentration levels.</span></h2>
          <p>The total concentration level of lead, cadmium, mercury, and hexavalent chromium added together in any packaging must not exceed the following amounts:</p>
          <p>(1) 600 parts per million by weight by August 1, 1993;</p>
          <p>(2) 250 parts per million by weight by August 1, 1994; and</p>
          <p>(3) 100 parts per million by weight by August 1, 1995.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.965.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Exemptions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Until January 1, 2010, the following packaging is exempt from the requirements of subdivisions 1 and 2:</p>
          <p>(1) packaging that would not exceed the total toxics concentration levels under subdivision 2 but for the addition in the packaging of materials that have fulfilled their intended use and have been discarded by consumers; and</p>
          <p>(2) packages that are reused but exceed the total toxics concentration levels in subdivision 2, provided that:</p>
          <p>(i) the product being conveyed by the package is regulated under federal or state health or safety requirements;</p>
          <p>(ii) transportation of the packaged product is regulated under federal or state transportation requirements; and</p>
          <p>(iii) disposal of the package is performed according to federal or state radioactive or hazardous waste disposal requirements.</p>
          <p>(b) Until January 1, 2010, packages that have a controlled distribution and reuse, but exceed the total toxics concentration levels in subdivision 2 and do not meet the requirements of paragraph (a), may be exempted from subdivisions 1 and 2 if the manufacturers or distributors of the packages petition for and receive approval from the commissioner. In granting approval, the commissioner shall base the decision on satisfactory demonstrations that the environmental benefit of the controlled distribution and reuse is significantly greater compared to the same package manufactured in compliance with the total toxics concentration levels in subdivision 2, and on plans proposed by the manufacturer that include each of the following elements:</p>
          <p>(1) a means of identifying the packaging in a permanent and visible manner;</p>
          <p>(2) a method of regulatory and financial accountability so that a specified percentage of the packaging manufactured and distributed to other persons is not discarded by those persons after use but are returned to the manufacturer or the manufacturer's designee;</p>
          <p>(3) a system of inventory and record maintenance to account for the packaging placed in, and removed from, service;</p>
          <p>(4) a means of transforming packaging that is no longer reusable into recycled materials for manufacturing or into manufacturing wastes which are subject to existing federal or state laws or regulations governing such manufacturing wastes that ensure that these wastes do not enter the industrial or mixed municipal solid waste stream; and</p>
          <p>(5) a system of annually reporting to the commissioner changes to the system and changes in designees.</p>
          <p>(c) Packaging to which lead, cadmium, mercury, or hexavalent chromium has been intentionally introduced in the manufacturing process may be exempted from the requirements of subdivisions 1 and 2 by the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency if:</p>
          <p>(1) the use of the toxic element in the packaging is required by federal or state health or safety laws; or</p>
          <p>(2) there is no feasible alternative for the packaging because the toxic element used is essential to the protection, safe handling, or function of the contents of the package.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner may grant an exemption under paragraph (c) for a period not to exceed two years upon application by the packaging manufacturer that includes documentation showing that the criteria for an exemption are met. Exemptions granted by the commissioner may be renewed upon reapplication every two years.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.965.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Certificate of compliance.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Beginning August 1, 1993, each manufacturer and distributor of packaging for sale or other distribution in this state shall certify to each of their purchasers or receivers that the packaging purchased or received complies with this section. The certificate of compliance must be in writing and must be signed by an official of the manufacturer or distributor. For packaging that has received an exemption under subdivision 3, the certificate of compliance must list the amount of total toxics concentration in the packaging, the specific toxics present, and the basis for the exemption.</p>
          <p>(b) The manufacturer or distributor shall keep on file a copy of the certificate of compliance for each type of packaging manufactured or distributed and shall make copies available to the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency or the attorney general on request, or to any member of the public within 60 days of receipt of a written request that specifies the type of packaging for which the information is requested.</p>
          <p>(c) Each purchaser or receiver, except a retailer, of packaging shall retain the certificate of compliance for as long as the packaging is in use.</p>
          <p>(d) If a manufacturer or distributor of packaging reformulates the packaging or creates new packaging, the manufacturer or distributor shall provide an amended or new certificate of compliance to purchasers and receivers for the reformulated or new packaging.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.965.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>This section may be enforced under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.071">115.071</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072">116.072</a>. A person who fails to comply with this section is subject to a civil fine of up to $5,000 per day of violation, court costs and attorney fees, and all costs associated with the separate collection, storage, transfer, and appropriate processing or disposal of nonconforming packaging, to be determined by the true cost of those activities per ton times the approximate actual tonnage of nonconforming packaging sold or otherwise distributed in the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.965.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/186/#laws.0.4.0">1997 c 186 s 4</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.965.6a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6a.<span class="headnote">Implementation.</span></h2>
          <p>In the interests of promoting consistent, nationally applicable standards, the commissioner shall have discretion to coordinate efforts under this section with similar efforts in other jurisdictions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.965.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.98.0">2012 c 272 s 98</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.965.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.50.0">1991 c 337 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/249/#laws.0.24.0">1993 c 249 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.28.0">1994 c 585 s 28</a>,29; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.1.27.0">1995 c 247 art 1 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.14.0">1996 c 470 s 14</a>,15,27; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/186/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 186 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/370/#laws.0.2.0">2000 c 370 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.9651">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.9651 LISTED METALS IN SPECIFIED PRODUCTS; ENFORCEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.9651.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Prohibition.</span></h2>
          <p>After July 1, 1998, no person may distribute a listed product for sale or use in this state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.9651.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p>(b) "Council" means the council established under subdivision 5.</p>
          <p>(c) "Essential product" means a specified product into which the introduction of a listed metal is required under military specifications or to ensure the integrity of a product essential for aviation or railroad safety, and which is being used only in that application.</p>
          <p>(d) "Intentionally introduce" means to deliberately use a listed metal as an element during manufacture or distribution of a specified product. Intentional introduction does not include the incidental presence of a listed metal.</p>
          <p>(e) "Listed metal" means lead, cadmium, mercury, or hexavalent chromium.</p>
          <p>(f) "Listed product" means a specified product that is included on the prohibited products list published under subdivision 4.</p>
          <p>(g) "New product" means a specified product which was not used, sold, or distributed in the state before July 2, 1998, or which has been reformulated so that it contains more of a listed metal.</p>
          <p>(h) "Official" means an officer of a corporation, a general partner of a partnership or limited partnership, a sole proprietor, or, in the case of any other entity, a person with high-level management responsibilities.</p>
          <p>(i) "Specified product" means an ink, dye, pigment, paint, or fungicide into which a listed metal has been intentionally introduced or in which the incidental presence of a listed metal exceeds a concentration of 100 parts per million.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.9651.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Certification of compliance.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) By July 1, 1998, each person who has filed the progress report specified in Laws 1994, chapter 585, section 30, subdivision 2, paragraph (e), indicating compliance would be achieved by July 1, 1998, shall certify to the commissioner that the products referenced in that report have been reformulated and no longer meet the definition of a specified product. The certification must be in writing and signed by an official of the company. If, due to significant change in circumstances, the person cannot so certify by July 1, 1998, a product review report and fee shall be submitted as provided under subdivision 6.</p>
          <p>(b) The person submitting the certification shall keep a copy on file and make copies available to the commissioner or the attorney general upon request or to any member of the public within 60 days of receipt of a written request that specifies the type of product for which the information is requested.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.9651.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Prohibited products list.</span></h2>
          <p>By October 1, 1998, the commissioner shall publish in the State Register a list of specified products for which the commissioner has received certifications as provided under subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.9651.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/133/#laws.2.13.0">2007 c 133 art 2 s 13</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.9651.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Product review reports.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided under subdivision 7, the manufacturer, or an association of manufacturers, of any specified product distributed for sale or use in this state that is not listed pursuant to subdivision 4 shall submit a product review report and fee as provided in paragraph (c) to the commissioner for each product by July 1, 1998. Each product review report shall contain at least the following:</p>
          <p>(1) a policy statement articulating upper management support for eliminating or reducing intentional introduction of listed metals into its products;</p>
          <p>(2) a description of the product and the amount of each listed metal distributed for use in this state;</p>
          <p>(3) a description of past and ongoing efforts to eliminate or reduce the listed metal in the product;</p>
          <p>(4) an assessment of options available to reduce or eliminate the intentional introduction of the listed metal including any alternatives to the specified product that do not contain the listed metal, perform the same technical function, are commercially available, and are economically practicable;</p>
          <p>(5) a statement of objectives in numerical terms and a schedule for achieving the elimination of the listed metals and an environmental assessment of alternative products;</p>
          <p>(6) a listing of options considered not to be technically or economically practicable; and</p>
          <p>(7) certification attesting to the accuracy of the information in the report signed and dated by an official of the manufacturer or user.</p>
          <p class="bl">If the manufacturer fails to submit a product review report, a user of a specified product may submit a report and fee which comply with this subdivision by August 15, 1998.</p>
          <p>(b) By July 1, 1999, and annually thereafter until the commissioner takes action under subdivision 9, the manufacturer or user must submit a progress report and fee as provided in paragraph (c) updating the information presented under paragraph (a).</p>
          <p>(c) The fee shall be $295 for each report. The fee shall be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the environmental fund. The fee is exempt from section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.1285">16A.1285</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) Where it cannot be determined from a progress report submitted by a person pursuant to Laws 1994, chapter 585, section 30, subdivision 2, paragraph (e), the number of products for which product review reports are due under this subdivision, the commissioner shall have the authority to determine, after consultation with that person, the number of products for which product review reports are required.</p>
          <p>(e) The commissioner shall summarize, aggregate, and publish data reported under paragraphs (a) and (b) annually.</p>
          <p>(f) A product that is the subject of a recommendation by the Toxics in Packaging Clearinghouse, as administered by the Council of State Governments, is exempt from this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.9651.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Essential products; published list.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) By January 1, 1998, a manufacturer or user of an essential product must submit a certification to the commissioner that the product meets the definition in subdivision 2, paragraph (c). By July 1, 2002, each manufacturer or user of an essential product shall submit a report to the commissioner which includes the information required in subdivision 6, paragraph (a), and a statement of whether the product continues to meet the definition in subdivision 2, paragraph (c).</p>
          <p>(b) By October 1, 1998, the commissioner shall publish in the State Register a list of essential products for which the commissioner has received certification pursuant to this subdivision. By October 1, 2002, the commissioner shall publish in the State Register a list of essential products based on reports submitted by July 1, 2002, as provided in paragraph (a).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.9651.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">New products; criteria for review.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) After July 1, 1998, but before July 1, 2005, no person shall sell, distribute, or offer for sale in this state a new product prior to the manufacturer or user submitting a product review report and fee specified in subdivision 6.</p>
          <p>(b) The council shall review reports submitted under this subdivision and provide advice to the commissioner. The council's advice to the commissioner under this subdivision shall be based on an evaluation of the environmental impact of the product and the ability of the manufacturer or user to reduce or eliminate the listed metal. Before making a recommendation that the commissioner take action under subdivision 9, the council must conclude that:</p>
          <p>(1) there is an alternative to the specified product that does not contain the listed metal that performs the same technical function, is commercially available, and is economically practicable, and replacement of the product with the alternative will result in an environmental benefit in the state; or</p>
          <p>(2) if there is no alternative to the new product, that the use of the listed metal in the new product presents a significant threat to the safe and efficient operation of waste facilities, or use of the listed metal does not increase the useful life span of the new product, reduce the overall toxicity of the final product or of material used in production of the final product, or otherwise provide a net environmental benefit to the state.</p>
          <p>(c) Notwithstanding subdivision 5, paragraph (f), where the commissioner determines that a new product subject to paragraph (a) is sufficiently similar to a product or products previously reviewed by the council, the commissioner may authorize the permanent members of the council to perform the duties established in paragraph (b) without the appointment of temporary members. In performing those duties, the council shall utilize information gathered in any previous review of a similar product or products.</p>
          <p>(d) Beginning July 1, 2005, no person shall sell, distribute, or offer for sale in this state a new product without the commissioner's approval. A person seeking approval of a new product shall submit a product review report including the information and fee specified in subdivision 6. The commissioner shall not approve the new product unless the commissioner determines that it meets the criteria in paragraph (b). The commissioner shall make a determination within six months of receipt of a complete request.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.9651.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Authority of commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may, upon the recommendation of the council, prohibit the distribution for sale or use in this state of a specified product that is not an essential product.</p>
          <p>(b) Before taking action under this subdivision, the commissioner must conclude that:</p>
          <p>(1) there is an alternative to the specified product that does not contain the listed metal that performs the same technical function, is commercially available, and is economically practicable, and replacement of the product with the alternative will result in an environmental benefit to the state; or</p>
          <p>(2) if there is no alternative to the new product, that the use of the listed metal in the new product presents a significant threat to the safe and efficient operation of waste facilities, or use of the listed metal does not increase the useful life span of the new product, reduce the overall toxicity of the final product or of material used in production of the final product, or otherwise provide a net environmental benefit to the state.</p>
          <p>(c) If the commissioner fails to take action under this subdivision as recommended by the council, the commissioner shall submit a report to the legislature explaining the reasons for not taking such action.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner shall provide the legislature a report and recommendations based on any report prepared by the council under subdivision 5, paragraph (c), clause (2).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.9651.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Application; enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) This section does not apply to art supplies.</p>
          <p>(b) This section may be enforced under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.071">115.071</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072">116.072</a>. The attorney general or the commissioner of the agency shall coordinate enforcement of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.9651.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Rulemaking authority.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Pollution Control Agency may adopt, amend, suspend, and repeal rules to implement this section.</p>
          <p>(b) Publication of notice under subdivision 5, paragraph (g), shall be deemed to satisfy the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.101">14.101</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may adopt a council recommendation under subdivision 5 as the agency's statement of need and reasonableness. A recommendation adopted in this manner shall be deemed to satisfy any content requirements for a statement of need and reasonableness imposed by law.</p>
          <p>(d) Any hearings on rules adopted under this section shall be conducted in accordance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.14">14.14</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.20">14.20</a> and address whether the rule meets the standards for review under which the judge is required to approve or disapprove the rule.</p>
          <p>(e) Section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.125">14.125</a> does not apply to the agency's rulemaking authority under this section.</p>
          <p>(f) A rule adopted under this section is effective until repealed by the agency.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.9651.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.51.0">1991 c 337 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/249/#laws.0.25.0">1993 c 249 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/366/#laws.0.7.0">1993 c 366 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.30.0">1994 c 585 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.1.28.0">1995 c 247 art 1 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/455/#laws.3.1.0">1996 c 455 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.16.0">1996 c 470 s 16</a>,27; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/221/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 221 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/370/#laws.0.3.0">2000 c 370 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.2.6.0">2003 c 128 art 2 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.97">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.97 SPECIAL WASTE; INCINERATOR ASH.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.97.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Policy; goals.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) It is the policy of the legislature that mixed municipal solid waste incinerators be planned and managed to achieve to the maximum extent feasible and prudent:</p>
          <p>(1) reduction of the toxicity of incinerator ash;</p>
          <p>(2) reduction of the quantity of the incinerator ash; and</p>
          <p>(3) reduction of the quantity of waste processing residuals that require disposal.</p>
          <p>(b) The purpose of this section is to establish temporary and permanent programs to achieve these reduction goals.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.97.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p>(b) "Incinerator ash" means ash resulting from the combustion of mixed municipal solid waste and ash resulting from the combustion of refuse-derived fuel.</p>
          <p>(c) "Noncombustible fraction" means constituents of mixed municipal solid waste, including glass, ferrous metals, nonferrous metals and other inorganics, that, when burned, disproportionately add to the quantity of incinerator ash.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.97.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency shall adopt rules to establish techniques to measure the noncombustible fraction of mixed municipal solid waste prior to incineration or processing into refuse-derived fuel and for at least the testing, management, and disposal of incinerator ash. The rules must be designed to meet the goals in subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.97.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/310/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 310 s 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.97.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Plans; report.</span></h2>
          <p>A county solid waste plan, or revision of a plan, that includes incineration of mixed municipal solid waste must clearly state how the county plans to meet the goals in subdivision 1 of reducing the toxicity and quantity of incinerator ash and of reducing the quantity of processing residuals that require disposal. The commissioner, in cooperation with the counties, may develop guidelines for counties to use to identify ways to meet the goals in subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.97.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Permits; agency report.</span></h2>
          <p>An application for a permit to build or operate a mixed municipal solid waste incinerator, including an application for permit renewal, must clearly state how the applicant will achieve the goals in subdivision 1 of reducing the toxicity and quantity of incinerator ash and of reducing the quantity of processing residuals that require disposal. The agency, in cooperation with the counties, may develop guidelines for applicants to use to identify ways to meet the goals in subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.97.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/685/#laws.0.13.0">1988 c 685 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.269.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 269</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/469/#laws.0.1.0">1990 c 469 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/337/#laws.0.52.0">1991 c 337 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/639/#laws.5.3.0">1994 c 639 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.2.21.0">1995 c 247 art 2 s 21</a>,22; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/13/#laws.1.9.0">2007 c 13 art 1 s 9</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.98"><b>115A.98</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/325/#laws.0.77.0">1989 c 325 s 77</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.981"><b>115A.981</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/370/#laws.0.5.0">2000 c 370 s 5</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">LITTER</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.99">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.99 LITTER; PENALTIES AND DAMAGES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.99.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Civil penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person who unlawfully places any portion of solid waste in or on public or private lands, shorelands, roadways, or waters is subject to a civil penalty of not less than twice nor more than five times the costs incurred by a state agency or political subdivision to remove, process, and dispose of the waste.</p>
          <p>(b) A state agency or political subdivision that incurs costs as described in this section may bring an action to recover the civil penalty, related legal, administrative, and court costs, and damages for injury to or pollution of the lands, shorelands, roadways, or waters where the waste was placed if owned or managed by the entity bringing the action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.99.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Disposition.</span></h2>
          <p>Civil penalties and damages collected under subdivision 1 must be collected and distributed as required in chapter 484.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.115A.99.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Joinder; private action for damages.</span></h2>
          <p>A private person may join an action by the state or a political subdivision to recover a civil penalty under subdivision 1 to allow the person to recover damages for waste unlawfully placed on the person's property.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.99.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.20.17.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 20 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/412/#laws.0.2.0">1994 c 412 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/13/#laws.3.10.0">2007 c 13 art 3 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.115A.991"><b>115A.991</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/470/#laws.0.28.0">1996 c 470 s 28</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.115A.993">
        <h1 class="shn">115A.993 PROHIBITED DISPOSAL METHODS.</h1>
        <p>A person must not dispose of waste treated seed in a manner inconsistent with the product label, where applicable, or by:</p>
        <p>(1) burial near a drinking water source or any creek, stream, river, lake, or other surface water;</p>
        <p>(2) composting; or</p>
        <p>(3) incinerating within a home or other dwelling.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.115A.993.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.3.16.0">2023 c 60 art 3 s 16</a>
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
